# kreators.app

Seven relationships as a daily game, with a creator super app on top: public site, learn-and-earn modules, content rewards, a shop with attribution, finance, circles, a life dashboard.
Full spec and history: README.md, docs/DECISIONS.md, and the build map (Dev Tools, from apps/web/src/lib/buildMap.ts). Read the relevant section before changing anything.

## Stack
pnpm workspace. apps/web: React 19, Vite 7, Tailwind 4, PWA. apps/api: Hono on Cloudflare Workers with D1, plain SQL migrations in apps/api/migrations, cron every fifteen minutes. packages/shared: the domain model both sides import, with Vitest tests. Since the wave 9 cleanup (2026-11-04) `src/index.ts` is only a barrel of `export * from './<domain>.ts'` lines in load order (a file after the files it reads at load time), over one file per domain (core, pillars, rewards, work, community, dates, api, events, store, badges, tasks, periods, money, invoice, agency, design, discover and the rest); the `.ts` extensions stay, because the smoke scripts load it with Node's strip-types. A new name goes in its domain's file and a new file gets a line in the barrel; "in shared" in these notes means through the barrel. A top-level constant may only call a function declared with `function` or defined above it, and after any edit `node --experimental-strip-types -e "import('./packages/shared/src/index.ts')"` must load. The subpath exports (`journalPrompts`, `agencyEmail`, `focusRun`, and `ladderChoices`: the strategy session's and vibe check's value goals and projects with their whys, `LADDER_CHOICES`, `ladderOptions`, `projectTasks`, split off 2026-11-06 when it took the shared chunk to 414 kB) are their own files as before.

## Non-negotiable rules
- The product is called kreators.app, lowercase with the dot, in every heading, footer, email, policy and share card; never "Kreators Life". Identifiers keep their old names: the Worker and database `kreators-life`, the `@kreators/*` packages, storage keys and the repo.
- Money is integer cents or integer points. Use `money()` and `feeBps()` from shared. Never floats for money. An amount a person types is read from its digits with `typedCents` in shared (never `Math.round(Number(x) * 100)`: 1.005 would be 100). The one older exception is a TV or Fund pitch's `budget_amount`, kept in whole units; it too is read with `typedCents` (`budgetOf` in routes/proposals.ts, `budgetUnits` on the screen), so ₹ and lakh commas work. Anything that moves points takes them first with a guarded `UPDATE … WHERE points >= ?` and checks `meta.changes`, and anything that pays or refunds moves its status first (`WHERE status = 'verified'`) and checks the change, so two taps at once pay or refund once. A key a person can type is looked up in a `byKey` object (no prototype), so "constructor" finds nothing.
- Times are stored as UTC ISO strings. Zone conversion happens only through `zonedToUtc` and `slotsFor` in shared. A day typed or sent (`?day=`, `date`, `anchor`, `until`) is checked as a real calendar day (`DATE_RE.test` in db.ts is that check, so 2026-11-31 and 2026-00-00 are refused, never a 500). A repeat is monthly or yearly from the day it started (`Repeat.anchor`, set by `spawnNext`), so a task that fell on the 28th of a short month goes back to the 31st. The cron's last tick of a day is 23:45 (`LAST_TICK`), so a reminder set later goes out then. A local time that does not exist (the clocks jumped over it) lands an hour later in `zonedToUtc`.
- India first, open to all (2026-11-03): India is the default and someone abroad changes it. `LOCALE` (`en-IN`) and `DEFAULT_ZONE` (`Asia/Kolkata`) in shared are the one locale and the one zone fallback, what a visitor reads. Every time on a screen goes through `lib/date.ts`, which follows the signed-in person (2026-11-07, the founder; `setDateDisplay`, called from `main.tsx` the moment the account lands, with a device copy `kreators.dateDisplay` for the first paint, cleared on sign-out): their zone (`users.timezone`, which also times their reminders) and their date and time format (`users.date_prefs_json`, migration 0109; `DATE_FORMATS`, `TIME_FORMATS`, `parseDatePrefs` the one reader, `datePrefsProblem` the one check, `formatDate`, `formatClock`, `formatStamp` and `formatMinute` in shared `dates.ts`; "7 Nov 26, 4:17am" unless changed; `PATCH /me {datePrefs}`, limit `prefs` only when it carries them; on `GET /me`, in the export, gone with the account, untouched by an erase), both changed under Dates and times (`components/DatesAndTimes.tsx`, which also offers the device's zone when its clock differs; names are not compared, since Chrome in India says Asia/Calcutta) in the Profile tab's form (`/you#dates`, which everyone can reach) and the Public page's (`/profile#dates`, where the zone always was). A helper that shows a year or a clock takes the format (`longDate`, `stamp`, `clockTime`, `dateTime`, `eventTime`, and for a clock with no zone in it `clockAt`, `clockOf`, `slotRange`); one that shows a day and month or a weekday keeps its shape (`shortDate`, `dayLabel`, `fullDay`, `weekday`, `monthYear`, `inZone`); a bare day is that calendar day and is never moved by a zone. Never call `toLocaleString`, `toLocaleDateString` or `toLocaleTimeString` for a time on a screen, and never cut an instant to its UTC day before a helper shows it (`scan.sh` refuses `prettyDate(x.createdAt.slice(0, 10))`: at 1:30am IST it reads yesterday; the helpers take an instant). Printed paper (`paperDate`, the invoice and agreement pages, the month sheet), a certificate, the policy pages and what the server words in a sentence keep their own; `todayLocal`, `lastDays` and `localDateTimeValue` stay the device's, since they are what is sent, not what is shown. Numbers pass `LOCALE`: counts go through `formatCount` (12,34,567), and `money()` groups rupees in lakh and crore and other currencies in thousands from integer paise. A person keeps their money in `users.currency` (migration 0070, INR unless changed under Profile with `PATCH /me {currency}`, one of `CURRENCIES` by `isCurrency`, read with `currencyOf` in db.ts); Finance, Life's money line and a review's period money add up only that currency and list the rest unadded, never converted. A phone without a country code is Indian (`normalizePhone`: a bare ten-digit number from 6 to 9, or one with a leading 0, gets +91; one typed with a plus is kept; `PHONE_HINT` under every phone field). KYC and a client's address take a PIN code (`pinCodeProblem`) and a state from `INDIAN_STATES`, beside PAN and IFSC; an INR invoice with GST not split prints IGST (18%). An event made with no zone (a Party event, or one the team posts) takes its host's zone, then `DEFAULT_ZONE`; any IANA zone can still be picked. Plans, top-ups and the live call are priced in `PLAN_CURRENCY` (INR, see Credits and plans).
- Auth is hand-built on Hono and D1, no package: Bearer session tokens in `sessions` (two UUIDs), PBKDF2-SHA256 at 100,000 iterations (the Workers free-plan ceiling) with a 16-byte hex salt from `randomHex`, constant-time compare. `passwordProblem` in shared is the one password rule, used by the sign-up form, the change form and both endpoints. Sign-in answers the same for an unknown email and a wrong password, hashes against a decoy salt when the email is unknown so the timing matches, and is limited per address and per email by the numbers in `apps/api/src/limits.ts`. `POST /me/password` needs the current password and deletes every other session. `secureHeaders()` runs on every API answer. Password reset: `POST /auth/forgot` answers the same for any email and mails a one-time link (32 random bytes, stored as a SHA-256 hash in `password_resets`, thirty minutes, one open per account); `POST /auth/reset` checks the rule, claims the token, sets the hash and deletes every session. Founder addresses in `apps/api/src/admins.ts` and `ADMIN_EMAILS` get the admin role on sign-up or sign-in (`promoteBootstrapAdmin`, audited); until mail verifies addresses, whoever signs up with one first gets it. A new account needs an invite code while Invite only is on (2026-11-10, see Joining by invite): `inviteGate` in `apps/api/src/invites.ts` runs in `POST /auth/anon` and in `POST /auth/signup` when it makes an account (`invite` in the body), claims the code with one guarded UPDATE before the account is made and gives it back if that fails, and answers 403 `{ error, reason: 'invite' | 'invite-unusable' }`; sign-in, a reset and a device session adding its email need none, and a founder's address (`isBootstrapAdmin`) never asks for one or uses one. Magic links were tried and removed (migrations 0018 and 0019). Sign-up also asks for a phone number and a date of birth (`phoneProblem`, `birthDateProblem` and `MIN_AGE` 18 in shared, migration 0026): nobody under eighteen gets an account, the phone can be changed under Profile, the date of birth cannot, and neither is ever shown to anyone else.
- Legal and privacy (migration 0059, `routes/legal.ts`, `components/ConsentPopup.tsx`, `PrivacyChoices.tsx`, `TermsBox.tsx`, `EmbedGate.tsx`, `lib/consent.ts`; the launch checklist is in `docs/launch-checklist.md`): `LEGAL_VERSION` in shared is the one version of the terms and privacy policy (the day they last changed, 2026-11-14 now, when AI chat's tables (Show to AI chat) joined the policy's assistant paragraph; 2026-11-13 before, when a coach's tables switched on by the person, a super admin's read of a member's tables and its audit note joined the policy and `RETENTION`; 2026-11-10 before that, when the waitlist and invite codes joined it; a change to their words, `RETENTION` or `SUBPROCESSORS` moves it, and every account then sees the notice once). Sign-up needs `acceptTerms: true` (the unticked box beside the button, `TermsBox`, in the sign-up page, the setup chat's second card and the account form), and `recordAcceptance` writes one append-only row per document and version in `terms_acceptances`; an account that has not accepted this version sees the one-time terms notice in the popup, saying the terms and privacy policy changed (`POST /me/legal/accept`). The three privacy choices (`CONSENT_PURPOSES`: the tracked-link cookie, videos from YouTube and Vimeo, marketing email) start off, are asked once in a card at the bottom left (`ConsentPopup`, mounted in `Root`; where it sits is decided by the page, not by a session (the slot is on every public page): on a public page on a phone it sits in the page's flow under the site header (`lib/consentSlot.ts`, the slot in `SiteLayout`) so it covers nothing, inside the app on a phone a floating glass card above the tab bar, and on the public site from the tablet width a full-width bar along the bottom edge (the founder, 2026-11-05: "full width on desktop is fine"): "Your privacy" with a shield, the one line, the Grievance Officer line, a quiet "Choose for each", then Reject all and Accept all side by side, equal and both outline), are stored per person (`user_consents`, with an append-only `consent_records` log of every change and where it was made) and on the device for a visitor (`lib/consent.ts`, which also writes the `kc_attr` cookie the server reads), and are changed any time under Profile, Privacy choices; the popup comes back only when `CONSENT_VERSION` changes or the footer's "Privacy choices" button asks. `/go` sets the `kl_click` cookie only where a yes is not needed first (outside `CONSENT_FIRST_COUNTRIES`: India under the DPDP Act 2023, the EU, the rest of the EEA, the UK and Switzerland) or after a yes (`kc_attr=1`), and never when the browser sends Global Privacy Control, even after an earlier yes (2026-11-05). A video from another site waits behind `EmbedGate` until videos are allowed or the person taps it. `sendEmail(..., { marketing: true })` sends only with a recorded yes and a business address, adds a one-click unsubscribe link and `List-Unsubscribe` headers (`/unsubscribe/:token`, `POST /public/unsubscribe/:token`); receipts and resets are never marketing. The business that runs kreators.app (name, address, email, registration, tax number, the Grievance Officer for India in `BUSINESS_GRIEVANCE_NAME`, `BUSINESS_GRIEVANCE_EMAIL` and the optional `BUSINESS_GRIEVANCE_PHONE`, copyright agent) is set as Worker variables (`BUSINESS_*`, `DMCA_*` in env.ts) and read from `GET /public/business`, never written in code; an empty field is shown as "not set yet", never invented. The one exception is `FALLBACK_EMAIL` in `site/legalBits.tsx`, the founder's own address, his ask of 2026-10-29 confirmed on 2026-11-07, used only until `BUSINESS_EMAIL` is set. Public pages: `/terms`, `/privacy` (its table of how long things are kept is `RETENTION`, which also names what stays after a delete, the counts of tries in `rate_limits` and a note already in someone else's inbox), `/refunds`, `/contact`, `/pricing`, `/subprocessors` (`SUBPROCESSORS`), `/copyright` (notices and counter-notices go to `copyright_notices` and the admins' inbox, `POST /public/copyright-notice`, limit `notice`), `/licences` (generated by `scripts/licences.mjs` into `public/licences.json`). `tidyOld` in cron.ts applies `RETENTION_DAYS` on the first tick of each hour, each statement through an index (migration 0114), so a quiet tick pays for no whole table; the export and the delete cover every table that holds what a person wrote or what is about them, and a request to export or delete is logged without a name in `data_requests`. `scripts/data-coverage.mjs` (run by `scan.sh`; `--table` prints the whole table) holds that promise (2026-11-07): every table with a column naming a person has a line saying its export key and what the delete does, checked against the export in routes/me.ts and `deleteAccount` in account.ts, and a new table fails the scan until it has one. The rule: what is private goes; what other people or the books rely on stays with the name and words taken out, under the account row called "Deleted member", which keeps only its id. So a review a person wrote goes (no rating is stored, so a product's average stays true to the reviews left); leads go with the account that gathered them, and a lead holding the person's own address goes too; an event, a run or a session still to come is cancelled and whoever was coming told (ticket points back, `cancelEventSeats`: each guest's refund, note and seat one transaction, twelve guests to a batch, safe to run again; a host's second tap goes on to the seats, and the cron finishes one that stopped, `finishCancelledEvents`), and the buyer of a paid question still open is told; an order, a booking, an invoice, a paid reward entry, an approved pitch, a campaign role and a past event with guests stay without their words, and what the team made (a program, published knowledge, a verified tool) without who made it. `deleteAccount` goes fifty statements to a round trip through `inBatches`, each safe to run twice, with the sessions and the account row last, so a run that stops halfway can be asked for again. The export never holds a hash, a salt or a session, nor a code someone else could use (a host's code, the calendar token, a coach's invite link, the key of a client link, team link, invoice, agreement or document, each kept only as `has_link`; 2026-11-07), with mail by subject only and clicks as counts. A note already in someone else's inbox keeps the words it was written with: it names no sender, so it cannot be found. The smoke check "Export and delete cover what a person wrote" searches every column of every table afterwards. India first, open to all (2026-11-03): the privacy page leads with the DPDP Act (the data principal's rights, consent or a use the Act allows in the ground table, the Grievance Officer first and then the Data Protection Board of India) and keeps the EU and UK ground and rights below, labelled for those countries; refunds lead with UPI, card or bank through Razorpay; prices are in rupees with GST shown before paying; the privacy popup carries one quiet line pointing questions to the Grievance Officer on `/contact`; a reply time is never stated unless the business sets one. Never claim a certification, a SOC 2, a compliance label, an uptime number or a customer logo in any copy.
- Security (the full table is docs/security/SECURITY_REPORT.md; map, runbook, secrets list and restore steps sit beside it): the site's own headers come from `apps/web/public/_headers` for static files and `apps/api/src/headers.ts` for what the Worker answers itself, and the two carry the same Content-Security-Policy (same-origin scripts, styles and fonts, Razorpay's script and frames, YouTube and Vimeo frames, no inline script). It is enforcing and was checked by browsing 118 pages (2026-11-03) with `node scripts/smoke/csp-browse.mjs`; a new third-party origin needs a line in both places and a new run of that script. HSTS has no preload. There is no CORS: the API is same-origin, so an unknown Origin gets no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, and a write with a foreign `Origin` header answers 403 `wrong origin` (no Origin, the site's own, `PUBLIC_URL`, loopback and webhooks pass). Authenticated API answers are `Cache-Control: no-store`, and so is a client's paper or a team or invite link under `/api/public`. Paths that should hold nothing (dotfiles, backups, dumps, config, source maps, `/src`) answer 404 from the Worker, never the app shell (`blockedPath` in headers.ts, mirrored in `run_worker_first` in wrangler.jsonc); `scripts/check-exposed.sh <domain>` checks a live site. A 5xx answers a plain line with a `requestId` (also `X-Request-Id`), never the error text. Any URL the Worker fetches that a person influenced goes through `safeFetch` in `safeurl.ts` (https only, no IP literal, localhost, internal name, credentials or odd port, redirects checked, timeout and size cap). A link a person types for others to open (a meeting, replay, video, event, product file, portfolio piece, hand-in or Army work) goes through `httpUrl` in db.ts (http or https only; `badLink` and `LINK_PROBLEM` refuse one out loud, never drop it). `securityAlerts` in alerts.ts tells every admin's inbox about bursts of failed sign-ins, 429s, 5xx and role changes, once an hour per kind, and each new note also goes out as one private email with counts only (`alertMail`; cron.ts hands over `sendEmail`, so the file still loads in Node; while `RESEND_API_KEY` is unset the mail waits in the outbox and the note says plainly that mail is not set up; a failed mail never stops the cron). The shared `ADMIN_KEY` on `/api/admin` has its own door (limit `adminKey`, ten tries in ten minutes per address, `ROUTE_LIMITS` `/admin/*` on every method with `when: 'adminKey'`, `adminKeyDoor` in ops.ts, the one compare, of the two hashes through `safeEqual`): each try takes a place first, a right key gives it back, once they are spent even the right key answers 429, an admin's own session is never counted, and a method with no door (OPTIONS) opens nothing. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs a frozen install, typecheck, shared tests, scan.sh, build and `perf:budget` on every push and pull request, read-only, with no secrets and actions pinned to a commit; the smoke suite stays local. The staff second factor is not built (left out for now, 2026-11-07). A password-reset link is built from `PUBLIC_URL`, never from the request's Host, and the outbox keeps no live link (`private: true` on `sendEmail`; an emailed paper and a coach's invite too, the invite under the coach's id so the export and the delete find it). `scripts/hooks/pre-commit` refuses a commit that carries a secret pattern (install it by copying it to `.git/hooks`). Session tokens in localStorage stay a recorded, mitigated risk. Never print or paste a secret value.
- Workers Free (the founder, 2026-11-07: no paid plan; numbers in docs/performance.md): one invocation stays under 50 D1 round trips (each first, all, run, raw and batch is one; the 51st fails), and any route or cron tick past 45 fails the smoke suite: `D1_GUARD` (d1Guard.ts, set only by blueprint.sh and the test server's script, one property read in production) puts `X-D1-Round-Trips`, `X-D1-Rows-Read` and `X-D1-Rows-Written` on every answer and `X-D1-Last-Tick` after a cron run. `batchedReads` (d1Batch.ts, on every invocation) sends the reads of one event-loop turn as one `db.batch`, so `Promise.all` of reads is one trip and a route needs no batch of its own; only a SELECT waits, a write goes at once after them, and a failed batch sends each read alone. Work that grows with a list checks `tripsOf(db)` against `ROUTE_TRIPS` (40) and stops while it can still answer: the assistant and the Companion (a tool runs only while a read's 8 or a write's 16 trips are left, then "go on"), a cancel's seats and a host's delete (the next ticks finish the seats), a retainer's backlog of months. The cron holds itself to `TICK_TRIPS` (38) and `TICK_STATEMENTS` (400) in cronTick.ts: every job reads first, together; those with work take turns, the alerts and then event reminders first (`first`, up to half of what is left, since an hour's reminder is worth nothing once the event starts) and the rest in an order that moves by one a tick from the clock, each with an equal part of what is left, so none starves the next. A job leaves nothing half done (a claim and its work are one batch; a note has a fixed id; a month's invoice carries its month in the one insert, so a stop never makes a second) and takes no place for someone who needs no note; while a reminder job with an hour's window (`timed`) has left work, `cron_state` says since when and the reminders' hour reaches back to it. The first tick of the hour alone runs the tidy-up; past supplement notes, the season, sequences, monthly entries, a retainer's months, stages, the weekly circle and discount uses run then too, and on the ticks after it only while one of them left work (`hourly_left`), on what the others leave (`spare`), so a backlog is never a day late and never makes a reminder late (a run by hand runs all). `node --experimental-strip-types scripts/smoke/cron-stress.mjs` (no server: 1,000 people, 100 and 900 of each reminder, a tick stopped dead at each of 40 points, a retainer's month stopped at each of 24, a rush of notes in the hour before an event) and `scripts/smoke/drive-day.mjs` (one person's day in a browser, and how many people fit) are the checks. Nothing asks number by number or person by person in a loop (`nextNumber` and `promoteNext` read once). Rows read are the first daily limit (5 million, about 115 people a day with three months of data and a changed block, about 135 with none, counting what a tick reads again), then writes and requests (about 600 and 1,000); a new account writes 307 rows, so about 325 sign-ups a day use all the writes, since the starting time blocks live in code until the person's first change to a block or a template (2026-11-07, the founder's yes): `startingSet` in timeBlocks.ts answers every read with the ids the rows get (`users.time_blocks_seeded_at` empty means code, read beside what it decides), a task, a date and a weekday need only the id, and `ensureTimeBlocks` writes the rows in one batch just before a change that passed its checks, under a claim no other tab holds, so a deleted template never comes back; that first change writes about 821 rows (780 before the three content days of 2026-11-12) (a slot whose block another tab just deleted is left out, never a 500; a seed key never goes away, a shared test holds it), a new read of these tables takes `startingSet` too (scan.sh refuses one outside those files), and the export says plainly when there is nothing of the person's own (`timeBlocksNote`). A new per-person read must not walk every task: `INDEXED BY`, a range, or `LIMIT 1` where only "any?" is asked. `GET /api/internal/platform-limit` (super admin, `SELECT 1` only) settles the live plan's real limit. Past a daily limit every D1 query fails until 05:30 IST and every screen shows its plain error line.
- Performance (docs/performance.md): every call out of the API goes through `fetchT` with a named `TIMEOUT` (http.ts) and is never retried; independent reads run together; new indexes live in their own migration with the reason beside each (0060, then 0083 for Emerging's agency read, then 0114 for what a quiet cron tick, the stage sync and the stillness read scanned: at 1,000 seeded people a quiet tick went from 18,189 rows to 9,183, and an agency tick no longer reads every piece); the module gate keeps flag settings for five seconds (`forgetAccessContext()` runs where an admin changes one). The web app loads page by page: every screen except Home, Daily, sign-in, Welcome, the setup chat, the landing page and the tab bar is `lazyNamed` (lib/lazy.ts, reloads once on a failed chunk), under one `Suspense` and `RouteFallback`; vendor code is split in vite.config.ts (shared is one chunk, with the line of the day split off as `shared-lines` and the legal lists as `shared-legal`, 2026-11-05, which keeps it at 395 kB; a file split off from shared must import nothing from it, or the two chunks cycle and the app stops); `pnpm perf:budget` fails the build when the entry exceeds 480 kB, the first download 1,500 kB or any other chunk 400 kB (after the 2026-11-05 type pass and Emerging's one list: entry 475 kB, first download 1,466 kB (2026-11-06; the privacy card now loads just after the first paint, `lazyNamed` in Root), shared 395 kB; the water card's Settings and Reminders panels are their own chunk, `components/ReminderPanels.tsx`, mounted hidden after the first paint (2026-11-06, entry 476.7 kB); `lib/api.ts` is preloaded in a chunk of its own, `app-api`, and motion's animation code loads after it, see Animation). Room for what comes next (2026-11-07): How it works' steps left the barrel, `HOW_TO` is help.ts, the subpath `@kreators/shared/help` (chunk `shared-help`), read only by the tests and the card `components/HowItWorksPanel.tsx`, which `HowItWorks` in HowTo.tsx loads the first time a screen shows the section (the place, the path check and the open state stay in HowTo.tsx; a failed fetch leaves the section out); the glossary stayed in the barrel as terms.ts. That took shared from 399.9 to 363.3 kB, the first download from 1,495.6 to 1,455.8 kB and the entry from 477.9 to 474.7 kB. `SummaryTiles` (the By period blocks' tiles) then became its own chunk, loaded after the first paint: the entry 472.2 kB and the first download 1,455.7 kB (2026-11-07; at the last gate, 2026-11-08, with the event pages, the QR code, the phone's bell and the pages in, entry 478.4 kB, first download 1,474.1 kB, shared 371.2 kB). The trim of 2026-11-07 (the founder: trim, never raise the line) took the entry to 447.9 kB and the first download to 1,443.6 kB with nothing seen changing: `/7rp` is `lazyNamed` (its `useRise` is `site/useRise.ts`, the landing's `useBookEntry` is `site/bookEntry.ts`, and `BOOK_CHAPTERS` moved into the lazy Books.tsx), the celebration's overlay is `components/CelebrationOverlay.tsx`, fetched once the first screen is drawn for someone signed in (and by `celebrate` itself), and a ruler's tick, the audio context and `unlockAudio` are `lib/audioCore.ts`, so the background sounds in `lib/sound.ts` (which re-exports them) load only with the screens that play them; after the spacing pass of 2026-11-08 the entry was 448.7 kB and the first download 1,444.4 kB, and after the phone pass (the held main action, the sideways tag rows, the lower buttons; 2026-11-09) 452.8 kB and 1,448.6 kB, and 452.9 kB and 1,448.7 kB after the seven calls that followed, and 453.0 kB and 1,448.8 kB after wave 24 (Connect people, the Admin design pass, Dev Tools; 2026-11-09), and 454.9 kB and 1,453.4 kB after wave 25 (joining by invite, its rewards and badges, the words; 2026-11-10), and 454.9 kB and 1,453.5 kB after wave 26 (staff in Connect people, a brand's team joined by a yes, a cohort's manager; 2026-11-10), and the same at the last gate, after wave 27 (Connect people by rank, place and pay, a referral paid on three days of use; 2026-11-10), and 451.8 kB and 1,450.5 kB after the search and answer engine work (2026-11-12; the site's words are their own chunk, never preloaded). A subpath file may import the rest of shared (like blockHints) only while nothing in the barrel imports it; a file split off while still in the barrel must import nothing. Statements that would run once per row in a loop go fifty to a round trip through `inBatches` (and `notifyMany`) in db.ts; D1 runs each fifty as one transaction, so only idempotent writes go in one batch. Loading placeholders are `Skeleton` (components/Skeleton.tsx), search boxes use `useDebounced` (300 ms), pictures are shrunk to WebP where the browser can, and images below the first screen are `loading="lazy"`. Real-visitor timing (`lib/vitals.ts`, `POST /api/public/vitals`, `web_vitals`, 90 days, no id, address or agent; staff read the 75th percentile at `GET /api/internal/vitals`) is skipped under Do Not Track and Global Privacy Control. `node scripts/smoke/perf-probe.mjs` loads pages with a slow CPU and Slow 4G.
- Search and answer engines (2026-11-12, docs/aeo-kit.md, its Status section says what is built and what waits): `packages/shared/src/siteMeta.ts` (`@kreators/shared/siteMeta`, its own chunk, loaded with import() by `lib/usePageMeta.ts`, never in the first download) is the one list of every public page's title (60 characters or fewer), description (120 to 170), opening and H1, the canonical answer, the fair questions (`SITE_FAQ`, `INVITE_FAQ`, `siteFaq`) and the JSON-LD builders. A new public page or app gets its entry there (`SITE_PAGES`, `FEATURE_PAGE_SLUGS`) or the shared test and `scripts/seo/build-static.mjs` fail the build. The build writes each stable page as HTML with its own head, part 5's JSON-LD and a `<noscript>` of its words, plus robots.txt, llms.txt and llms-full.txt, all from the same data (never a file in public/); the Worker writes only the head of `/`, `/events` and `/events/:id` (routes/go.ts, one round trip, a minute in the edge cache, a 503 over the shell when D1 fails) and `/sitemap.xml`, one urlset. Every address comes from `PUBLIC_URL` (Worker) or `SITE_URL` (build, and `__SITE_URL__` for the client's canonical), else https://kreators.app, never the request's host. Typed words reach a head only through `escapeHtml` (HTML) or `jsonForHtml` (JSON-LD); a business detail comes only from the Worker's business variables and is left out when empty, never invented; and words that depend on a switch (`PAYMENTS_LIVE`, a book's points in `REWARDS`) are built from it, never typed. The smoke section "Search and answer engines" checks it.
- Accessibility (WCAG 2.2 AA): ink and fill tokens in app.css were tuned for 4.5:1 and the contrast probe now reports against AA (3,375 hits became 30); `components/SkipLink.tsx` is the first stop on every screen; every dialog (`Sheet`, the assistant, the reminders bubble) uses `lib/useDialogFocus.ts` (focus moves in, Tab stays inside, Escape closes the top one, focus returns); `scripts/scan.sh` fails on an `<img>` without `alt`; the type is one family, Google Sans Flex (the founder's pick, 2026-11-05: "use only this font family"), self-hosted as two woff2 subsets under `public/fonts` (latin and latin-ext, the rupee in latin-ext; weight 300 to 800 and roundness only, about 72 kB for latin, the one font preloaded in index.html; no third party sees a visitor on load), SIL OFL, listed in `LICENSE.txt` and on `/licences`. `--font-display`, `--font-body` and `--font-mono` all name it so each role can still be tuned: eyebrows in capitals with tracking, and figures (`.font-mono`, `.type-eyebrow`, `.type-figure`) in tabular numbers. It is the plain face everywhere (ROND 0); only the two wordmarks are rounded (ROND 100, the founder's pick): the kreators.app logo at the top (`BrandMark`, `.brand-mark-face`, weight 700) and the big fluted wordmark in the public footer (`.site-footer-mark`). Never add another family and never round anything else; the one other face is Caveat, kept for a guide's handwritten line on the public site (`.guide-hand`, `site/GuideLine.tsx`, `caveat-latin.woff2`). Type follows Apple's app text styles set in Flex (2026-11-05, the founder: "follow apple best apps ui design to determine thickness and sizes of all text", with umbrel.com measured for Google Sans tracking): one semantic scale in `@theme` in app.css, each utility carrying size, leading, tracking and weight together, in rem so the browser's text size applies (`html` is 100%): `text-large-title` 34/38, `text-title1` 28/32, `text-title2` 22/26 (every plain section title and sheet title, one style per app), `text-title3` 20 (a title inside a card), `text-headline` 17/21 at 550 (card and two-line row titles, buttons), `text-body` 17 (plain list rows, inputs), `text-callout` 16, `text-prose` 16/23 (reading text), `text-subhead` 15 (the everyday secondary line, ink labels with `font-semibold`), `text-footnote` 13, `text-caption` 12, `text-caption2` 11 (the floor), `text-eyebrow` 12 caps at +0.05em, `text-figure-lg` 40 and `text-figure-hero` 64 for numbers (with `.type-figure` for tabular figures), and for the public site `text-display-1` to `-3` (hooks, 40 to 64 and smaller, growing at `md` and `lg` by the height-aware queries) and `text-lede` 18 to 20 (with `.type-context`). Cards and tick rows have their own four roles (2026-11-05, the founder: "reduce from body and increase in heading", no more than 6px in all, "a bit more space maybe 1 to 3px"): `text-card-title` 20/24 at 550 and `text-row-title` 20/24 at 450 (a `CheckRow` title, only when it has a line under it) over `text-card-note` 13/17, or `text-card-prose` 14/19 for School's program description, each 50 lighter on a dark surface like the rest. They are used only by `CheckRow`, `RowCard`, the `CardHead` cards and the icon rows. The note sits 2px lower through its own rule ("Card notes" in the components layer of app.css), so an `mt-xs` beside it still wins. A line that is a 12px caption (pre-owned, Up next, Agency rows), the Apps list, game covers, chats and paper keep the scale (the public `ListingCard` takes the card roles since 2026-11-05: the kind and the kind of thing as one eyebrow, the title and who it is from left-aligned, the app's icon in its pillar's soft colour in the top right corner with Featured or Not live before it, up to three `FactTag`s stacked at the foot with the arrow, the whole card one link). Leading and tracking are tight, after umbrel's measured curve (2026-11-05, the founder: "check for line heights and kernings to be tighter"): tracking −0.03em at 64 to −0.015em at 20, just under zero from 17 down to 13 (−0.008em at body), zero at 12 and +0.006em at 11; titles lead at about 1.1 to 1.2 (34/38, 28/32, 22/26, 20/24), labels and small text 17/21, 15/19, 13/17, 12/15, display 1.02 to 1.1, reading text 17/22 and prose 16/23; kerning, `liga` and `calt` stay on, `text-rendering: optimizeLegibility` only on large type, and macOS smoothing left on everywhere, as umbrel leaves it (turning it off on dark surfaces took about a fifth of the ink). Weights follow umbrel and the surface (2026-11-05, the founder: "the same thickness/boldness style as in umbrel.com", then "the fonts are so much more bolder, but yours are not"; measured in Chrome: at the same number Flex's strokes equal umbrel's, and what made umbrel read bolder was white on black with smoothing on, since dark ink on a light page reads about 50 weight units lighter): one fixed order, heading over label over tag over reading, on a light surface reading 450 at every size, tags and small chrome `font-medium` 480, labels, buttons, card titles and `text-headline` `font-semibold` 550, titles `font-bold` and `text-large-title` to `text-title3` 600, display and figures 550, caps eyebrows 500, and every one 50 lighter on a dark surface (reading umbrel's own 400; dark mode, `.night-card`, `.mf-panel`, `.text-on-fill`, `.fx-fglass-host`, the white fills `.bg-r1` and `.bg-r4` to `.bg-r7`, a fill whose colour is set inline from the on-fill token, and `.bg-ink` and `.text-on-ink` in light mode; the orange and yellow fills carry dark ink and keep the light weights), set as tokens in app.css ("Weights on dark surfaces"; a surface that changes them sets its reading weight again in the base layer, since a weight is inherited as a number); `font-extrabold` is a `font-bold` alias (`scripts/scan.sh` still refuses it outside paper), the site's header links and the footer's policy links take the reading weight as on umbrel, and nothing is under 400 (the hook's quiet half, `.type-hook em`, is 400 in `--color-ink-mute`). No px font size on screen text: a screen uses a role, emphasis is `font-semibold` on a role (never `font-bold`), text in `ink-soft` or `ink-mute` is Regular (the colour makes it secondary; only a value that is its row's main fact keeps semibold), a link drawn as a button is semibold like `Button`, every tab root opens at `text-large-title` (Home's greeting too), a selected state never changes weight, and hierarchy comes from size and ink colour first. Left as they were: printed paper (its weights, reading weight and text styles' weights pinned on `.legal-root`, `.legal-invoice`, `.legal-doc`, `.print-doc` and `.month-print-sheet`, so a printed page never changed), the two rounded wordmarks, the guides' Caveat lines, chart SVG text sizes, the card table's scaled text, the Mindfulness timer's and ruler's and the activity grid's CSS sizes (the panel's tabs, Start, labels and pickers take the role tokens), and glyph sizes on icons and emoji. There is no serif on a screen. Printed paper keeps its own faces (`--doc-font`, Georgia by default, sans as a choice; the typed Trial layout on Arial). A coloured fill takes its colour from the tokens (`lib/relFill.ts`), never a hex in a screen; orange fills carry dark text like yellow.
- Devices: an account can be signed in on at most `MAX_DEVICES` (3) at once. `newSession` in apps/api/src/routes/auth.ts is the one place a session is made, and it signs the oldest ones out (by `created_at`, then `rowid`) when a new one makes it four; the signed-out tokens go to `session_evictions` (migration 0051, thirty days, cleared by the cron and with the account), so a device that was pushed out gets `{ error: 'unauthorized', reason: 'devices' }` instead of a bare 401, the client keeps `kreators.signedOutNote` and the sign-in screen says why (`SignedOutNote`). A password change still signs out every other device. Help has a line on it.
- Every owner-scoped query filters by the session user id. Staff powers come from `can(user, permission)` in apps/api/src/db.ts.
- A switched-off module is refused by the server, not only hidden: `MODULE_FOR_PREFIX` and `modulesForPath` in apps/api/src/access.ts map every API prefix to its module or modules, and the middleware in index.ts answers 403 when none of them is available to the person (global flag, override, rollout or minimum role). The person's own on/off is a preference the client keeps with `Gate`. A new route file gets a line in that map or it is treated as core and always open.
- Rate limits live in one file, `apps/api/src/limits.ts`: `LIMITS` holds every name with its count and window, `ROUTE_LIMITS` says which method and path each one guards, and index.ts wires them (the doors without a session per address before the session check, everything else per person after it). Change a number there and nowhere else; `rateLimit(name)` in ops.ts reads it. A key never holds an address or a typed email (the founder, 2026-11-07): `limitKey` in ops.ts turns each into a salted hash over `CLICK_SALT`, with /go's fallback, so `rate_limits` holds neither (`signin-email:`, `forgot-email:` and every per-address key; a key by user id stays as it is), and `deleteAccount` works out the hash of the person's email to clear them. Posts, replies, likes, follows, chat messages, offers, listings, tickets, applications, run entries, attendee code claims (`claim`), coaching asks (`coachAsk`) and what a coach writes (`coachWrite`) are limited as well as the auth doors.
- Lists are served a page at a time: `PAGE_SIZES` (10, 25, 50, 100) and `Page` in shared; `pageOf(c, prefix)` and `paged(rows, ask)` in db.ts on every list route (one extra row is fetched so `more` says whether a next page exists); `Pager` and `usePage` in `components/Pager.tsx` on the screen (the page lives in the URL, a prefix tells two lists on one screen apart; a list with its own sizes passes them, as done tasks do with `DONE_PAGE_SIZES`); `page` and `onPage` on `DataTable` for the admin tables. Never a bare `LIMIT 100` on a list a person scrolls. Every list that can pass about fifty is paged (2026-11-07, the founder: "pagination for all lists"): the bar offers 10, 25 and 50 (the server still takes 100 for a picker that reads every row, and the host's guest list keeps 100 at the door, `DOOR_SIZES`); a list paged from then on is ten by default (`TEN_A_PAGE` in db.ts and Pager.tsx: what you host in Party `h`, your Store listings and offers `ml` and `mo`, Circles' upcoming `up` and your tables `gt`, Mindful's Sessions `s` on `GET /wellbeing/mindful?list=1`, a product's coupons `cp`, Win's submissions `me`, Work's applications `ap`, Priority DMs answered `an`, Audience's sent `bc`, a campaign's What happened `ev`, a cohort's learners `l` with `learnerCount` whole and progress counted in SQL), and one already paged keeps its default; the server reads the prefix the screen sends (ledger `use`, browse `s`/`e`, sales `s`, bookings `b`, reviews `r`, leads `l`, links `k`, mutes `muted`, collabs `c`, run talk `rt`, channels `cl`, followers `f`). A count beside a list stays whole, and bulk actions (Mark all read, Archive read items, Delete all archived) act on everything. A filter, view or search changed on a later page goes back to page 1 (the setter calls `reset()` or clears the prefix: Inbox's view and kind, Store's filters, Daily's area and sort, Manage's member search, a post's replies per post), and a page past the first that comes back empty (`empty` on `Page`, set by `paged`) steps back by itself in `Pager`: one page when it had rows a moment ago (its last row archived, restored or deleted), else to the first. Army's list reads only the offers waiting, and a cohort's learner reads only their own feedback and attendance. Left whole on purpose: chats and threads (read from the bottom, with Load earlier), arranged sets (a program's lessons, a product's lessons, Studio's products, link blocks), lists capped by a MAX (not to do 7, supplements 12, reminders, templates, review schedules), small previews with their full list elsewhere (a client page's last 30 log lines, 20 time entries and 12 invoices; a public page's 10 posts), a waiting list that must be answered (open Priority DMs) and the kanban lanes (Show more).
- Club benefits are code, not words: an opportunity can be `club_only` (migration 0027) and is then listed, opened and applied to only by an approved, unexpired member (`isClubMember` in db.ts), and never shown on the public site; a reviewer names a staff member as a member's manager (`POST /club/memberships/:id/manager`), shown on their Club page and sent to their inbox. A reward can change after launch only through `PATCH /win/:id` (its creator, or review or talent staff): the version goes up, the audit row says what changed, every entrant gets an inbox note, and the pool never drops under what was already paid.
- Not every product takes points: `productPayWays` in shared is the one rule (points when it has a points price, is free or is pay-what-you-want; money when it has a money price). `POST /store/:id/checkout` answers 400 "This one is paid for with money, not points" for a points order on a money-only product, before any coupon is used, and the buy box, the Studio editor's hint and the Studio list show only the ways that product can be paid.
- Payment code talks to apps/api/src/payments.ts, never to a provider SDK directly. A money order becomes `paid` only in the webhook after signature verification.
- Webhooks: verify the signature on the raw body, store the raw payload in `webhook_events`, then process. Duplicates are no-ops.
- The /go redirect writes the click after the redirect is sent and never stores a raw IP, only a salted hash.
- Journal and wellbeing data never reach brands, staff, the leaderboard or Club selection. They reach the assistant's model only for their owner, the journal only when the owner asks about it, through providers that keep no data (see the assistant below). The one other door is a life coach the person said yes to: wellbeing numbers (mood, sleep, stress, mindful minutes, movement and the eating number, never a tracker's note) and saved reviews (never their snapshot) reach that coach only while the person's own Reflection switch on that link is on; the journal and gratitude never reach a coach through the report, Patterns or the six parts, whatever is switched on (see Life coaches). The one exception (2026-11-13, the founder: "entire tables can be visible to coach"): the person may switch on whole tables of their own for one coach under All tables, the journal, gratitude and every Body and mind table included, read only, each off until they turn it on, and it reaches no other coach (see All tables). The second (2026-11-14, the founder: "give one toggle for ai chat access just next to coach button too and even in profile section"): the person may switch on whole tables for AI chat, Show to AI chat, which the assistant and the Companion read through `read_table`, never changing them, and the journal, gratitude and every Body and mind table only through a model that keeps nothing (`aiPrivateOnly`); the assistant's own tools are unchanged by it (`read_journal` still reads the journal when asked, on such a model). Staff still never see them: a super admin's view of a member's tables leaves out the journal, gratitude and every Body and mind table.
- Everything that pays out has a wait or a reviewer: task age, daily tick cap, verified views, finance permission for payouts. A task cannot be ticked before its do date (see Tasks below): `POST /nodes/:id/complete` takes the person's date and refuses an early tick, and `TaskRow` locks the box with "Tick it on the day"; the due date never holds a tick back. A ticked task stays in sight: `GET /today` returns the tasks ticked on the person's day (`done`, bounded by their zone through `zonedToUtc`), and Daily lists them under Done today, a closed toggle (`Fold`, `#done-heading`) with the day's count beside it and ten a page inside (10, 25 or 50, prefix `d`; an address naming a page or size opens it; the founder, 2026-11-08), struck through with the time; tapping one unticks it, which takes back its xp.
- Unlocks: a product, a store item or a tool can ask for a points balance, a badge, or both (`min_points`, `badge`, migration 0024). `unlockFor` in shared is the one rule and the one wording; the server computes the viewer's `Unlock` (`badgesNow` plus `honorKeys`) for the product page, every store item and every tool, refuses a gated checkout with 403, and blanks a locked item's or tool's link. Creators set it on their products, staff on items and tools, from the same two inputs (points floor, badge select over `BADGES` and `HONORS`).
- Nudges: one quiet line (`components/Nudge.tsx`, 12px, soft) under the thing it talks about, never a banner, each linking to Help, "How you earn" (`earningGuide` in shared, the one list of what pays). Every app's main screen ends with a "How it works" section (`HowItWorks` in `components/HowTo.tsx`, rendered by `Screen` from the module key that `Gate` and `explained` in App.tsx provide; it shows only when the path is the module's own path, so detail pages inside an app leave it to the main screen) with three to five concrete steps from `HOW_TO`, each a sentence or two with the numbers and the rules, then the words it leans on from `TERMS`. The section is one card that is a button (`toy toy-soft`, lifts and tints on hover, stays tinted while open, closed by default); tapping it opens the steps and the words together, each a smaller flat toggle inside (`toy-flat`, tints on hover, darker while open), and tapping it again folds everything away. Opened content slides in with GSAP (`gsap.set` under reduced motion). Never a floating button or a sheet; the section carries `span-all` so on Home's two-column grid it takes the full width. Help lists every word under "Words we use", each group a closed `Fold` with its count beside the jump pills. A new app gets a `HOW_TO` entry (the shared test insists; in help.ts, imported as `@kreators/shared/help`, never through the barrel) and new words go in `TERMS` (terms.ts, in the barrel); a screen that must not show it passes `howTo={false}`.
- Tasks (migrations 0048 and 0049, `routes/nodes.ts`, `components/TaskPlan.tsx`, `components/TaskRow.tsx`, `screens/Today.tsx`): a task has a **do date** (`do_date`, the day it will be done; it opens on Daily from that day and is the only date that holds a tick back) and an optional **due date** (`due_date`, the deadline; shown as "Due" and "Overdue", never a lock). The do date cannot come after the due date (`DATES_PROBLEM`, checked on create and edit). `openDay` is the day a task opens (do date, else due date); `GET /today` lists what is open on or before the person's day. A task with neither date is in **Not scheduled yet** (`backlog` and `backlogTotal` on `TodayResponse`, a folded list under Tasks on Daily), and never in the main list until a do date or a due date is picked; `BacklogTask` offers Today, Tomorrow and a date field, and nothing auto-fills undated tasks into the main list. **Close the unfinished** (2026-11-07, the founder's ask, then his "could just be a filter like the others with three group colors like time blocks" the same day; migration 0120 `nodes.open_kind`, `OPEN_KINDS`, `parseOpenKind` and `openKindProblem` the one reader through a `byKey` object, `openRank`, `openTag`, `isOpenFilter` in shared tasks.ts; the words in the leaf `@kreators/shared/openBusiness`, chunk `shared-open`): a task may carry one kind of **open business**, Unfinished, Unresolved or Undecided; a tag and a view only, never a change to what a tick pays, set by `openKind` on `POST /nodes` and `PATCH /nodes/:id` (an unknown value is a 400 in plain words, "none" clears it), carried by `spawnNext`, the "Bottleneck" select in `TaskPlanFields` (None or one of the three, its question under it), `OpenTag` in `TaskTags` (a warm Puzzle `IconTag`, on Daily, a pillar page, the month calendar's day sheet), the assistant's `openKind` on `add_to_pyramid` and `edit_pyramid_item` (Undo puts it back) and `get_daily`, in the export with nodes. While a task carries a kind, `TaskEndings` (in TaskPanel on Daily and the pillar page's edit sheet, under Start focus, in its look) offers **End it** three ways, each the ordinary `PATCH /nodes/:id`: **Do it** (`{doDate}` today; "On today" once it is), **Give it a time and date** (`{doDate, dueTime, remind: true}`, a form in place; a day after its due day moves the due day with it, said under the form, since a late task could otherwise never be given one) and **Let it go** (the archive, `{status: 'archived'}`, a second tap with `.red-armed`, out of every list and reminder). Daily's Sort has one more choice, **Bottlenecks** (the founder's name, 2026-11-08, so it does not clash with the kind called Unfinished; the task form's field and the row tag say Bottleneck; `taskSort=open`, not a `TASK_SORTS` key, so nothing else changes): the server cuts the lists to the three kinds (`?open=any` with `group=1`, also on `/today/waiting` and `/today/done`) and the lazy `OpenBusinessView` in `TaskGroups.tsx` draws them as By time block draws its blocks, one `tb-box` per kind in `OPEN_KINDS` order with its question, the open ones whose day has come and the undated ones together (so To remember and Not scheduled yet stand aside while it is on), in `OPEN_SWATCH`'s colours (`tb-yellow`, `tb-blue`, `tb-green`: none of the priority colours, words ink on a 12% wash; inside its own box a row's kind tag steps aside, `.tb-box[data-group-key^='open-'] .open-tag` in app.css, and the note under the controls says only the kind of day), a kind with none left out and one plain line for none at all; under the boxes a closed **Let go** fold (`letGoTotal` on `GET /today`) pages `GET /today/let-go` (owner only, prefix `lg`, 10, 25 or 50) with **Bring back** (`{status: 'active'}`). **By context** puts open business after every priority, its kinds in order (`OPEN_RANK_SQL` on the server, `openRank` in `sortTasks`). Home's Daily card ends with **Close the unfinished**, only while a bottleneck is open (the founder, 2026-11-07: hidden at 0, back with the first one, since a task write marks Home stale), on the card's own darker wash (`bg-ink-fixed/20`, so its white words keep AA on the green) with its count (`today.openBusiness` on `GET /home`, the same tasks the view lists: a kind, and a day that has come or none), to `/today?taskSort=open#tasks-heading`. The words say only that unfinished things tend to stay on the mind (a shared test bans "life force", "drain", "energy" and the effect's name). **Done tasks are paged** (2026-11-07): `DONE_PAGE_SIZES` 10, 25 or 50, ten by default (`pageOf(c, prefix, { sizes, fallback })`, `Pager` and `usePage` with `only`): Daily's Done today (the first page and `doneTotal` on `GET /today`, the rest from `GET /today/done`, prefix `d`, `components/DoneToday.tsx`, lazy), a pillar page's Done tasks (`GET /nodes/done?relationship=`, prefix `dn`) and a coach's view of a student's day (prefix `cd`); the counts stay true. A task can **repeat** (`Repeat`: daily, weekdays, weekly on chosen days, monthly, yearly, every 1 to 52, an optional end day; `parseRepeat`, `nextOccurrence` and `repeatLabel` in shared are the one rule, a month end is clamped and a leap day falls on the 28th): it needs a do date or a due date to count from, ticking it makes the next one in `spawnNext` (same place, words, note, estimate, time, priority, remind and subtasks unticked, the do to due gap kept, `spawned_from` so a tick never makes two) before the project-clear check, and unticking deletes the untouched spawned child. A repeating task wears the **Habit** tag unless the person says otherwise: "Count it as a habit" (a `Toggle` in `TaskPlanFields`, shown only while a repeat is set, on by default) is `nodes.habit` (migration 0082: NULL keeps the old rule, 1 or 0 is the person's pick; `habit` on `POST /nodes` and `PATCH /nodes/:id`, null to go back), carried to the next one by `spawnNext`, read through `taskIsHabit` in shared by `TaskTags` and `TaskRow`, offered to the assistant's `add_to_pyramid` and `edit_pyramid_item` (`habit`, and `get_daily` lists it), in the export; Emerging's Make it a habit sets it on. One the strategy session or vibe check made (`origin = 'build'`) wears **From pillar**, a coach's wears **From your coach**. **Tap a tag to filter** (2026-11-13, the founder: "apply click for those tags and make filters from them"; he chose: filter this list): in Daily's Tasks (the main list, N more waiting, the grouped views, Past due and Upcoming, To remember, Not scheduled yet and Done today) and a pillar page's Tasks to do, a task's pillar, priority, Remember, For review, Habit, From pillar, From your coach, kind of open business, area of life and time block (the block mark, or on Daily the time chip when its time falls in a block) are real buttons raised over the card's stretched control (`data-tag-filter`, the same look at rest, the tags' quiet tint on a mouse hover, `aria-pressed` while on, named by their own words (the time chip by its time and its block) and described by one hidden line, `TagFilterHint` ("Filters the list by this tag…"), so a row's description still reads its tags, never "Filter: …" (review 2026-11-13); `TagFilterContext` in `lib/taskTagFilter.ts` round those lists only, so the month sheet, Hidden tasks, a coach's view and every other place keep plain marks), and a tap turns that filter on, or off when it is on; the due badge, Ready in and the subtasks count are states and never filter. The filters live in the URL as `tags` (`parseTaskTags` in shared the one reader through `byKey` objects, `taskTagsKey` one fixed order, one of each kind, the kinds combined with AND) beside `area` (an area tag sets the Area filter); the server cuts every list and count the way it cuts by Area (`readTaskCut` in `apps/api/src/taskCut.ts`: GET /today's six, its counts and the tabs' counts, the backlog, /waiting, /past-due, /upcoming and /done; a block id that is not the person's is dropped, never a 400, and the screen drops it from the address), echoed as `tags` on `TodayResponse`, while Your day, the time plan, /hidden and /let-go stay whole; a pillar page cuts its own list on the device (`matchesTaskTags`). The chips above the list (`components/TagChips.tsx`, BacklogTask's pill, `fill-row-all`) say each filter that is on with an X ("Remember ✕"), "Clear filters" with two or more, focus to the next chip or the heading after an X, and `sayStatus` says what the list shows; an empty list says "No tasks match these filters." with Clear filters. There is still no Block dropdown. **Priority** is `P0` to `P3` (`PRIORITIES`, `parsePriority`, nullable; P0 red `--color-r1`, P1 orange `r2`, P2 purple `r6`, P3 grey `ink-mute`, the classes `.pri-0` to `.pri-3` and `.pri-chip`): it tags and sorts, and never changes what a tick pays. Daily sorts by `TASK_SORTS` (day, priority, due date, By context) through `sortTasks`, kept in the URL (`taskSort`). **Kind of day** (migration 0077 `day_types`, `DAY_TYPES`, `DAY_TYPE_KEYS` and `dayTypeOf` in shared `dayTypes.ts`, `GET` and `PUT /today/day-type` under limit `prefs`, owner only, one row a day for today or tomorrow from the device, in the export, gone with the account): one dropdown (`DayTemplateSelect` in `components/DayTypePicker.tsx` since time blocks: the person's day templates, "Follow your week" first, up to about 280px) at the left of Time blocks and Plan my week (the founder, 2026-11-06; the three are one group and always share one line on a laptop or tablet: from a 41rem section the group is right-aligned, on the Tasks heading's line with at least an `lg` gap after the title and its count whenever 36rem is left there (32rem before the View eye joined the group, 2026-11-12), else on the next line, still at the right; it grows to 640px, the dropdown up to about 280px, so only a long name such as "Follow your week (Everyday)" is shortened when the line is tight (the founder's "right aligned, a space in between" over the older never-cut rule, 2026-11-06); from 30rem to 41rem, a narrow laptop column, the title, its count and the dropdown share the first line and the two buttons take the next, half each; under 30rem, a phone, the title and its count stay alone on the first line, the dropdown takes the next at full width and the two buttons the one after, half each), then one line under it with the tabs at the left and Area and Sort as two `FilterSelect`s at the right, Sort after Area (2026-11-12, the founder: "instead of sort by call it sort and make it right aligned to area dropdown in same line"; the two move to the next line together when the line is short, and stack one per line under a 34rem column, the tabs then in equal parts), `DayTypeNote` under them with "Edit kind of day" at its bottom right; By context keeps P0 above P1 and the rest and, inside each priority, puts the day's lead pillar first and its second next. By time block (the lazy `TaskGroups` with `@kreators/shared/blockHints`, 2026-11-07; each open box's rows sit `xs` under its header on an inner wrapper) also shows a task with no block and no time inside the block `suggestBlock` picks for it (`groupWithSuggestions`, the server's per-slot minutes as the room used), after the placed ones, with a calm Suggested tag, why (from `sm`; on a phone the tag and Keep here are one line), and an outline Keep here (`PATCH /nodes/:id {timeBlockId}`), only into a block with room that is not over today and never the night's sleep (a block whose slots all run past midnight, `slotCrossesMidnight`, which `suggestBlock` never offers); a task whose time put it in a block meant for another area or pillar, when a block of the day fits it better and has room, gets one quiet line (`blockMismatch`, "Fun/social is for Friends. This fits Busy work, 3:00 pm to 5:00 pm.") with Move it (the better block's first start, in 15-minute steps and never past now today, where the whole estimate fits and overlaps no timed task of that day; none free, no line) and Keep here (picks that block, so it never asks again); after either tap focus moves to the task's Plan button; the task form says the same right under its time with a "Use <block>'s time" link, reading Daily's cached answer so it names the same block, and never stops a save; nothing is written before a tap, and By area is unchanged. Each kind names the book and pages it comes from or says it is the app's own, and it pays nothing. An identity tag can imply a kind (`dayType` on `IDENTITIES`, `dayTypeFor`), which the dropdown does not read yet. On a task row the priority (`PriorityChip`) is `CheckRow`'s `corner`, the pillar a tag in its soft colour is its `foot` and the time chip its `time`, always directly left of the pillar, the two wrapping as one pair (2026-11-13, the founder: "put the time tag on left of pillar, wrap the tags on tasks"); every other mark, Remember, Habit, For review, From pillar, the area, the due day and the subtasks, is `CheckRow`'s `marks`. Where they go is read from the card's own width (`@container` on `.check-row`, `@xl`, 36rem): a wide card (a laptop's Daily or a pillar page) has them all at its right, held to 40% so the title keeps at least 60%, wrapping onto more lines inside the card (right to left: the edit button, the priority, the time and pillar pair, which wraps inside itself when the 40% is narrower, then the marks, which drop to a line of their own right-aligned, each line filled), except that when the marks would need that second line while the title fits one line beside the time, pillar, priority and edit button, the marks go in a row of the card's whole inner width under the title, from its left edge, each at its own width, left-aligned (and only when they need more than one line there, each line filled, so none stands alone; review 2026-11-13), and the rest stay at the right level with the title; at the right the tags are drawn and reached by Tab in the order they read, left to right (the other marks, the time, the pillar, the priority, the edit button), and when the marks wrap there the pair and the priority keep the first line and the marks take the line under them (`useMarksUnder`'s four modes `line`, `stack`, `under1`, `under2`; the title's one-line width adds back the space each soft break swallowed) (2026-11-13, the founder; `useMarksUnder` in CheckRow.tsx reads each tag's own unstretched width, the title's words on one line and the card's inner width, so it never flips back and forth, again on a resize and once the font is in; a title that wraps anyway keeps the layout above); a narrower card (a phone, a tablet's column, 1024 with the sidebar open, a time block's box) keeps only the priority and the edit button at its top right and every other tag flows under the title at the card's full width, the marks filling each line (`fill-row-all`) and the time and pillar one pair at their own widths at the end, never stretched (a time with no pillar, on a pillar's own page, is one more mark); the describedby order follows the wide card's left to right (marks, time, pillar, priority); no pillar tag on a pillar's own page, and `TaskTags` takes `priority={false}`. The month calendar's day sheet keeps the pillar as its row's subtitle and the time in its badge, as before (no pillar tag there). While a task's plan is open in place on Daily, its close is `RowClose` (in CheckRow.tsx, 2026-11-13, the founder: "put the close button outside on scroll in daily tasks"): a rail as tall as the card and the plan, its button sticky so it stays in view down the plan and stops at the plan's end; with 60px of margin at the row's right (past the painted box round it, such as a time block's, and before On this page when it shows) it is the 44px round `CloseButton` 8px outside the card or box, centred on the card's first line by its sticky slot, with a "Close the plan" tip; with none (a phone, a tablet's column, a narrow laptop) the 32px `CloseButton` (`small`, a 44px hit area, no tip, since one would sit on the card's words) runs down the row's own right edge where the edit button was and the open plan keeps a 48px lane for it (`.row-lane`, padded only while the row says `data-row-close="in"`), so it never covers a field; either way it is a real button named "Close the plan for <title>", right after the card's own control in the page's order, so Tab meets it before the plan's fields; Escape anywhere in the open plan closes it too (unless a popup or a control took the key), and either hands focus back to the card, which a tap from deep in the plan brings back into view; the card's edit button keeps its slot unseen while open. By time block's and By area's boxes clip only while they slide, so the close is never cut off. "Add a task" is a `Fold` with `adds` right under Daily's task list that opens `AddTaskForm` (from `components/TaskMonth.tsx`, lazy) on today, the cursor in its title; saving folds it again and a status line under it says where the task went ("Added “…” to today's tasks.", or the day it shows from), since the new row lands higher up the list. A pillar's page has the same fold and form under Tasks to do (2026-11-07, `projects` on `AddTaskForm`: that pillar's own, so nothing is read and a task never lands on another pillar; with no project, one plain line; `?add=1`, from an Emerging card, lands with it open), and its layers keep their sheet. An open task moves to another pillar with `PATCH /nodes/:id` `{parentId}`, only under one of the person's own active projects, and takes that project's pillar (the "Pillar and project" select in `TaskPanel`, grouped by pillar); a ticked task stays. An open task's words are changed and the task is deleted from `TaskPanel` too (2026-11-06, the founder: "give ability to change the name of task too", "there is no delete button in tasks"): its first field is the task's title (`FIELD`, 140 characters, never blank, sent as `title` on the ordinary `PATCH /nodes/:id` only when it changed, so the ten-minute wait starts again only then, as the pillar page's edit sheet says), and "Delete task" (`DELETE_BTN`, the pale red outline) sits at the panel's bottom right and deletes on a second tap ("Tap again to delete", `.red-armed`, since 2026-11-13; see Confirmations) through the pillar page's own `DELETE /nodes/:id` (its subtasks go by the foreign key's cascade); a subtask's ✕ and the pillar page's "Remove this task" are second taps too. **Remember** (`remind`) is a tag, with or without a day. With a do date or due date it also sends one inbox note on that day at its time (09:00 when it has none) from `sendTaskReminders` in cron.ts (id `task-<id>-<day>`, so once, in the person's zone, never for a task that is done, put away or hidden). With no day it lives in **To remember**, a list always in sight on Daily above the folded Not scheduled yet (the backlog query puts the tagged ones first), and the inbox note starts once a day is picked. **Subtasks** (`subtasks` table, up to `MAX_SUBTASKS` 20, `GET/POST /nodes/:id/subtasks`, `PATCH/DELETE /nodes/subtasks/:id`, owner only, `withSubtasks` puts the counts on a list) are a checklist that pays nothing and never blocks the tick; they are in the export and go with erase and delete. A task's **time estimate** (minutes, chips 15, 25, 45, 60, 90) feeds **Start focus**, which opens Mindfulness at `/mindful?task=<id>&min=<n>` with the task picked and the first round set to that length (the saved lengths do not change). `TaskPlanFields` is the one form for all of it (`AddTaskForm`, the edit sheet on pillar pages, and `TaskPanel` under a task on Daily), `TaskTags` the one set of marks. **Hidden tasks** (2026-11-12, the founder: "tasks that go under a toggle tab below called hidden tasks", picked each time, "quiet, still counts"; migrations 0137 and 0138 `nodes.hidden_until` and `idx_nodes_user_hidden`; `TASK_HIDES`, `parseTaskHide`, `taskHideProblem`, `taskHideOf`, `taskHideState`, `HIDDEN_ALWAYS` in shared tasks.ts): `HideTask` in the plan panel and the edit sheet, beside the endings, opens two choices in place, Until tomorrow (back by itself on the person's next day, their zone, never the server's UTC day) or Until I unhide it, each the ordinary `PATCH /nodes/:id {hide, today}` (only the owner's; null shows it again; waiting while the plan above has changes not saved); a hidden task leaves the Today, Past due and Upcoming tabs and their counts, the six and the waiting list, Not scheduled yet, To remember, Place my day and the loose list, Home's counts and the brief, the Companion's list and a coach's view, and sends no Remember or task reminder; it keeps its dates, the month calendar marks it Hidden from Daily, and a tick pays and counts like any. Daily's last fold under Tasks, **Hidden tasks** (`components/HiddenTasks.tsx`, lazy, closed, its count beside it, only while one is hidden), pages `GET /today/hidden` (owner only, prefix `hd`, ten a page, Until tomorrow first, whole: no Area or Bottlenecks cut), each an ordinary task row with its state and an outline Unhide under the card; the news goes through one live region (`lib/sayStatus.ts`) and the keyboard's place to the fold, or Tasks' heading when none is left (`focusAfterHide`). A pillar page (2026-11-13, the founder's yes) cuts them on the screen, since `GET /nodes` is the whole pyramid and its layer counts and paces count every task: one hidden on the person's day (`taskHiddenOn`, `todayLocal`) leaves Tasks to do for a closed **Hidden** fold at that list's foot (`#pillar-hidden-tasks`, its count beside it, only while one is hidden, Until tomorrow first), each the ordinary `TaskRow` (a tick pays) with Daily's own `HiddenFoot` (exported, `back` and `place` naming the page's words and its fold and heading); the edit sheet's `HideTask pillar` says it waits there and on Daily, and once that sheet closes on a row that moved, focus goes to the fold's toggle (hidden) or Tasks to do's heading (`landAfterEdit`); with every open task hidden the list says "Nothing waiting. N hidden below."; the drawn pyramid's task layer names the first shown task; the month calendar keeps them with its Hidden from Daily mark; `/realm/self#pillar-hidden-tasks` is in the shot list. Letting a task go ends its hide. The section View menu stays as it was. The assistant's `add_to_pyramid` and `edit_pyramid_item` take `doDate`, `dueDate`, `priority`, `repeat`, `every`, `remember` and `openKind` and list them in `get_daily`. The focus dashboard in Mindful shows focus time in Minutes or Hours (`focusUnit` in the URL).
- Month calendar on Daily (`components/TaskMonth.tsx`, a closed `Fold` called "Month calendar" under the Tasks heading, downloaded only when opened; `GET /today/month?month=YYYY-MM&day=<device day>` in `routes/today.ts`, core like the rest of `/today`): a Monday-first grid (`monthGrid`, `monthShift`, `MONTH_RE` in shared; a month that is not real or a day that fails `DATE_RE` answers 400) where every day is a button showing how many tasks are still to do, a tick when all are done, and up to two titles from the tablet width up (priority dot from `.pri-0` to `.pri-3`, ticked ones struck through). A task sits on `openDay` (do date, else due date); the route is owner-scoped, bounded to `MONTH_TASKS_MAX` (500, `more` says so) and also returns the person's active projects (up to 100) for the add form. A day opens in a `Sheet`: its tasks as tick rows (the ordinary `complete` and `uncomplete`, so the do-date lock and the ten-minute wait hold) and "Add a task on this day", the pillar page's own create (`api.createNode`, a project parent, `TaskPlanFields` with the do date set to the day; no projects shows the plain "Tasks need a project" hint and a link to Pillars, nothing invented; a past day is allowed, the task then shows late). "Print this month" gives the body `printing-month`, and the portal sheet `.month-print-sheet` (hidden on screen, in app.css) is then the only thing on a landscape named page: black on white, a box per day with ☐ or ☑, the time, the title and P0 to P3 as text, a legend; the title is `Tasks YYYY-MM` while printing and comes back on `afterprint`. No new write route, so no new limit.
- Daily's tabs, View, Place my day and content days (2026-11-12, the founder's five decisions; docs/time-blocks.md has the content days and the placement rule): **Tabs.** Daily's Tasks section has a `Segmented` "Which tasks" of **Today**, **Past due** and **Upcoming**, each with its count, kept in the URL as `taskTab` (`TASK_TABS`, `parseTaskTab`, `taskTabOf` in shared tasks.ts; today the default and left out of the address). Today is open tasks whose `openDay` is the day: `GET /today` and `/today/waiting` take `tab=today` and cut the six, `tasksTotal` and "N more waiting" to it (no tab keeps the old `<= day`, so the assistant's `get_daily`, Home and older tabs are unchanged), and the counts `todayTotal`, `pastDueTotal` and `upcomingTotal` ride in the same count read (SUM of CASEs, no new trip). Past due (`GET /today/past-due`, prefix `pd`) is open before the day, oldest first, with Sort offering the day, priority and due date, kept apart in the URL as `pdSort` so the Today tab's `taskSort` never moves with it; Upcoming (`GET /today/upcoming`, prefix `uc`) is open after it, soonest first with a small day label per date; both `TodayTabResponse`, 10, 25 or 50 a page (`TASK_TAB_PAGE_SIZES`), read `INDEXED BY idx_nodes_user_open_day` (migration 0133, on `COALESCE(do_date, due_date)`, the exact expression), and refuse a day that is not real with 400. The By time block and By area views and Your day belong to the Today tab; the Bottlenecks view stands apart and is never cut by a tab (no tabs while it is on); undated tasks stay in To remember and Not scheduled yet; the tabs carry the counts and the heading says only "N of M shown" while more wait (the founder: "remove the other 7 past due"); a link to one task (`?task=`) opens the tab its day puts it on, and when it is not on the page shown that tab shows it first under "The task you opened"; an Upcoming task with a do date keeps "Tick it on the day". `useToday` in lib/queries.ts carries the tab (default `today`), so Daily and the not to do list share one answer. **View.** A round eye at the end of the Time blocks and Plan my week row opens an inline panel (not a sheet; `aria-expanded`, Escape closes) of switches that hide Daily's sections (`DAILY_SECTIONS`: Done today, To remember, Not scheduled yet, Your day, Month calendar, Habits, Not to do, Patterns), kept on the account (`users.daily_view_json`, migration 0134; `parseDailyView` the one reader, `dailyViewProblem` the one check; `PATCH /me {dailyView}`, limit `prefs` only when it carries it (`when: 'dailyView'`); on `GET /me` as `user.dailyView` and in the export, cleared by the delete, untouched by an erase; `components/DailyView.tsx`); a hidden section is not drawn, so its read is not sent; while any is hidden one quiet line says "N sections hidden" with Show all; a hash or a note that names a hidden section's anchor shows it for that visit (`dailySectionOfAnchor`); the eye is 44px at every width, and Show all hands focus to the Tasks heading (the line) or the panel's status line. **Place my day.** `autoPlace` in `@kreators/shared/blockHints` places the Today tab's open tasks with no block and no time into the day's blocks on read (By time block and Your day show each with a Placed tag and its start, and No room today with why); one outline **Place my day** (`components/PlaceMyDay.tsx`, lazy inside Your day) saves it with `POST /nodes/place` (limit `prefs`; one guarded `UPDATE … RETURNING` through the day index that writes only `due_time`, one round trip whatever the count, up to 60; skipped rows come back `changed` or `gone`). Nothing is written before the tap. It never overlaps any timed task of the day (`timedSpans` on `GET /today`, from the slot read, whatever the cut), counts a block's room only in its slots still to come on today (`blockUsedMin` with `after`), reads the 50 loose tasks in its own order, and never places a task that repeats (`spawnNext` would carry the time to every next one; it reads "Not placed" and the person gives it one, and the route refuses one too). **Content days.** Edit day, Shoot day and Plan and post day are starting templates for everyone (four new blocks: Shoot, Edit, Plan and prep, Post), brought to accounts seeded before them by Bring back the starting set, which never goes past `MAX_TIME_BLOCKS` or `MAX_DAY_TEMPLATES` (`startingToRestore`) and names on Time blocks what did not fit and why (`leftOut`). Their whys say only what the Create kind's cited line says (make, edit and publish on different days, never publish what you made that day), since they cite its pages. On such a day each content block shows its steps (`CONTENT_STEPS` in the leaf `@kreators/shared/contentDays`, chunk `shared-content-days`; `components/BlockSteps.tsx`, lazy), a list to read and never to tick, with an outline **Add to my tasks** that makes one task in that block on that day with the steps as subtasks under a project the person picks once in the block's pillar (`POST /nodes/breakdown`, limit `create`, origin `breakdown:<template>:<block>`, a second one for the same list and day 409 with the first while it is not let go; Daily then says Added with Open it; its duplicate check reads through the day and done indexes, never every task; the project picker reads only projects, `GET /nodes?kind=project`). It pays like any task. The assistant has no tool for Place my day or a breakdown yet (pending).
- Layout never leaves a lone tile: every grid of tiles or cards takes even rows (2026-11-07, the founder: "evenly distributed to maintain full widths"): `evenGrid(count, cols)` on the grid and `evenTile(i, count, cols)` on each tile, from `lib/bento.ts` (`cols` is the column count at each step, `{ base: 2, md: 4 }`, or a container's own steps, `{ base: 2, '@lg': 3, '@3xl': 4 }`, never both). The one rule is `evenRows`: the fewest rows that hold the tiles, the tiles shared so two rows differ by at most one, the shorter rows first, every tile in a row one width and every row the grid's full width (6 in 4 columns is 3 + 3, 5 is 2 + 3, 7 is 3 + 4; one row when they fit, a week of seven too); only two columns can leave a row of one, an odd count's lead, which takes the row. `evenSpans` lays it on shared tracks (k times k + 1, at most 30) and the class names it builds are listed by `@source inline` under the custom variants in app.css; `.bento` sits in the components layer so a bento list can take them.
