A hand-made bento must still come out in full rows (Home's metrics, the public home's villains and 5 W's, three then two on a laptop). `scripts/smoke/bento-probe.js` (`SHOOT_PROBE`) reports a row of a tile grid that stops short of the full width, a row whose tiles are not one width and a row of one beside rows of three or more (a cell spanning two rows is the meant feature; seven columns, sideways rows and paper are left out): 0 at 360, 390, 768 and 1280 in both themes and at 1024 and 1440 in light (2026-11-07). A list whose count changes takes the rule too, a paged one included (Explore, Knowledge, Content's folders, Manage's tiles, business metrics, Agency's invoice templates: on a long page the shorter rows come first, and they are the wider ones); fields of a form and a picker whose columns line up (Library's time slots) are not tiles. Past ten tiles a block reads in categories (2026-11-07, the founder: "whenever there are more than 10-12 keep them all in seperate categories"): `components/TileGroups.tsx` is the one part (`TILE_BLOCK_MAX` 10; groups of `{key, name, tiles}`, `flat` for the order at or under the limit, `cols`, `render`, `list`), a category's name in the quiet eyebrow, then its own `evenGrid` and `evenTile` rows; a category with nothing to show goes, and at or under the limit it is the one even block it was, with no heading. Home's By period alone stays one even block past the limit, at every width (`whole` on `PeriodPanel`, `SummaryTiles` and `TileGroups`, the founder, 2026-11-07: 13 tiles on a phone under seven headings, three over one tile each, took the section from 1,004 to 1,434 px); it writes its why in `data-tile-list`, the door `bento-probe.js` already has, and the limit stays ten everywhere else. At a grid's widest step short categories that fit share a line (Emotional's three beside Spiritual's one), each taking its tile count of the columns, so every tile on the line is one width and one tile never takes a laptop's row. `SummaryTiles`: Tasks and days, the five areas of life in `LIFE_AREA_ORDER` holding what the life dashboard gives them, then Work and learning, then People (a tile label outside `TILE_CATEGORIES` is a type error); a saved review draws the same way, and only Home, Life and Reviews pass ten. Also Public's badges by family and Awarded, Admin's "What does this person see?" by app group, the Leaderboard's goal boards by `SETUP_GOAL_GROUPS`, a coach's Report numbers and a student's patterns, Manage's queue tiles (By permission, then Tools, once Connect people made an admin's thirteen; 2026-11-10), and /7rp's Show all (`site/SevenRPMore.tsx`, its own chunk, since /7rp is in the first download). `bento-probe.js` reports a grid of more than 12 tiles; a paged or person-made list says so with `data-tile-list="<why>"` (Explore, Knowledge, Store's two lists and your own listings, Content's folders, your own and house invoice templates, the build map's checklists), since a page cannot be split honestly. The half-width sections on Home pair up (Life, Knowledge and Money are the optional ones; when an odd number is on, the last takes the whole row), with Your pillars and Emerging one pair of their own (2026-11-13, the founder: Emerging the left half and Your pillars the right, one row on the laptop's two columns and one height, `.home-pair` in app.css, Emerging's cards one column in its half; on a phone and a tablet Emerging right above Your pillars; Emerging holds its half while its patterns or its code load (`paired` on `EmergenceSlot`: a skeleton under its heading, so the row never jumps), and with nothing ready its quiet line sits in a card that fills the half under the lede "Patterns in what you log.", the pair staying; only while Emerging draws nothing at all do the pillars take the row). Every widget on Home belongs to its app and shows only while that app is on for the person (the founder, 2026-11-06, `isOn` in Home.tsx over `useModules`' `on`): the metric cards (Life, Mood, Wellbeing: three make the bento, two sit as halves, one takes the row), This week's lines, the Trackers rows, Up next (Party, Kreators Run, Circles; a session needs My purchases, a booking you host the Shop), the Companion, Life, Knowledge, Money, the Shop, the period tiles and their Review it link (`keep` on `PeriodPanel`), the header's mood link and Breathe and focus; Home's numbers, Daily, the pillars, xp, the brief, Emerging, water and Books stay. The four halves under the brief (Daily, Trackers, Up next, Companion) pair up by the same rule, the last of an odd number taking the row. Home's xp is one full-width section, "Xp, last 14 days", with a `Segmented` By day (the line and the task dots) or By source (the donut), kept in the URL as `?xp=source`. Home's Go to is picture tiles (2026-11-07, the founder's reference; `components/AppTile.tsx`, `.app-tile` and the `--app-tile-<pillar>` tokens in app.css): on a phone and a phone on its side each group is one sideways `.scroll-row` of 145px tiles with proximity snap (a group of one or two grows to fill the row, `flex-[1_0_145px]`), and from `md` a grid by `GO_COLS` (three, four from `lg`, five from `xl`) through `evenGrid` and `evenTile`, 168px tall; each tile is one `toy` link, its name in `text-headline` at the top left over a bright graded matte fill in the group's colour under dark ink in both themes (from pixels 8:1 or better in light, 6.3:1 or better in dark, the title's light weight kept in dark), and under a mouse the fill deepens and the name turns white (the founder, 2026-11-07, his own exception to "never a new filled hover": `--app-tile-hover-<pillar>`, the lightest each hue can go with white at 4.5:1, one set for both themes, a layer that fades in; the weight never changes, a touch never sticks), and a picture at the bottom right, 124px, 8px over both edges, in the flow under the name so it can never cover it. A picture's file is the module's key (`public/app-art/<key>.webp`, no list in the first download, not precached), one that fails gives way to the app's `AppIcon` at 72px, and `scripts/app-art/build.mjs` is the one list (module, Fluent asset, why, at a pinned commit): `--check`, run by `scripts/scan.sh`, fails when a module has no picture or two share one, so a new app brings its picture. Section ledes balance (`text-balance` in `Section`) so a line never ends on one word, and a lede never repeats the number its hero or score already shows. A wrapping row of pills or buttons never leaves one alone on its last line either: `.fill-row` (phone) and `.fill-row-all` in app.css let the items fill each line (DataTable's filter row, Explore's card facts, the feature pages' pill lists, Profile's badges and the build map's legend among them), and `scripts/smoke/lone-probe.js` (`SHOOT_PROBE`) must report 0 at 360, 390, 768 and 1280 in both themes.
- From the Whop study (`docs/whop-study.md`, which also says what was left out and why): a reward page carries a live "What could I earn?" (`EarnCalc` in `screens/Win.tsx`, the same `bountyPayout` the reviewer uses, with where the payout starts and where it stops growing), and a product can carry up to `MAX_FAQ` (6) creator-written questions and answers (`products.faq_json`, migration 0052, `parseFaq` in shared the one reader, set with `faq` on `POST` and `PATCH /studio/products`, edited in the product editor, shown as a fold-out list at the foot of the product page, public like the rest of the page). Observability's Discover block counts each add-on finished and how many switched "how your mind works" on, counts only.
- Typed words reach a LIKE through `likeFit` in db.ts, since D1 refuses a pattern over 50 bytes (17 Hindi letters) with a 500. Search (`SearchBox`) shows apps and help from the client and tasks, journal, knowledge, products, people, store items, pre-owned listings, tools and community posts from `GET /search`, grouped, under the box as you type. Life's five bars are labelled by name. The five areas of life always go physical, financial, mental, emotional, spiritual (the founder's rule, 2026-11-04: each one holds up the next, so when one fails it pulls down the one after it and when it is strong it lifts it): `LIFE_AREA_ORDER` in shared is the one order, `LIFE_AREAS` keeps its keys in it, `GET /life` (and Home's gauges through it), `/7rp`'s five picks, help, setup lines and every sentence that names them follow it, and a shared test and the smoke check it; the seven pillars keep their own order. The public site is three pages (beside Explore, the events list `/events` and each public Party event's own page `/events/:id`, where a visitor joins and signs up; see Event first, setup later under Onboarding): `/` tells the story in the PASTOR order (the problem, the five villains and what they cost, the story where the person is the hero and the guides help, the 5 W’s, how each villain is beaten with the apps that do it, before and after with the live numbers, the offer, the one next step), `/features` lists everything a creator needs by group (its top is the eyebrow, the hook and one line, "Courses, gigs and money in one place. Keep only the apps you use.", with Bumling's line beside it; no helper line, 2026-11-05), and `/7rp` lists every app a relationship serves and can switch to the five areas of life (`LIFE_AREA_FEATURES`); it shows one pillar (or area) at a time, an inset list on the left (a row of pills on a phone) and a calm panel on the right with the guide, its question and the first six apps as tiles, the rest behind "Show all" (`Explorer` in `site/SevenRP.tsx`, the choice in the URL as `pillar`), never every pillar stacked with a wall of buttons and never a fixed band along the bottom. Claims on `/` are only what the app does; it never shows a made-up quote or number. While Invite only is on (`publicApi.join`, `GET /api/public/join`), the home's Fair questions add "Do I need an invite?" (`INVITE_FAQ` in `@kreators/shared/siteMeta`) second and /pricing's fine print one line, each gone when it is off; /terms says a new account needs a code while joining is by invite (2026-11-10). The site's buttons are three looks, one job each (`site/buttons.ts`): `SITE_PRIMARY` is the one main action of a page or band (Start free, Go to app; inside `LiquidMetal` only on the home hero, and on the last call to action, where the look is `SITE_PRIMARY_ON_GLASS` on the fractal glass; `SITE_PRIMARY_ON_INK` on an ink band), `SITE_SECONDARY` every other action button (no fill, a hairline edge, semibold words, `SiteArrow` after them when it opens another page; `SITE_CHIP` its small size; the main action is 48, the secondary and the header's Go to app 40, a chip 36, at every width), and `SITE_LINK` underlined words for a jump (`SITE_LINK_INLINE` inside a sentence); bands that follow each other never switch looks for the same kind of action, and a button never looks like a guide's speech. A guide speaks through `site/GuideLine.tsx`: the guide over its name in one small grid column, the line handwritten in Caveat inside a drawn blob in the speaker's colour (a 7% fill, a curl toward the guide, no edge, no lift, no hover) centred on the guide's face, the guide in the page margin from 1200px and at most 72px on a phone. The home hero (2026-11-05, after the founder's reference, then his changes the same day) has no box of its own: it sits on the page's wash (no panel, edge, radius or top band) and reads well in both themes through tokens (`--hero-metal-*`, `--knot-shade`, `--knot-rim`, `--hero-glow` in app.css, light and dark sets; text AA sampled from pixels at 360 to 1600 in both themes). In order: one row over the hero, a `StatusTag` with the seven dots ("Seven relationships, one life") at the left and the audience line ("For creators, freelancers, founders and students.", `text-subhead` in `ink-soft`, reading weight; the 18-and-over rule lives in the terms, at sign-up and on the green card, not in this line) right-aligned on the same baseline, the line dropping under the tag on a phone and, where it must wrap, breaking on purpose after "freelancers," (two `inline-block` parts); the hook "Grow your work without losing your life" in metal ink (`.hero-metal`: each `TextReveal` word clipped to a gradient, dark slate leaning violet in light mode and silver in dark mode, the quiet half dimmer; each word's box is grown by 0.16em and taken back with a negative margin so no descender or ascender is ever cut, checked by comparing its ink with plain text at every width; hand-broken from the tablet up with `br` parts that carry a class); the knot (`components/fx/SevenKnot.tsx`: one glossy tube tied as a trefoil in the seven pillar colours, "seven relationships, one life", drawn once onto a canvas by `fx/knotDraw.ts`, its own small chunk after the first paint, from the tokens: a ball swept along the strand into a depth buffer pixel by pixel, each pixel lit by the tube's own normal, so every crossing is a clean over-and-under with a shadow under the near strand, a tight highlight and a thin rim; redrawn on a resize or a theme change; a blurred glow of pillar colours fades out round it so no edge shows; it is drawn at 98% of its layout box (`scale: 0.98`, from its left edge from the tablet up; the founder first asked for "a bit bigger", then "smaller by 10%"), 65% of the column wide and at most 270px on a phone and from the tablet up reaches back over the end of the hook (`--knot-reach`, with `--knot-grow` holding its size apart and `--knot-lean` following the dark hook's narrower words), in front of it, crossing about a tenth of "life" (12.1% of its ink in light and 13.5% in dark at 1280, 1440 and 1600, 2026-11-07) and never another word, the pitch, Start free or the tag row (measured from pixels; on a phone it sits under the hook and covers nothing; the hook stays a real text node and the knot is `aria-hidden` with no pointer events), sways a few degrees with GSAP, paused off screen and in a hidden tab, still under reduced motion, and leans to a mouse); the pitch with the page's one main action under it (Start free to Welcome, `SITE_PRIMARY` in `LiquidMetal` and `Magnet`; with a session it reads Open Daily, as the line above it says, so Go to app shows only in the header); the touchstone beside a sprout chip and the See how it works jump to `#story`; and three bold cards at its foot, facts only: the yellow "Apps, each one a switch" card is one link to `/features` (named "See every feature: N apps, each one a switch", N from `SITE_FEATURES`, 43 now; the toggle icon top right, the count from `SITE_FEATURES` as its figure, "See every feature" with `ArrowUpRight` and `.read-arrow` as text inside the link at the bottom right), a dark one (`night-card`) with the seven guides stacked and the live member count only when over 0, and a green "No card needed" with the 18-and-over line (its green deepened toward `--fill-shade`, so white stays AA at its top left). The grid is `.hero-body` (one column on a phone in the order hook, knot, pitch and Start free, touchstone; two columns on a tablet with an `lg` row gap; three on a laptop with `lg` column gaps either side of the knot, the hero a gap wider than the page column each side so the knot keeps its size), and the cards sit `xl` under it: room round the knot, never over the pitch or the button (the founder, 2026-11-05: more breathing space), with the hook and Start free on the first screen at 1280x800 and 1600x900. The problem's eyebrow, hook and two lines are the heading of the five villains under it, at the page's width (no band of its own and no "Meet the five villains", 2026-11-05). Bumling's first line is the Problem band's lead-in, on the right (`side="right"`), with 64px above and below it from the tablet up (32px below on a phone). The home keeps three guide lines (Bumling after the hero and in the story, Sunny at the offer), one idea a band, and no row of app pills in the hero. The founder's three books (`BOOKS` in shared `books.ts`, his own words only, covers in `public/books`) are `components/BookShelf.tsx` on Home's Books section, the public home and the foot of Features: flush cards, each cover whole and never cropped, a strip in the book's colour with its flower and title; from `sm` three equal cards left to right (The Relationship Method, the 20s book, the Creators book; the founder, 2026-11-06, in place of the large one beside two), stacked full width on a phone; a 3D tilt and a light over the cover for a mouse or the keyboard only. Each opens its place on `/features/books` (`site/Books.tsx`) or in the app on `/book` (`screens/Book.tsx`, not a module, `howTo={false}`): the Shop product (made a draft, unpriced, under an admin account by `scripts/books/create-products.mjs`; a draft reads "Book: coming soon", a live one opens its product page), the School course and Rewards for points, all from the public, read-only `GET /public/books` (`routes/books.ts`, core, no limit, names nobody). The footer is laid out after the founder's FyndFlow footer (2026-11-05; it sits 64px under the page, its own margin, since `main` keeps no bottom padding, 2026-11-08): the logo and a glass card (the one line, the tagline, the theme switch) on the left, the links in three headed columns on the right (Explore, Get started, Legal; on a phone the first two side by side and Legal across both in two columns, every link on one line at 44px), a hairline, a bottom bar (© year kreators.app and Built with ❤️ in India by BrandingBum on the left, Terms, Privacy and Privacy choices on the right), the privacy line, the emergency line (kreators.app cannot help in an emergency; the local emergency number or the nearest hospital, someone you trust, and Tele-MANAS on 14416 in India), then the wordmark as wide as the footer (`.site-footer-mark`, CSS content so it is never a text node, the pillar colours cut into fluted ribs, fading into the ground band; `clamp(40px, 15.5vw, 236px)`, so it fits a phone whole; it is a link to the home, 44px or taller, and on a mouse hover or keyboard focus it rises a little and fades less so more of the letters show, still under reduced motion). `/features` opens with one composition: the eyebrow, the hook and one lede line as one block, and from the laptop width Bumling beside it, its foot level with the lede's last line (bottom-aligned, the founder 2026-11-05; a phone stacks); `/features/books` and every `/features/<app>` put their 128px guide beside the words only from 1200px, under them at the right below that. The site's header and footer are the same width on every page (`max-w-[1080px]` in `SiteLayout`); only the reading column narrows (720px). The logo everywhere is `components/BrandMark.tsx`, one wordmark: kreators.app in the display face with the dot in the seven pillar colours (`.brand-dot`), no character in it; `compact` shows k and the dot under 380px (the phone corner); screen readers hear "kreators.app".
- Leaderboard (`leaderboard`, group Essentials, on for every account, path `/leaderboard`): boards by `LEADERBOARD_SCOPES` — everyone, goal (`SETUP_GOALS`, read from `intake_json`), pillar (that pillar's own xp from `relationship_scores`), city, identity tag and experience level — each ranked by xp, this month, streak or badges, paged, over a pool of the top `LEADERBOARD_POOL` people who opted in. Badge counts skip the private families. People appear only after they opt in and finish setup; nobody blocked either way with the viewer is listed; journal and wellbeing never count; no date of birth or age band is ever used. `GET /leaderboard/options` lists what can be picked, `GET /leaderboard/blocks` gives a small top-three board for every goal and pillar; both read `board_cache` (migration 0118, three hours, ids and numbers only, emptied by any account delete), and the blocks read who shows fresh (the viewer's blocks, still opted in, the name as it is now). The xp and streak boards rank from the person's row and read the badge counts only for who is on the page; the season and badge sorts visit everyone's tasks, so they are kept six hours. A new account has no `modules` override for it, so it is on by default; the setup chat never switches it (not a setup-switchable group).
- All tables (`tables`, group Essentials, on for every account, existing ones too, and not locked, so it can be hidden; path `/tables`; 2026-11-13, the founder: every list of a person's own data as a table; the registry is the leaf `@kreators/shared/dataTables`, chunk `shared-tables`, never in the first download, whose head comment is the contract, with the screens `lazyNamed`): `/tables` wears the cover and lists 55 tables in nine groups (Daily, Pillars, Reviews, Body and mind, Journal, Money, Learning, People, Account; `DATA_TABLES`, `listTables`) with no row counts, since a count reads every row, each opening `/tables/:table`, a page of its own with a `PageHeader` back to the list. `DATA_TABLES` is an allowlist of tables and columns: SQL is built only from its strings (`tableSql`: `?1` is always the subject's id, everything typed is bound, a search through `likePattern` that fits D1's 50 bytes, ORDER BY only a sortable column with the id last, nulls last), every read filters by the owner column, a key a person types is looked up through `byKey`, an unknown table is 404, and no hash, salt, token, secret, password, session, link key or code column is ever listed (a shared test reads every column by its name). The grid is `ServerTable` (`components/ServerTable.tsx`, in `DataTable`'s look) with the server doing the work: tap a header to sort, a `FilterSelect` per filterable column and a date range where it helps, a search (`useDebounced`, 300 ms), and a change to any of them goes back to page 1; rows a page 10, 25, 50 or 100 at the bottom left, always shown, ten by default (`TABLE_DEFAULT_SIZE`, the founder, 2026-11-14, for the owner's, a coach's and an admin's view alike), and "Page 2 of 7" with its buttons at the bottom right; page, size, sort, dir, `q`, `f_<column>` and `view` live in the address (`useParamState`, `parseTableQuery` the one reader, `tableQueryString` the one writer), so Back and a reload keep them. Tick rows (the header ticks the page, "3 selected") to Copy them (tab-separated, `toTsv`, through `copyPlain`) or download them as CSV (`toCsv`; a word cell that starts like a formula gets a leading quote), or Download all from the server (`GET /tables/:table/csv`, every row that matches, at most `csvMaxRows`: `CSV_MAX_ROWS` 5,000, fewer on a wide table, since a file holds at most `CSV_MAX_CELLS` 40,000 cells (tasks 1,739 rows; 5,000 rows of a wide table took 30 to 55 ms of CPU), `X-Table-Capped` past it, one round trip, `editableKeys` and the person's lists' cut skipped; limits `tablesCsv` 30 an hour and `tablesCsvDay` 10 a day, per person across the owner's, a coach's and an admin's downloads, so about 50,000 rows a day; a daily limit's 429 says "too many tries today, try again tomorrow"). Dates and times go through lib/date.ts, money through `money()` from integer paise, yes or no as words, choices by their words and stored JSON as a line of words (`LEAF_DERIVES`, `SERVER_DERIVES`), never raw JSON. **Edit in place** (owner only, the Active view): a cell with `edit` opens its input on a tap or Enter, Enter or leaving it saves, Escape cancels, shown at once and, on a refusal, only that row's changed cells rolled back with an `ErrorLine` (a 404 or 409 reads the page again); saves of one row go one after another, each under the id the one before left it (a day's log gets a new id each save), and a ticked row follows its edit; `POST /tables/:table/edit` re-reads the row by its owner, checks `rowEditable` and `editValue` (a repeating task's only do or due date is never emptied, a not to do day is offered only while Daily's route would take it, and an empty gratitude line takes the prompt Journal offers now), and sends the app's own route as the person through the Hono app's own fetch with their Bearer token and `X-Kreators-Via: tables` (the assistant's dispatch caller, one action, two callers), so the session, module switches, limits, waits, xp caps and owner filters hold and a 4xx comes back as it is; a column no route edits is read only, and ledgers and records always are (xp, points, credits, orders, certificates, badges, honors, signed or sent paper, a review's snapshot, coach notes and the log, friends, followers, other people's posts and replies; each such table says why in `readOnlyWhy`). **Archive** only where the app already had one: tasks (`PATCH /nodes/:id {status}`, the Let go list) and inbox notes (`POST /inbox/:id/archive`); there a `Segmented` Active or Archived (`view=archived`), bulk Archive and Restore, and in Archived Delete forever asked once for the set in `ConfirmDelete` (`POST /tables/:table/bulk`, at most `BULK_MAX` 100 ids, each one delegated call in sequence while `tripsOf` stays clear of `ROUTE_TRIPS`, what was not reached coming back in `left` for the screen to send again; the server checks a row is in the archive before it deletes, and the delete itself takes only a row still archived: a task's `DELETE /nodes/:id?archived=1` answers 409 once it was brought back, and an inbox note's `DELETE /inbox/:id` was always archive only, so a row restored in another tab in between stays); every other table has no archive and no delete. Workers Free: one round trip a page (the rows with LIMIT size + 1 for `more`, and a `COUNT(*)` only with `count=1`, the first load and after a filter or search change, over a subquery with `LIMIT -1` so SQLite drops the LEFT JOINs nothing reads, in one `Promise.all` that `batchedReads` sends as one batch), every read through an index on its owner column (migration 0144, a reason beside each; 0147 adds the default sorts of water, focus rounds, subtasks and followers), the not to do streaks reaching back as far as Daily's, limit `tables` (600 an hour) on the reads, edits under `prefs` and the delegated route's own limit, React Query with `keepPreviousData`. Its log area `tables` is private like the journal's (`PRIVATE_AREAS` in logs.ts). **AI chat** (2026-11-14, the founder: "give one toggle for ai chat access just next to coach button too and even in profile section") reads a table only when the person opened it to AI chat: `users.ai_tables_json` (migration 0153, a JSON list of table keys, NULL none; `parseAiTables` the one reader; on `GET /me` as `user.aiTables`, in the export, cleared by the delete, untouched by an erase), changed by `PATCH /me/ai-tables` (core, limit `prefs`; `{ table, on }` one table applied to the list as it stands inside a write guarded on the list it read, tried once more then 409, or `{ tables }` the whole list), from **Show to AI chat** in the table's cover beside Show to my coach (`components/TableSwitches.tsx`, shown while the assistant or the Companion is on) and from Profile's "AI chat" section (`components/AiChatTables.tsx`, `#ai-chat`, a closed fold "Tables AI chat can read", `#ai-tables`, drawing `AiTablesShare` over the same `TableShareList` that Tables your coach can see uses: a switch sends `{ table, on }`, Share all and Share none the list). The assistant's `read_table` tool (read, needs `tables`, offered to the Companion too in `COMPANION_TOOLS`) lists the open tables with no table, else reads one page through the owner's own `GET /tables/:table` as words (`cellText`), and refuses the journal, gratitude and every Body and mind table (`aiPrivateOnly`, the `adminHidden` ones) to a model that may keep data (`ToolIO.private`, set by `modelKeepsNothing` in model.ts for both chats: the host's promise and the list's flag, so a model the list marks as keeping data never counts as private on another host); a page is 10 rows unless 25 are asked, empty cells left out, a stamp in the person's own zone through `localStamp`, and held under 7,000 characters (the Companion's cap is 8,000) by leaving out its last rows and saying which rows and where they are at size 10; the route checks it too: a call marked `X-Kreators-Via: assistant` (the dispatcher's mark) lists only the open tables, reads only those (403 in words naming the switch), and is refused every download, edit, bulk action, coach's and admin's view. **A coach** sees a table only when the student switched it on for that one coach: My coach's closed "Tables your coach can see" fold on the student's Profile tab, one `Toggle` a table in the list's groups with Share all and Share none, every switch off at first (the switch is the student's consent), and the same yes on each table's own page (2026-11-14, the founder: "in each table give toggle to show it to coach or not, give better name to that toggle switch, put it in hero section button position on left on large screens and below helper text on mobile"): **Show to my coach** (`components/TableSwitches.tsx`, the owner's view only, one compact `Toggle` per active link, "Show to my coach" with "<coach> can read it, never change it." under it for one coach, "Show to my coach, <coach>" each for several, nothing without a coach; a waiting ask keeps its switches on the Profile tab; the All tables list reads the coaches ahead, `TABLE_COACHES_KEY`, so the switch is in the cover from the first paint) in the cover's action slot beside Show to AI chat, side by side left of the helper line from the tablet up and one under the other on a line of their own under it on a phone (`.cover-share-switches` in app.css), each tap sending only `{ table, on }` to the same share route, which adds or takes that one table from the list as it stands inside its guarded write (a bad table, a missing or non-boolean `on`, or `tables` beside it is a 400), the taps in order, the switch showing the newest until the server's answer is written into the page's copy; the Profile fold's switches (`TableShareList`, shared with AI chat's list) send one table at a time too, so neither place can put back a table switched off in the other, kept in `coach_links.share_json` as `tables` (`CoachShare.tables`; `parseCoachShare` keeps keys by shape and the server only `knownTableKeys`), changed through `PATCH /me/coach/links/:id/share` with one `share.change` line naming the tables in words, never their contents (the write is guarded on the share it read and tried once more, so a part and a table switched at once both land, else 409); the coach's student page has an outline Tables link to `/coaching/students/:id/tables` and `/coaching/students/:id/tables/:table`, the same grid read only (sort, filter, search, paging, ticks, Copy and CSV; no edit, no archive), served by `/tables/coach/:studentId` (access map `tables/coach`, the Coaching switch), which checks on every request that the link is active, the viewer is its coach and still holds `coach`, and the table is shared, else 404; a coach never gets an anonymous post or reply, nor what would say who wrote one (`coachWhere`: no `social:` xp or point, which lands the moment a line is written, and no reply, thread, moderation or team note in the Inbox), only their own notes, nobody in Friends, Following or Followers with a block by name either way with the coach, and in `options` only the blocks, templates and items the rows on the page use, never the student's whole list. That is the one door by which the journal, gratitude, money, water, focus, time blocks, supplements and protein reach a coach; the report, Patterns and the six parts stay exactly as they were. **A super admin** (role `admin` with a real session, never the shared `ADMIN_KEY`) opens a member's tables from Admin, People's Tables action at `/admin/people/:id/tables` and `/admin/people/:id/tables/:table`, read only (no edit or bulk; a CSV download of the rows that match is its own `GET /tables/admin/:userId/:table/csv`), served by `/tables/admin/:userId` after the session check (access map `tables/admin`, 403 to any other role); never the journal, gratitude or any Body and mind table (mood, sleep, stress, mindful minutes, movement, eating, water, focus sessions, supplements and supplement days are `adminHidden`: left out of the list and 404), never a deleted member, never an inbox note's words or link (`ownerOnly`), never a supplement or focus nudge in the Inbox (`othersWhere`, body and mind, kept from a coach too), and every read is in the audit log, never what it holds: a page read writes a `tables.view` row (the member's id and the table's key, at most one per admin, member and table in half an hour, a guarded insert, so paging adds no noise) and every CSV download a `tables.csv` row; the member's export lists when. The privacy policy says both (`LEGAL_VERSION` 2026-11-13: a ground row each, Who sees what, Security, Life coaches; and 2026-11-14 for AI chat's tables: a ground row, Who sees what's supplements line and the assistant paragraph) and `RETENTION` names the audit note.
- Media, event extras and blocking (migration 0037, 0036): every listing type and School lesson can carry pictures, GIFs and videos (`media_items`, `MediaOwner`, `MAX_MEDIA_ITEMS` 8). A picture or GIF is uploaded (`POST /media/upload`, `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` 1 MB, JPEG, PNG, WebP, AVIF or GIF, checked by its first bytes, base64 in D1 `media_files`, served from `/api/public/media/:id`; the browser shrinks pictures first, `lib/shrink.ts`) or linked; a video is only ever a link (`videoEmbed`: YouTube and Vimeo in their own player, a direct .mp4/.webm). `canEditMedia` in routes/media.ts is the one rule for who may change an owner's set; `listingsFor` attaches `media` so cards show a cover (`MediaCover`), and `MediaSection` is the block every detail page drops in (with `trigger={null}` and `open`/`onOpenChange`, like `MoreMenu`, its add button moves into the page's More menu through `useMediaMenuItem`, null for someone who may not edit; Store, Tools, the product editor, a Work opportunity, a Win reward and a Party event do this, the cover's More menu, so no add button sits loose between the cover and the first card, 2026-11-08). Uploading to R2 for video is not built. Inside Party events and runs (`EventApp`; circles stay anonymous and have none of it) `components/EventPanel.tsx` gives the host's photo-folder link (`photoLinkProblem`, `PHOTO_HOSTS`: Google Drive and Photos, Dropbox, iCloud, OneDrive, WeTransfer; guests are told once), a conversation for guests and host (`event_messages`, open until `CHAT_DAYS_AFTER` after the end, limit `message`) and the guests who chose to be listed (`event_networking`, `networking_opt_in`) to send a friend request through `requestFriend`. Anyone blocks anyone (`POST /bond/block`, ends a friendship and any coaching, hides posts, replies, event lines and leaderboard rows both ways; `GET /bond/blocks`, `DELETE /bond/block/:userId`). What a block does is one rule in db.ts (2026-11-07, after the review found both directions told who an anonymous writer was): a block by name (`blocks.by_name = 1`, `blockedByName`, `blockedEither`) works both ways on everything with a name on it; a block behind a made-up name (`block_aliases`) touches only anonymous writing; neither crosses over, since blocking a suspect by name and seeing an anonymous post vanish, or the other way round, would unmask them. `hiddenByBlock(viewer, writer, anon)` is the check for any line that may be anonymous (a post, reply, channel, event or run line, a cohort line by a private member), and the writer's own block of the viewer always hides. A note follows the line (`seenBy`): anyone a block keeps it from hears nothing, and a blocked person also cannot react to, follow or answer what they cannot see, follow the blocker, make an offer, write in a listing's chat or ask a blocker to coach them; each answers as for nothing there. A circle's alias chat reads only the other person's block. A guide is anyone holding the `moderation` permission, a creator included (`CREATOR_PERMISSIONS`; `isGuide` on the server, `isStaffFor(user, 'moderation')` on the screen; 2026-11-05), and reaches the guide's tools under `/manage/community` (hide, pin, held first posts, the hold setting; mapped to the `community` module in access.ts and mounted before `/manage`, whose staff-only check covers the rest). Everything a guide does is by rank (`COMMUNITY_RANKS` member, guide, staff, admin): muting (`canMute`), hiding a post or reply and taking down a Community channel line (`canModerate`, the same ladder) touch only someone of a lower rank, so a guide never acts on staff or an admin; undoing a hide, a pin, a mute or the hold setting needs at least the rank that did it (`canUndo`); a refusal reads the same whatever the writer's rank, so an anonymous writer's rank is never told. A Bond channel stays its owner's.
- Water and reminders (migration 0039, `routes/reminders.ts`, `components/Reminders.tsx`): Home, Daily and Journal carry water intake (a goal, a glass size, taps that add a glass or any amount, undo, a seven-day chart) and the person's own reminders (a line, a time, the days; up to `MAX_CUSTOM_REMINDERS`). On a laptop (from `lg`) it is a small bubble at the left of Daily and Journal (Home shows the same section inline on every width, and a tablet has it inline on Daily and Journal like a phone, so Daily's and Journal's columns keep the bubble's lane, `WATER_LANE` in `lib/ui.ts`, a full-width button in a block of its own so the lane narrows it; 2026-11-07, Journal 2026-11-08) that opens into a panel (`ReminderBubble`, mounted by `Shell`); water is one dark card in the look of Breathe and focus (2026-11-06, the founder; `WaterBlock` in `components/Reminders.tsx`, `.night-card .mf-panel .water-night`, which pins the dark theme's colours on itself so its fields read the same in both themes): three round icon buttons at the top right of the card, centred on a phone, and in the rail outside the card in a popup (Water, Reminders, Settings, in that order; `.water-tab` with the `ExpandButton` look, the name sliding out on hover or keyboard focus, the open one filled in the water green, the card's keyboard ring green too) switch the card between its panels in place, and a sideways swipe on a touch screen does the same; nothing opens over the page. Only the open panel is shown (the others are hidden and keep what was typed), so the card is its height and nothing is clipped; it eases in from the side it came from (GSAP, set under reduced motion); a narrow card takes a smaller glass. Water: the glass that fills (`fx/WaterGlass.tsx`: an SVG tumbler whose water glides to its level and sloshes once on every change, a soft wave only while on screen, still under reduced motion; `--water-glass-*` tokens) with today's amount under it and an amount picker (the arrows step from 100 ml to 1 L with the person's glass among them, the middle button adds the amount shown, focused when the bubble opens, Undo beside it), and the last seven days as small glasses filled against the goal (`.water-days`: the amount above, the day under, a tick when met, today ringed; then goal met on N of 7 days and the daily average); where there is room the glass is on the left and the week on the right (`.water-grid`), and under 460px of card one centred column, the glass, its buttons, then the week. Reminders: the person's own reminders and the tasks to remember (a new reminder's day pills read two letters until the card, not the screen, is 460px wide, then three). Settings: two `ChipSelect` pickers (goal, glass) side by side and one line saying what they make ("That is 8 glasses of 250 ml a day."); while a picker's list is open the short card grows to hold it (`.water-pane:has(.mf-pop)`), so the bubble never cuts it and the card below never covers it. The heading sits above the card. In a popup (the bubble and the card table's sheet, `railIn` on `WaterBlock`, 2026-11-07) Close and the round buttons leave the card for the rail outside the panel, a column on the card's own slate, so they stay in reach as a long panel scrolls, and the panel never shrinks as its panes change, so the rail stays under the pointer; there no word slides out over the panel, each is named by a smart tip off it (see Icons and the HIG rules); elsewhere they stay in the card and their words slide out as before. The bubble and the card table's sheet show the whole block; the section on Home, Daily and Journal keeps its row as it was, its + Glass the water green (Journal keeps the outline, its New entry being the solid button), (the founder: Home's water section is not to change) and opens the card without the heading and the count, its amount button the same water green (the founder, 2026-11-06: green in Home's water too). A round button's name is a smart tip beside it; on a phone there is no floating button, only the "Water and reminders" section inside the screen (`RemindersSection`) with the glass tap in its collapsed row (`quiet` makes "+ Glass" outline, on Journal, where New entry is the solid button). The cron (`sendCustomReminders` in cron.ts) sends a custom reminder once on each of its days within an hour after its time, in the person's zone. Water sends no note at all (`tidyOld` clears any the Worker before it writes back around a deploy; 2026-11-07, the founder: "remove notifications for water from inbox"; migration 0112 deleted the stored ones), and nothing else reminds anyone to drink: `reminder_prefs` keeps `water_on`, `water_every_min`, `water_from`, `water_to` and `last_water_at` as columns nothing reads or writes, `PUT /reminders/water/settings` takes `goalMl` and `glassMl` and ignores what an old tab still sends, the answer still carries that old tab's four fields as fixed words (`OPEN_OLD_TAB` in routes/reminders.ts, to go with the next deploy), and `runScheduled` does nothing for any trigger but the fifteen-minute one or a run by hand (`* * * * *`), since the old two-hour one can fire once around a deploy. The panel's "Your reminders" side also lists the person's tasks tagged Remember (`GET /reminders` `tasks`) with a tick that switches the tag off, and "Remind me about a task" tags any open task from Daily with a time (and a day when it has none), through the ordinary `PATCH /nodes/:id`, so the inbox note and the Daily tag are one thing; Daily's own section leaves that list out (`RemindersSection tasks={false}`), since Daily shows them under To remember, and puts one 12px link to `#remember-heading` in its place only while an undated Remember task exists. The days of a new reminder sit on one line (two letters on a phone, three from `sm` up). Owner-only, in the export, gone with the account; the day comes from the device and only today or yesterday counts. Water pays no xp.
- Getting started on Home is one small toggle (`aria-expanded`): folding it is reversible (`PUT /home/getting-started {folded}`), and it still goes by itself after `GETTING_STARTED_DAYS`.
- Anonymous and threaded conversations (migration 0038): a Community post, a reply and a line in an event conversation can be written anonymously (`anonymous`, `AnonToggle`): the reader gets a made-up name from `anonAlias` (`anonAliasFrom`: a word, an animal and two digits, hashed from the writer and the conversation, so it is the same all through one thread and different in the next), and never the id, handle, follower count, Pro mark or rank; staff keep the id so a report can be acted on, and the writer's own `mine` flag lets them delete theirs. Every post and reply leaves the server through `presentPosts` and `presentReplies`; an anonymous post never shows under Following, on a public page, in search by name, or in a notice by real name, and is blocked by post or reply id (`/community/posts/:id/block-author`) so nobody learns who it is; a block by name leaves anonymous writing alone (see `hiddenByBlock`), and a held post tells its mentions under its made-up name. Replies thread (`parent_id`, `depth`, `MAX_REPLY_DEPTH` 4, `threadOrder`): an answer sits under what it answers and past the deepest level lands beside it. Event connections need the other person's yes like any friend request, and the request says which event it came from.
- Usernames and friend codes (migration 0036): every account has a unique `handle` (username, `user_xxxxxx` until changed, never blank, `isAutoHandle`) and a unique 8-character `friend_code`. A code sends a friend request in Bond (`POST /bond/friends` with `code`; by code a block either way answers exactly as a code nobody has, 404) and starts a coach link (the coach's box takes an email, @username or friend code). **My QR code** (2026-11-07, `components/FriendQr.tsx`, `lib/friendAdd.ts`): on Profile (`/you#qr`, open), in Bond (a `Fold`, `#bond-qr`) and in an event's Meet other guests (`EventPanel`, a `Fold`, no new code there), the link `/u/<handle>?add=<code>` drawn on the device by `qrcode` as a data: picture (no new origin; the CSP allows data: images), dark on a white tile in both themes; the card takes its column's full width at every size (2026-11-13, the founder, after a reference of a QR panel: a quiet `--fill-2` panel with a hairline, the white tile raised in its middle, 240px and 288px from `lg`, then the @username and one line on what a scan does, then the friend code as one box a character, split 4, a dash, 4, smaller under `sm` so all eight fit a 360px phone, read out once through an `sr-only` copy), with Copy link, Share where `navigator.share` exists and Make a new code (`POST /me/friend-code`, core, limit `prefs`, asked in `ConfirmDelete`; the old code and every QR from it stop at once). The page (`GET /public/:handle?add=`) answers `add` only to someone signed in: `self`, `ok` (the code is this person's, no block either way, and requests on or a request already between them; a page switched off still takes the code, as Bond does) or `nobody` for everything else, in one line ("This code does not work."), so nothing leaks; `QrAddCard` in `screens/Public.tsx` then offers one Add friend through the ordinary `POST /bond/friends` with the code (limit `intro`), and the cover drops its own. Signed out it offers Sign in (with `next`) and Join, keeps the link for the tab only (`kreators.friendAdd` in sessionStorage) and `Shell` brings them back to it, as the coach's join link does.
- The apps shown under each relationship (`featuresForRelationship`, from each feature's `relationships` in site/content.ts) are chosen by what an app does for that relationship, not by habit: Parent & child, for example, gets Party, Community, Life, Mood, Sleep, Stress, Fitness, Journal, Reviews, Companion and Finance; With the divine gets Party, Community, Circles, Life, Mindfulness, Journal, Reviews, Companion, Coaching and Knowledge. Help is never listed, and Fund and TV sit under Servitude. A new app is added to the relationships it serves in the same place.
- Journal prompts (`packages/shared/src/journalPrompts.ts`, `components/JournalPrompt.tsx`, "Prompt me" on Journal): 16 categories (`JOURNAL_PROMPT_CATEGORIES`: shadow work, inner child, triggers, self-talk, self-worth, forgiveness, gratitude and joy, energy and boundaries, purpose, manifestation scripting, 3-6-9 writing, love, money, body and rest, future self, weekly reset) and about 340 prompts (`JOURNAL_PROMPTS`), each `cat-NN` with `text`, `why`, `expect`, `minutes` and optional `steps`; the wording is ours (ideas and methods follow the shadow work, light work, manifestation and 3-6-9 traditions in Keila Shaheen's journals, never their text; the credit line is `JOURNAL_PROMPT_CREDIT`). A `heavy` category ends every `expect` with `JOURNAL_CARE_NOTE` (stop and talk to a professional or someone you trust), a `plan` category with `JOURNAL_PLAN_NOTE` (a planning tool, not a guarantee); no cures, diagnoses or promised results. `nextPrompt(history, category, seed)` is the one rule (a seeded round like `lineForDay`: nothing repeats until the pool is used up, a new round never opens on the last prompt, same input same prompt). Private like the journal: the history, category, seed and the prompt on screen are in localStorage only (`kreators.journalPrompts`), never a route, a table, analytics or the assistant. "Write about this" opens the entry sheet with `promptTitle` as the title and `promptBody` ("Prompt: ...") as the first line. A new prompt goes in its category's list and nowhere else; the shared test checks counts, fields, wording and the no-repeat round.
- Gratitude for the day (2026-11-07, the founder: "gratitude for the day with prompts is missing for journal", then "can the pillars for gratitude be changed? and the prompts never be repeated?"; migration 0102 `gratitude_days` and 0106 `gratitude_offers`, `gratitude_seen`, `gratitude_prefs`, `GratitudeCard` and `GratitudeLines` in `components/GratitudeCard.tsx`, the routes under `/journal/gratitude` in `routes/journal.ts`, the leaf `packages/shared/src/gratitude.ts`, its own export `@kreators/shared/gratitude` and chunk `gratitude`): on Journal, under New entry, "Gratitude for today" is three short lines, each under its own prompt shown as the field's label, from one of the seven pillars or the day itself (`GRATITUDE_GROUPS`, `GRATITUDE_GROUP_NAMES`, 30 prompts in each, 240 in all, the first nine of each word for word from the first day, keys `<group>-NN` never reused; our own words from the plain idea of gratitude practice, never a quote). The prompts are per person: `gratitudeOfferFor` is the one rule (the day's three groups are the person's usual three when pinned, else `gratitudeGroupsForDay`, a seeded eight-day turn that gives three different groups a day and each group three days in eight; in each group the next prompt of `gratitudeRoundOrder` they have not seen, read from their marks by `gratitudeGroupState`), worked out on the day's first open and kept in `gratitude_offers` (`GET /journal/gratitude?day=&today=` answers `prompts` and `usual` for today or yesterday), so a reload, another tab or another device shows the same three and yesterday keeps yesterday's; two open days never share a prompt. Seen is written under (a line's first words, marked by the PUT) or skipped, never only shown, so an unwritten prompt can come back; a group's round ends when every prompt is seen and the next never opens on the one it closed with; marks are never taken back (`/privacy`'s retention row says a mark stays until the account goes). On an empty line the pillar's name opens an inline row of the eight as chips (`chip`, two columns on a phone, four from `sm`; `aria-expanded`, Escape closes and focus returns) and the round arrows ("Another prompt") mark the prompt seen and give the next unseen of the same group (`POST /journal/gratitude/:day/another`, `gratitudeSkip`); picking (`POST …/pick`, `gratitudePick`) takes the group's next unseen, swaps with another empty line that has it, refuses a written one, and marks nothing; both are guarded on the exact prompts read and on the line having no words, so two taps or two tabs change it once (409 with the day's prompts otherwise). "Make these my usual three" at the card's foot pins the day's groups (`PUT /journal/gratitude-settings`, `parseGratitudeUsual`, three different groups or null) for each day opened after; "Stop my usual three" clears it. A PUT puts an empty line under its place's prompt and answers 409 for words under any other (and is guarded on the kept prompts); the assistant's `write_gratitude` writes under the same three. It saves as the person types (`useAutosave`, one `PUT /journal/gratitude/:day` 800 ms after the last change, `ifUpdatedAt` so another tab's newer save answers 409 and the card reloads, the write itself guarded on that version (`UPDATE … WHERE updated_at = ?`, an insert that does nothing when the day exists) so two saves at once never both pass, and the assistant's Undo carries the version it wrote, so a day changed on Journal since is never emptied; no Save button; limit `gratitude`), one row per person per day (`PRIMARY KEY (user_id, day)`, the person's own day), only for today or yesterday (the device sends `today`, checked by `dateNearToday`), and all three lines empty removes the day; `parseGratitudeLines` is the one reader (a real prompt key each, none twice, one line of plain text up to `GRATITUDE_LINE_MAX`). Any past day can be read: the calendar marks a day with gratitude with a small dot under it (`CalendarGrid` `dots`, `GET /journal/days` `gratitude`), the day's panel lists its lines and, for today or yesterday, opens the card on that day; an older day shows read only. Journal data: owner only, never brands, staff, the leaderboard or Club, and a coach only as a whole table its owner switches on for that coach (All tables); the assistant reads it only through `read_journal` (sensitive, a model that keeps nothing, only when asked about the journal) and, with the Companion, through `read_table` while the person has switched the Gratitude table on with Show to AI chat (2026-11-14), on such a model too; in the export (`gratitude`, `gratitudeOffers`, `gratitudeSeen`, `gratitudePrefs`), gone with the account, and kept through an erase of progress like a journal entry, the seen marks with it. Only a count leaves it: `PeriodNumbers.gratitudeDays` (days with lines in a period) for Reviews and the Journal's By period tile.
- Calendars (Mood and Journal, `CalendarGrid`): every day is a button, with previous and next month buttons; tapping a past or present day opens a sheet to add, change or remove that day's mood (`POST /wellbeing/mood` with a `date`, one per day) or journal entries (`POST /journal` with a `date`, `GET /journal?date=`, `GET /journal/days?month=`), and a future day is refused by the server. A journal entry is filed on the person's own day in their zone (`users.timezone`, `dayBounds` in routes/journal.ts; one added from a day is stamped noon there), never the UTC date, so it sits on the same day as that day's gratitude.
- Admin, People: the list ends in a delete icon (`DELETE /admin/users/:id`, the same erase as the person's own delete, in `apps/api/src/account.ts`; an admin account and your own are never deleted from there; audited as `user.delete`). A deleted account never shows as a member anywhere: `DELETED_NAME` and `liveUser(alias)` in db.ts are the one condition, and nobody alive may take that name (`isDeletedName`, on sign-up, setup and `PATCH /me`), and Admin People and its search, Manage members and counts, Explore's member count, Home's joined line, search's people and event networking all leave the "Deleted member" rows out (the rows stay for the books), and so do Bond's friends and requests, Club's review queue, member count and staff picker, and a Party event's seats (`seatsTaken`), waitlist and the host's guest list (paged with a `Pager`, prefix `g`; 100 a page unless a size is in the address, for checking guests in at the door), and a reward's All entries and Most watched board in Win. `deleteAccount` removes the account's friendships and Club membership and cancels its seats at events still to come; a seat at one that is over stays for the host's records. The delete asks first in a `Sheet` with a red yes, never `window.confirm`, and Admin's tab lives in the URL (`?tab=`). "What does this person see?" lists every app as a tick row: ticking or unticking writes a person-level flag override (`reason` "Set from People", one per app, replaced each time), a struck-through app is not allowed for them, "Reset" removes the override, and locked apps cannot be blocked (the server refuses it too). **Connect people** (2026-11-09, the founder: "can u add ability to connect people in admin center?"; tab `connect` right after People, the lazy `components/AdminConnect.tsx` with `lib/connectApi.ts`, `routes/adminConnect.ts` at `/admin/connect` behind Admin's own door, admins and the key, and since 2026-11-10 (the founder: "yes to all") also at `/manage/connect` behind Manage's staff-only check, `screens/ManageConnect.tsx` drawn by Manage's chunk (route `/manage/:page?`, so the first download gained nothing) with a Connect people tile; kinds and words in the leaf `@kreators/shared/connect`, chunk `shared-connect`; migrations 0124 `team_connections` and 0127, which rebuilt it for two more kinds) is the one place the team connects people: pick a kind, find each person by email, @username, friend code or id (`GET /admin/connect/person`: a picture, a name and a handle, never an email, phone or birth date), the place a kind needs, how, and one solid Send. Each kind runs the app's own action as its second caller: a coach and a student (`teamCoachLink`, ask, or Link now), an Agency team member or client member (`inviteToTeam`, only while the owner's Agency is on, `MAX_TEAM` and `MAX_TEAM_INVITES` held, the owner told and never a friend request in their name; every tab starts Off whatever is sent (`byTeam` on `inviteToTeam`), so what the member sees is the owner's own choice, and the member's note says the team asked), a private program or Shop course (`inviteToProgram`, `inviteToCourse`: the note names the price, nothing is bought), a Club manager (`nameClubManager`, direct), a cohort's manager (`setCohortManager`, direct with a reason, pays nothing: a job given, not a connection), a brand team member (`inviteToOrg`, ask only), and friends, a suggestion both answer on their Profile tab (`/you#suggested`, `components/TeamSuggestions.tsx`, `/me/suggestions`, core, limit `intro`): the first yes sends an ordinary request through `requestFriend` (never to someone who has switched friend requests off since; their own yes sends it the other way), the second accepts it, a no closes it for both and takes back that request, and nobody is told who said no. A direct link needs a reason, kept only in the audit row (`connect.<kind>`, and `.end`, `connect.friends.answer`), whose words `deleteAccount` clears with either person. Two sends at once make one (a coach link's second insert answers 409, never a 500). Nobody, a deleted member, a blocked pair and someone who takes no friend requests all read "We could not find that person."; self is refused; the same pair twice answers 409. "Connections the team made" is a paged `DataTable` (state read from each kind's own row: asked, connected, said no, ended) with End or Withdraw asked in `ConfirmDelete`; limit `connect`, per person for a session at either door (`when: 'session'` reads it before /admin's door) and per address for the key. **Staff**: `CONNECT_KIND_PERMISSION` and `connectKindsFor` in connect.ts are the one map, checked on every call (`kindsOf` in adminConnect.ts): an admin and the key make every kind; a staff member (role `sub_admin`, never a creator, so a guide's Moderation opens nothing) makes, lists and ends only theirs, Cohorts a program, a course and a cohort's manager, Reviewer a Club manager, Talent manager a brand's team, Moderator friends; a coach link and an Agency team stay admins only (no staff permission governs either: the team's coach routes were always admin only and a link shares a student's reflection, and Talent runs Army, not anyone's Agency). The list answers `kinds`, the form and the list offer only those, another kind's End reads 404, a place list of another kind 403, and the People sheet is Admin's only. Each place keeps its own rule there (2026-11-10, the founder's "fix all these properly"; one predicate per place, asked by the place's own route and by the place lists, POST and End): a private program is its maker's and an admin's (`mayManageProgram` in school.ts), a private course its owner's and an admin's (`mayEditProduct` in studio.ts), so Cohorts staff reach only their own, refused in the place's own words (403, `PROGRAM_INVITER_ONLY`, `COURSE_INVITER_ONLY`; the course route now answers 403 to someone who can see it, 404 to anyone else), and their empty list says why (`mine` on the places answer); a cohort is listed where `mayNameManager` lets the caller name its manager, with `managerId`, which the form sends back as `from` (409 reads the list again), and `label`, its words without who runs it, for the line after a send; End reads the list again too. A staff member's list holds only rows on places they may reach (`cohortManagerSql` and the program's and course's maker in SQL), and End of any other reads 404. Undoing goes by rank, the rule a guide's undo follows, one rule for every door (`apps/api/src/connectRank.ts`; the break-it check of wave 27): an admin and the key end, take back or replace everything; staff only what staff made, by how its maker stood when they made it (`team_connections.made_as`, migration 0131, never their role now), so a row an admin or the key made shows `CONNECT_ADMIN_ROW` where End would be and DELETE answers 403 `CONNECT_ADMIN_ONLY`, and the same holds on the place's own route (`adminHolds`: Army's Withdraw by talent staff, the Club's manager route by a reviewer, the cohort page and Connect's replace by Cohorts staff, `ADMIN_NAMED_MANAGER`); a place's own maker on their own place keeps their route's power (a brand's owner, a program's maker, a course's owner), in Connect too. Naming a cohort's or a Club member's manager ends every row before it on that place in the same batch (`endManagerRows`), a brand's ask answered no ends its row with the answer, and End of a cohort or Club row is guarded on the one it named (`from`), so one row is live per real connection and an older one never ends a later one. The maker's branch of `mayNameManager` asks Cohorts too, so a maker taken off staff names nobody and looks nobody up; the Admin guide is refused to everyone, an admin and the key too, in its own words, and is in no list. A lookup by email is `email = ?` with the words lowered (`personBy`, Admin's coach link): every write was always lower case and migration 0129 made sure, so the unique index serves it (a Connect send by email read 4,160 rows on 8788, now 10). Each People row also has a Tables action for a super admin with a real session (never the key), opening that member's tables read only at `/admin/people/:id/tables`, never the journal, gratitude or a Body and mind table, each read in the audit log as `tables.view` or `tables.csv` (see All tables). Each People row has a Connections button (`GET /admin/connect/people/:id`: coach links either side, Agency teams, cohorts and invites, the Club manager, open suggestions, and a friend count, never the list) whose "Connect to someone" opens the tab with them filled in (`?with=`) and the keyboard in the next field. The form never sends a person its field no longer names (a lookup counts only for the words it answered, so a slow one or an emptied field leaves Send off), a double tap sends once (the cohort page's change, Army's Ask and Profile's Yes too, each held by a ref), and the keyboard moves on after a send (the emptied field; a kind with one person empties that one), an error (Send) and an End (the list's heading); the list's kind reads short so End stays inside the card at 1024. Life coaches says where linking lives right under its lede. Run clubs, Party guests and Army campaigns are not offered: none has an invite the person answers. **A brand's team is joined by a yes** (2026-11-10, migration 0127): the owner's "Ask a teammate by email" in Army (or talent staff, or Connect people; `inviteToOrg` in `routes/orgs.ts`, limit `intro`, a block with an owner reads as nobody) writes `organization_members` with status `invited`, which no reader counts (every member read asks for `active`; `GET /orgs/:id/members` lists the asks apart as `invited` for the owner and talent staff, each with Withdraw asked in `ConfirmDelete`); the person answers on their Profile tab (`/you#org-invites`, `components/OrgInvites.tsx` in the suggestions' look, `/me/org-invites`, core, limit `intro`): yes makes them a member and tells the owners, no takes the ask away and tells nobody; a block by name either way with an owner takes the ask away at once, quietly, from the owner's Asked list and the person's Profile, its note too (`blockAsksStmts` in `apps/api/src/blockAsks.ts`, the one list, run by `POST /bond/block` and by the Block on a named post, reply, channel, run or cohort line, never behind a made-up name, which also touches no friendship: it ends a team friend suggestion between the two and both its notes, an Agency team invite between owner and member, whose yes across a block then reads as no invite, and a private program's or course's invite between its maker and the person not yet taken up, each with its Connect row; nobody is told; the break-it check of wave 27); everyone on a team before stayed a member. **A cohort's manager** (2026-11-10): `PATCH /school/cohorts/:id/manager {userId}` and Connect people's Cohort manager run one `setCohortManager` in school.ts: an admin on any program, staff with Cohorts on a public one, and the program's own maker on theirs in any state, who finds the person through the cohort's own lookup (`GET /school/cohorts/:id/manager/person`, limit `coachLookup`) and is named in the notes (`mayNameManager`, the cohort answer's `canSetManager`), never the Admin guide's; the one named is staff, an admin or a creator with an email and setup done, a deleted member or a block with the program's maker reads as nobody, guarded on the manager it read and, from the cohort page, on the one the page showed (`from`, else 409), the new and the old told (`/school/:id`), audited `cohort.manager`; the cohort page's "Who runs it" (`components/CohortManager.tsx`, lazy) has Change the manager. The People sheet names a program or course by its real state (`label` on `PersonConnection`: a cohort of the public Onboarding program reads "Public School program. Enrolled in a cohort", never an invite; 2026-11-10). A connection pays each side who is not an admin `CONNECT_REWARD` when it comes true, never whoever made it (a friend suggestion naming the caller is refused, `CONNECT_NOT_YOURSELF`), never the side given a job (a Club manager link pays the member only, a cohort's manager nobody) and nobody when its maker made or owns its place (their private program, course or brand: the place's own invite never paid, so making places pays no friend), once per kind, pair and place ever, so one ended and made again pays nothing more, an erase of progress included (`CONNECT_PAID_BEFORE` reads the rows that came true, which an erase keeps) (`apps/api/src/connectPay.ts`: `CONNECT_PAYS`, and `CONNECT_PAY_KEY` the claim's key, which migration 0129 put on the older claims; 2026-11-10) (`payConnection` in `apps/api/src/invites.ts`, claimed by `team_connections.came_true_at`, migration 0125: both yeses on a friend suggestion or a friendship made any other way, a coach link going active, an Agency invite accepted, a cohort joined or a course bought from the team's invite, a direct link at once), friends to the friendship pillar, a coach and School to teachers, Agency and the Club to servitude; an ask pays nothing and nothing comes back when it ends. In the export (`teamConnections`), gone with either person.
- Joining by invite (2026-11-10, the founder: "dont allow people to join the website without waitlist approval by admins or a secret invite code ... add this in admin"; migration 0125 `invite_codes`, `waitlist`, `signup_settings`; the leaf `@kreators/shared/invites`, chunk `shared-invites`; `apps/api/src/invites.ts` and `routes/invites.ts`): while Invite only is on (`signup_settings.invite_only`, on from the migration, Admin, Invites' switch, `PUT /admin/invites/settings`, audited `invite.gate`) a new account needs a code (see Auth). A code is ten characters with no look-alikes (`normalizeInviteCode`), used once by one account (the guarded claim; a partial unique index on `used_by`), shared as `PUBLIC_URL` + `/join/<code>` or the code itself, with a private label and 7, 30 (default) or 90 days or never. Made by an admin, staff or anyone holding Inviter (`invite` in `PERMISSIONS`, a creator can hold it; `canInvite` in shared the one rule, db.ts wraps it): `/me/invites` (core, prefix `iv`, ten a page, `MAX_OPEN_INVITES` 50 unused for anyone not an admin, counted inside the insert; DELETE takes an unused one back, 409 once used; limit `inviteMake`) and `/admin/invites` (admins and the key: every code with who made and who used it, filters, make, take back, the waitlist; audited `invite.make`, `invite.revoke`, `waitlist.approve`, `.decline`, `.delete`). The public side: `GET /public/join`, `GET /public/invite/:code` (`{ ok }`, one answer for unknown, used, expired and taken back, limit `inviteCheck`) and `POST /public/waitlist` (the same answer for every email, a new row gets one private mail, the admins a count in the Team inbox once an hour, limit `waitlist`); approving makes one code tied to the row and mails its link privately (`emailed` says whether it went; twice answers the same code), joining with it marks the row joined. The account a code makes is credited to the maker (`users.referred_by` = their referral code, given them if they had none; an invite beats a ref); the maker hears it once (`invite-<code id>`, renewed at setup and again when it pays), named by the label, never the person. The referral pays a maker who is not an admin `INVITE_REWARD` (50 xp, 20 points, five a day, friendship pillar) once, when the person has used the app on `INVITE_ACTIVE_DAYS` (3, beside `INVITE_REWARD` in community.ts) different days of their own, each a Daily check-in or a task's tick by when it happened (the server's clock, at their zone's offset now), never the day the device named, so three check-ins in one sitting are one day (the break-it check), on a real account with setup done (2026-11-10: emails are not checked and plus-addressing beats a check, so the gate is use, not mail; `inviteActiveStmts` in invites.ts, a guarded UPDATE of `invite_codes.active_at`, migration 0129, the maker's pay claimed in the same transaction while they earn and may still make codes, and a read for the note, ride in the batch the check-in and the tick already send, so they cost no round trip and read nothing for anyone without an unpaid code; `afterInviteActive` moves the totals and renews the note, one round trip once ever). `payInviteOnSetup` (both setup saves, and the sign-up that adds an email to a device account) claims `setup_done_at` and tells the maker when it will pay, and pays then for someone already past three days; never at the bare sign-up or for setup alone, never for a device account alone and never twice after an erase. A deleted maker's unused codes are taken back and labels cleared; their waitlist row goes; codes appear in the export without the code (`has_link`), and the person who joined with one sees its row without the maker's label. The safety review (2026-11-10): a code stops working once its maker can no longer make codes (Inviter or the staff role taken away, `MAKER_CAN_SQL` in the claim and in `inviteWorks`, and `revokeIfCannotInvite` takes the unused ones back in the same batch as the change in both role routes and the permissions route), except a waitlist yes and a code the key made; `/auth/signup` looks at the code (`inviteWorks`, nothing used) before "that email already has an account" and before hashing, so without a working code it tells nobody who has an account; the waitlist's mail never repeats the typed name and goes out after the answer (`waitUntil`, with the admins' count), so the time says nothing, and removing a row also takes its two mails (`WAITLIST_MAIL_SUBJECT`, `INVITE_MAIL_SUBJECT`) out of the outbox. `RETENTION` and `tidyOld`: a declined row 180 days after the no, a waiting one after a year, an invited or joined one 30 days after its code was used, ran out or was taken back. The codes and the switch never reach the log (`cleanPath` names `/public/invite/<code>` and `/join/<code>` as `:token`) and their answers are `no-store`. Test scripts turn it off on 8788 only (`scripts/smoke/openJoining.mjs`, the ordinary admin call; the API has no test bypass). The screens (`lib/invite.ts` keeps the code for the tab in sessionStorage `kreators.invite`, cleared by the account it makes or a sign-in; `lib/inviteApi.ts`, `components/InviteGate.tsx`, `InviteParts.tsx`, `InvitePeople.tsx`, `AdminInvites.tsx` and `screens/JoinInvite.tsx` are lazy, behind one `InviteDoor` reference in the first download): Welcome reads `publicApi.join` and, with no session and no kept code, shows the gate instead of Start (the code with Continue, the screen's one solid button, checked by `GET /public/invite/:code` before `/auth/anon`; Join the waitlist a `Fold` with `id="waitlist"`, so `/start#waitlist` opens it); `/join/<code>` keeps a code that works and goes on to Welcome ("Your invite code is ready."), and one that does not shows the heading "This invite does not work" over `INVITE_UNUSABLE_WHY` (the rest of `INVITE_UNUSABLE`, said once; 2026-11-10) with the gate and the waitlist open, the waitlist's privacy caption a plain 12px line whose link's 44px hit area is a `::before`; the sign-up page shows the code field in place after a 403 `reason: invite` (what was typed stays), an event's page shows it up front while Invite only is on and Join waits for it, and the setup chat's account card never needs one (it upgrades the device session); a kept code the server refuses as unusable is forgotten (Welcome, the sign-up page and the event page), the gate's Continue holds a second tap in the same moment with a ref (one check, one `/auth/anon`), and the waitlist form says a bad email in its own line (`noValidate`, so the browser's bubble never stands in for it) (the break-it check, 2026-11-10). Admin, Invites (`?tab=invites`, after Connect people): the switch (off asks in `ConfirmDelete`, "Yes, let anyone join"), the waitlist (`DataTable`; Approve outline, showing the code in place with Copy link, Copy code and whether it was emailed; Decline a second tap; Remove asked in a sheet), then the codes (Make codes the tab's one solid button, a sheet; State a `FilterSelect`; "Only codes <maker> made" from a row's More; Copy link, Copy code, a QR code drawn on the device, Take it back asked in a sheet). Profile's Invite people (`#invites`, only for `canInvite`): `INVITE_CREDIT_LINE` with how many joined, Make a code a `Fold` with `adds`, the codes ten a page with Copy link and a More of Copy code, Show QR code and Take it back on a second tap; Manage has an Invites tile to it. `node scripts/smoke/drive-invites.mjs` (`DRIVE_WIDTH`, `DRIVE_THEME`) drives every way in on 8788 and puts the switch back as it found it.
- Home's header is always the night slate (`.night-card`), in both modes, so its flower and white text show in dark mode; the greeting wraps instead of truncating, so a name is never clipped. The assistant's button sits 96px above the bottom (and its panel 80px), clear of the 72px tab bar; Public page's sticky Save bar rests above it until the laptop column clears its corner (172px up on a phone, 88px from the tablet, 16px from `xl`), so it never covers Save profile.
- Not to do: at most `MAX_NOT_TO_DO` (7) items a person, titles up to `NOT_TO_DO_TITLE_MAX`; one row per item per day in `not_to_do_days` (kept or slipped; no row means missed). `notToDoStreak` in shared is the one streak rule: today counts once ticked, the run ends at yesterday until then, and a slip or a missed day ends it. A kept day pays `XP.notToDo` once per item per day and is never clawed back. Only today or yesterday can be marked (`dateNearToday`), and the request says which day is the person's today so the streak is counted as of that day. The section sits under Tasks on Daily and again on Pillars under the seven pillars: one block, `NotToDoBlock` in `components/NotToDoList.tsx`, reading Daily's own data so a tick in one place shows in the other. It is not one of the three boxes; the list is in the export and goes with erase and delete.
- Periods and reviews: `periodFor`, `periodShift`, `periodLabel` and `periodDays` in shared are the one period arithmetic (a day is one calendar day, weeks start on Monday, quarters, halves and the year follow the calendar, both ends inclusive, labels like "7 Nov 2026", "19 to 25 Oct 2026" and "2026"). `PERIOD_KINDS` is day, week, month, quarter, half and year (the year came 2026-11-07, migration 0101 `review_year`, and the day the same day, the founder's daily report, migration 0105 `review_day`, each rebuilding `reviews` and `review_templates` so their CHECK takes it; `review_schedules` has no CHECK); the dashboards offer `DASHBOARD_PERIODS`, the day to the half (the day first, the week still the default), and Reviews all six; Business metrics keep the week to the year (`KPI_KINDS` in kpis.ts, and Reviews hides them on a day). `numbersFor(db, userId, period, { ctx?, supplements? })` in reviews.ts is the one read of a period's numbers from every app's own table, for any kind the day included (`numbersContext` reads the person's zone, own today and module switches once; `periodWindow` turns the period into UTC stamps from the person's own midnight through `zonedToUtc`, and groups stamps by their own day); `GET /reviews/summary` (the dashboards) and `GET /reviews` (with the layer) put it next to the period before; journal and wellbeing numbers go only to their owner. Fitness and gratitude count there too (2026-11-07): `moveMin` (active minutes), `moveDays` (days with movement), `eatAvg` and `gratitudeDays`, counts only, every period the year included; Reviews' The numbers takes `keep` like Home (`keepForApps` in lib/modules.ts, the founder, 2026-11-07): a tile that opens an app shows only while that app is on, whatever the period holds, so a category left with nothing goes and someone with only Reviews on sees the core tiles (6 at first, down from 25); a review's saved words and snapshot never change; the `fitness` tiles (Active minutes, Days moved, Eating) follow Fitness, with the first two staying while Kreators Run is on and opening its tool, and the assistant's `get_review` passes the layer it walks (titles and counts). Fuller numbers (2026-11-07, optional on `PeriodNumbers`, each read only while its app is on): water (core: `waterDays`, `waterAvgMl`, `waterGoalDays` against the goal as set now), focus (Mindfulness: `focusMin`, `focusSessions`, `focusQualityAvg` of the rated sessions only), tasks (core: `habitTicks` by `taskIsHabit`, `topPriorityDone` P0 and P1, `overdueAtEnd` still open past their due day as the period ended, as of today while it runs, and `routineDays`, days with every morning step of the routine ticked), money (Finance, the person's own currency only: `budgetsOver` of `budgetChecks`, each budget against each month the period touches up to its end, `owedToYouCents` and `youOweCents` from dues open at its end, and the savings goals as they stand today, `goalSavedCents`, `goalTargetCents`, `goalsReached`, `goalsCount`, only for a period that holds today, since the app keeps no dated record of each amount put in) and supplements (`suppTaken` of `suppDue`, see Fitness). `SummaryTiles` (its own chunk, `components/SummaryTiles.tsx`, loaded after the first paint by `PeriodPanel`) draws them as parts `tasksMore`, `water`, `focus`, `moneyMore` and `supplements`, each only while the server sent it and the period or the one before holds something: Reviews shows them all, Home and Wellbeing water, Finance the money extras, Mindful focus.
