One review per person per period (`UNIQUE (user_id, kind, period_start)`), saved with the prompts and a snapshot of the numbers so a changed template or new data never rewrites it (saved again from the same template, a review keeps the questions it was written with, so an answer never moves under another question); a period that has not started is refused (after the person's own today, `personDay` with the device's `?day=`, so a daily review is never written for tomorrow). **Reviews by layer** (2026-11-07, the founder; `packages/shared/src/reviewPrompts.ts`, server-only text): each built-in review walks one layer of the person's own pyramids, pillar by pillar (`REVIEW_LAYER`): a day the tasks (ticked that day in the person's zone, open on it, and late ones marked `late`, each with the project it holds up), a week the projects, a month the goal outcomes, a quarter the value goals, half a year the guiding principles, a year the foundations. The half is two parts by which half it is (`halfPart`, `reviewNameFor`): January to June, opened in July, is the **Mid-year check-in**, and July to December, opened on 1 January beside the yearly review, is the **Start-of-year setting**. Picking Half year or Year on Reviews (`PeriodFilter` `pick`) and the assistant's "this" open one by `reviewPeriodNow`, the one whose end is nearer: the half is the setting from October to March and the check-in from April to September, the year the one just ended until June; so the setting and the yearly review say "the past year" and "the year ahead", never "this year" (a shared test checks it); Day opens today, and a daily review reminder set before noon opens the day before (`reviewPeriodFor(kind, day, time)`). `builtInTemplateFor(period)` gives the one built-in set per period (`REVIEW_PROMPT_SETS`, five for a day, six or seven for the rest, the first always the walk of the layer; id `builtin:<kind>` whatever the part; `builtInTemplates()` lists all seven, the half's two parts each, for `GET /reviews/templates`), each question with a `why` line naming its method in plain words (the founder's methods: CBT, ACT, trauma-informed care, family systems, improv, positive psychology, self-compassion, the Gita's karma yoga, the Stoics and the Buddha; "The app's own way" for the walk), our own words, every heavy question saying it can be skipped, Flesch 60 or above, never a promise, a diagnosis or therapy talk (a shared test checks all of it); How it works says a review is a look back, not therapy, with the care line, and lists the methods' names as `TERMS` (CBT, ACT and the rest); it cannot be edited or removed; a person's own templates (`review_templates`, up to `MAX_REVIEW_TEMPLATES`) recur with their cadence. `layerFor` in reviews.ts reads that layer's own items (owner only, as the period ended: those still going then (active now, or done since) and those done inside it, none made after it, grouped by the seven pillars in order, `REVIEW_LAYER_PER_PILLAR` each with the total), each with the tasks under it ticked in the period and still open as it ended and, for a week's project, its pace as the period ended (`projectPace`); `ReviewSummary.layer` carries it on `GET /reviews` and in a saved review's snapshot, and on `GET /reviews/summary` only with `layer=1`, so the dashboards never pay for it. The screen (2026-11-07) keeps the period and the step in the address (`kind`, `date`, `step`, so a reload, Back or a reminder's link keeps them; a kind with no day, such as Emerging's "Write this month's review", a day not on the calendar or a period still to come opens that kind as picking it would, and only an address with no kind opens the week; a server that answers another period says so in a line, never quietly), shows the layer as "Your projects, this week" (a day's "Your tasks, today": Done, Open or Late) above the questions, each item with an "Add to my answer" that adds its line under the first question and moves the review to it, and "Your weekly review" (the period's own name) steps through its questions one at a time (`components/ReviewSteps.tsx`: a row of numbered tabs, then Rate it and Save, a tick on each answered one, `aria-current="step"`, Back and Next, focus to the step's field; every answer kept while moving, one Save for all); Templates (`components/ReviewTemplates.tsx`) is a closed fold listing every period's built-in set with its why lines, read only, each with Make my own copy, then the person's own; daily reviews fold apart under Past reviews (`recentDays`, prefix `rd`), the calendar marks one with a dot and rings no bell for a daily reminder, and Emerging's "since your last review" looks back from the last longer one. `components/PeriodFilter.tsx` is the one period control (its periods span the full width in equal parts, 2026-11-06; `periodGrid`: five on the dashboards, one row from 30rem and three over two below; six in Reviews, one row from 36rem and three over three below) and `components/PeriodPanel.tsx` (the filter plus the lazy `SummaryTiles`) is the "By period" block every dashboard carries: Home, Life, Finance, Wellbeing, Mood, Sleep, Stress, Mindful, Journal and the Shop. Reviews live in the Reflection group, off by default.
- Text on a phone is light (wave 5, `docs/copy-brief.md` over CopyOS; CLAUDE.md wins, then the brief): one-line ledes, a card in one or two short sentences, the action before the explanation, sentences around fifteen words, headlines of twelve words or fewer, long explanations behind How it works or a `Fold`; a laptop may show more (`sm:`/`md:` reveals) but never a different meaning, and no claim the page needs is cut. `scripts/smoke/density-probe.js` (`SHOOT_PROBE`, phone 390) flags a paragraph over four lines with thirty words or more, a card over sixty words (a rounded select or input is not a card), more than ninety words before the first action and a page over 700 words outside reading blocks; reading blocks (`.prose-long`, legal documents, `data-reading` pages, chat logs, `pre`) are exempt, and any other hit that stays is a reading page said so.
- Text is a rectangle (the founder, 2026-11-05: "keep all text boxes look like rectangles, not long lines followed by short one, u can break lines at commas or periods, or can have the lines increase in length in the ones below each of them"): no line of a heading, card, list line, button or paragraph is noticeably shorter than the line above it, on a laptop or a phone, in either theme. The allowed shapes are lines about equal, lines that grow going down, and a line broken on purpose after a comma or a full stop; a single line is fine. `html` carries `text-wrap: balance` in app.css (inherited by everything, long reading text and `.type-context` too; `text-pretty` is not used on a screen any more), and headings, captions and fold-out titles balance on their own rule. A browser balances only up to about six lines, so `lib/rectText.ts` (started once by `Root` in App.tsx, downloaded after the first paint as its own small chunk) finds every block with a line under 80% of the widest line above it since the last break made on purpose (a forced break after a comma, full stop, semicolon, colon, question or exclamation mark or an ellipsis, `ENDS`, or a new line the writer typed; `worst()`; so a slow taper of steps just over 80% no longer passes) and tries, starting from the block's last shape when it measures again, in slices of 8 ms between idle moments (the block shows its best shape so far in between): a little more padding on the side the text runs out to (never past 6% of its width (the founder, 2026-11-06: "rectangles can be wider"; a shape that narrows the block by more is refused, and the full-width shapes are tried first; only when no shape within 6% makes a rectangle does it go on to the balanced wrap and up to 15% narrower, `FALLBACK_TRIM`, and last of all to the balanced wrap and the lines that grow at any width, since the rectangle comes before the width, all inside the same sliced search)) with balanced, plain or `pretty` wrapping; then the first line held shorter so the lines grow (`data-rect-first`, a float one line tall from the `::before` in app.css; centred text takes as much indent; never under half the line below it, `FIRST_FLOOR` 0.5, so no stub like "A curated / membership with…" is left); then both; and last one more line, never ending on one word. It keeps the box's outer size (a box sized to its words is held at its width), keeps the number of lines where it can, runs again on a resize, a theme change and a page change (debounced; a change made only inside `[data-rect-quiet]`, the ticking focus pill, its sidebar dot and the Mindful dial, never starts a pass), and leaves out printed paper (`.legal-doc`, `.legal-root`, `.legal-invoice`, `.print-doc`, `.month-print-sheet`, which follow the founder's PDF; the typed agreement keeps `useNoLoneWord`), anything editable, code and hidden text; a child that holds no words, an svg or picture, or anything out of the flow (a screen-reader-only label) never hides its block from the fixer or the probe, and words straight inside a flex or grid box get a span of their own, since the fixer cannot style that anonymous box (the agreement and document Accept labels, Party's introductions box). Where the words themselves cannot make a rectangle (a short line of facts in a narrow card), break them on purpose: parts kept whole with `inline-block` spans after a comma or full stop (Agency's "1 waiting," "1 late", a pitch's meta line, a circle's when and tables), one block per part (a guide's name over its role, an honor's Locked over its how), a layout that gives the words room (the honors' seal over the words on a phone, an invoice row's words at least 60% wide, the policy tables' fixed columns, a policy table of five or more columns kept as stacked cards at every width), or plainer copy; never a hard-coded width. A line clamp switches balancing off, so it is not used on text that can wrap. `scripts/smoke/rag-probe.js` is the check (`SHOOT_PROBE`): it measures each line from its words' rects and reports a line under 80% of the widest line above it since the last break made on purpose, unless the line above ended on purpose (a `<br>` or the end of an inline-block after punctuation, or a new line the writer typed); run it over the four shot lists (360, 390, 768, 1280) after touching text or layout (the final gate of 2026-11-05, after the type pass made card titles larger: 6 or 7 at 390 a theme, 4 at 1280, about 20 at 360; what stays is the smoke test's own names, policy paragraphs and table cells on /privacy a hair under 80%, a 96px chart label, Club's blurb around "members-only", Party's introductions line and the badge "Ten projects cleared" at 360, Bumling's handwritten line on the home at 360, and notification titles in /inbox; none is a page's own heading). At 768 (first counted 2026-11-07, over 127 routes: 102 rag and orphan hits in light and 94 in dark, then 16 and 12) `md` brings the sidebar, so a tablet's column is 464px, narrower than it looks: a grid of tiles, cards or halves that hold words starts its columns at `lg` and a tablet takes the phone's layout (Home's halves, badges and honors, plans, a period's tiles, Store's shelves, a client's tiles, business metrics, the Discover add-ons, the focus dashboard, the build map, Emerging's cards), and laptops stay as they were; what stays at 768 is /privacy's table cells, the books' stacked titles in a 122px strip on Home, the smoke test's own names, one title a hair under 80% on Knowledge, and an unfilled blank's word on the paper, which the orphan probe cannot see because CSS draws it. The fixer marks the page `data-rect-busy` until every block had its turn, and `shoot.mjs` waits for that before it shoots and probes. `scripts/smoke/orphan-probe.js` still checks for a lone last word (it groups words into lines the same way, so a code-like link or a count on the same line is not a line of its own): 0 hits on a laptop (a phone may keep a rare long word that fills over half its line).
- Only spacing values 8, 12, 16, 32, 64, 120 (tokens xs, sm, md, lg, xl, 2xl). Never hardcode hex colours in screens; use the theme tokens so dark mode works. The look is calm glass on a gradient (the 2026-10-30 redesign, researched from NN/g, Axess Lab and bento-grid guides): the page is never a flat fill but a soft wash (`body::before`: four glows, `--glow-1` to `--glow-4`, over `--page-base-a` to `--page-base-b`; periwinkle, seafoam, blush and lavender in light mode, deep indigo and teal in dark mode, darker since 2026-11-04: the base #07090f to #080e14, the glows at about half and `--card-strong` rgba(15, 18, 30, .985), each app's dark mode leaning the wash toward its own hue). `--paper` and `--card` are translucent glass; only the big surfaces blur (`.bg-card` with `rounded-card` or `rounded-tile`, nav, dialogs, `--glass-blur` 14px light and 16px dark, saturate 1.3), small chips do not. `--card-strong` (`bg-card-strong`) is the card that must stay readable whatever is under it: dialogs, nav, tooltips, buttons on a coloured fill; a `.bg-card` inside a coloured or ink fill turns solid by CSS. The glass turns solid with `prefers-reduced-transparency`, `prefers-contrast: more` and where `backdrop-filter` is missing. Ink is a cool slate (`#25293a`, soft `#444a5f`, mute `#565c72`, tuned for AA over the whole wash; `#eef0f8` in dark mode), not grey; the seven relationship colours are calm (`--color-r1` to `r7`, mirrored in `RELATIONSHIPS` in shared): red for Servitude, orange for friends, yellow for family, green for love, sky blue for yourself (`--color-r5`, `--tint-sky`), indigo for teachers (`--color-r6`, `--tint-indigo`) and deep purple for faith; the old brown is no pillar's colour now but stays as `--color-earth`, `--soft-earth` and `--deep-earth`, and the public footer is glass (`.site-footer`, blurred, with a faint dusk-to-ground tint: indigo and sky blue above, then green, then earth) with a four-colour ground band at its foot, so the site ends on something solid, and coloured fills are gradients (`Hero`, `MetricCard`, `.bg-r1`..`.bg-r7` and `.bg-ink` carry one; `--fill-shade` is where they deepen to), never flat. A solid surface (an ink or coloured card, the night slate, a strong card) also wears the matte finish (wave 15: `--matte-grain`, a tiled grey noise at about 4%, over `--matte-sheen` or its own gradient), gone under reduced transparency and more contrast and never on printed paper; controls keep their plain look at rest. Bento is `.bento` with `.bento-w2`, `.bento-w3`, `.bento-w4` and `.bento-h2` on a 4-column grid (one column on a phone), one gap (`--spacing-sm`), one radius, the most important cell the biggest (`.bento-feature` washes it); the public home uses it for the villains, the 5 W’s, the story steps, what changes and the offer, and Home's metrics. The contrast probe reads a gradient fill's first colour stop and flags any fixed or sticky chrome that carries text on a fill under 88% opaque. Corners are concentric the way Apple draws them (`--radius-card` 32px, `--radius-tile` 24px, `rounded-inner` 16px for a tile inside a card with 16px of padding, `rounded-inner-sm` 20px with 12px) and continuous (`corner-shape: squircle`) where the browser draws them. What sits behind a hero or coloured card is grown, not drawn with circles: `Posy` in `components/Hero.tsx` (`HeroBlobs` is its top-right placement) is one big flower drawn in its own 200 by 170 box and scaled to fit (`max-w-full`), so no petal is ever cut off, with its edges faded instead of ended. Fewer, bigger shapes, and a different flower in different places rather than one repeated: seven kinds (`FlowerKind`: blossom, daisy, lotus, poppy, tulip, cosmos, violet), each pillar wearing its own (`PILLAR_FLOWER`: a poppy for Servitude, a blossom for friends, a daisy for family, a tulip for love, a violet for yourself, a cosmos for teachers, a lotus for faith), other heroes and cards picking one from their title or colour (`flowerFor`, the same text always the same flower). White at very low opacity on a coloured fill, ink on the page. The water card is one full-width cell (`span-all`), with its tiles opening as a two-cell bento.
- Wording is universal and plain, readable by a teenager (Flesch 60 or above; `python3 -I scripts/flesch.py <file>` scores a file, `--strings` scores only the quoted text of a TypeScript file). The words are: seven *pillars* (never tower, floor or area for the seven; the five *areas of life* keep that word), each pillar is a *pyramid* with six *layers* (foundation, guiding principle, value goal, goal outcome, project, task; never rule, feeling, proof, rung or ladder; the internal kinds pillar, principle, value_goal and outcome stay as keys only), the daily *small act* (never spark), the *not to do* list on Daily (things to stay away from; never habits, vices or avoidances), *reviews* by *period* (a day, a week, a month, a quarter, a half year or a year; the half is the *mid-year check-in* in July and the *start-of-year setting* in January; never sprint, cycle or retro), the *Daily* tab (never Today as a screen name) and the *Profile* tab (never You; the public page module is *Public page*), *Servitude* for the servant_leader pillar (its guide Ember is "guide to servitude"; never work and money) and *Mindfulness* for the app (never Mindful minutes as its name; mindful minutes stays the measure). App groups are Contribution, Reflection, Connection, Creation, Essentials and Operations, always in that order (`MODULE_GROUP_ORDER`; Operations is team only and Dev Tools sits there). Route paths and keys keep their old names. Every action in the app is a checkbox row with the `toy` hover affordance, its box outside the card (2026-11-12, the founder: "why is the checkbox not seperate and out of the clickable task card itself?"): `CheckRow` is three things side by side, none inside another, the box at the left (its own 44px control, named "Tick <title>" and described by the note and the card's tags), the `toy` card holding the words and tags, then any `aside`; with `open` (a task's plan on Daily, its edit sheet on a pillar page) a tap on the card opens it and the box alone ticks, else the words tick too; the keyboard meets the box, then the card, then the aside; a pillar's row takes its box from `pillarTick` (`lib/relFill.ts`: the fill, the tick and the deep shadow from the tokens, the empty edge `--tick-edge-<key>`: the fill, the deep colour for orange and yellow, and in dark mode the deep colour for all seven, so each holds 3:1); a subtask and a reminder's row (`ReminderTick`) keep the box outside too.
- Screens open with `PageHeader` (or `BackLink` on coloured headers) and are built from `Section`, `StatTile` and the charts in `components/Charts.tsx`. A tile's figure never loses a digit to its edge (the 2026-11-07 gate: ₹1,00,00,000 read ₹1,00,00,00): `figureSize` in Charts.tsx steps a figure with no space in it down to `text-title2`, nine or ten characters under `lg` and eleven or more at every width, and one longer still breaks rather than clips. An app screen whose header has a helper line opens with a cover card (2026-11-04, Agency's is the reference; `PageHeader` draws `components/AppCover.tsx` when it has `back` and `lede`): one rounded glass card, the back button at the top left and any top actions level with it at the right, the title with its helper line under it at the bottom right, right-aligned, and the header's actions on the helper's line at its left (the main action first, the gear right beside it; on a phone they take the bottom left and the helper wraps above them), over the app's own art (`fx/CoverArt.tsx` inside `fx/LiquidRipple.tsx`) anchored right and faded so the words keep AA ("App covers" in app.css). Profile wears the cover too, left-aligned (`app-cover-start`), with no back button and its compact Sign out at the top right. The hero rule: an app with a number or state about the person opens with a plain header (`BackLink`, the `h1`, the lede) and a coloured `Hero` in its pillar colour holding that number (`HeroNumber`, a `Gauge` or a word like "Needs care"), its character whose mood follows it and a faint `Posy`, as on Life, Sleep, Journal, Stress, Wellbeing, Companion, Coaching and Games; an app with no such number (Agency, Store, Help, Admin, Plans, Inbox, Reviews (the founder, 2026-11-06; its period numbers sit under The numbers) and the rest) wears the cover; a screen never gets both. Headers with no helper line and the main tabs (Home, Daily, Pillars, Apps) stay plain. A screen's first plain header that opens with a `BackLink` (`data-back`) is a grid: the back button at the left of the first row, the title at the right of the same row, the lines under it right-aligned, on a phone and a laptop ("Screen headers" in app.css; `PageHeader`'s `aside` becomes a `[data-aside]` block at the end of the title's line from `md`); under 360px (`22.49em`, so a larger browser text size moves it) its `text-large-title` steps down to `text-title1`, so a one-word title ("Mindfulness" at 320) never runs past the edge, never cut or hyphenated, and 360 and up are unchanged (2026-11-09). Every admin list is a `components/DataTable.tsx`: sort by any column, search, filter by a column's values, tick rows for bulk actions, edit a cell in place (`edit` on the column says which call runs), and page through the server's rows (`page`, `onPage`); on a phone a filter's label keeps 76px so the dropdowns line up, and a cell's switch keeps a 44px hit area (`tap-pad`). Admin, Manage, Dev Tools and Logs take the app's passes (2026-11-09): a tab's heading with its button is `SectionHead` (`components/SectionHead.tsx`, Section's heading markup in its own file), a search is `FIELD_FILTER` with its one-value filters as `FilterSelect`s under it, a choice of a few views is `Segmented` (KYC, a discount's kind), errors are `ErrorLine`, a long sheet's action is `StickyAction` (a listing, a discount code), a repeated row action is outline, and the cover's Admin guide is outline so a tab's own main action (Send, Make a code, Bring back) is the one solid button; the public site's "Go to app" goes to sign-in without a session and to Home with one, and "Start free" goes to Welcome, where the session starts and the account is made inside it. Numbers come before actions on any dashboard. Tags are one vocabulary: a state is a `StatusTag` (`TONE` in `components/StatusTag.tsx`: neutral, info, good, warn, bad, and calm, warm, deep for facts), a fact on a card is a `FactTag` (`FACT_KINDS` and `FACT_KIND_META` in shared: price yellow with coins, free green with a gift, place orange with a map, online sky with a globe, when indigo with a calendar, then places left, checked, kind and status; the server sends `ListingFact`s built with `fact()`, as Explore does), and any other mark is an `IconTag`; the word is always shown, so colour and icon are never the only sign. A tag that would sit on a row of its own on a card whose top right corner serves nothing else goes in that corner, level with the card's first row (`components/CardHead.tsx`: an optional lead, the title keeping the rest of the row, the tags held to half of it and lined up with the first line, a long one truncated with `fit` and its words in `title`); a corner that holds a menu, a close, a featured mark or a leading price is left alone. Personal screens follow the same card rule (2026-11-05): a card in a row that can be stretched ends in one foot (`mt-auto`) with its tags or state on the left and its action or arrow at the right, so feet line up; a chart or an empty-state line takes the leftover room and sits in its middle rather than leaving an empty band (`InsightCard`'s chart, Home's Up next with nothing booked); a mark one item has, every item has (each badge tile ends in a bar, "0 of 1" for a one-step badge, a full bar and Done once earned; each `KitTile` in a row carries a hint); an empty chart is one short line, never a tall empty square (`Bubbles`); a hero's word and line go under its gauge on a phone (`flex-col sm:flex-row`), never a sliver beside it; a time stays whole (a `whitespace-nowrap` span). No new shadow values; use `shadow-toy-soft` or the `toy` classes.
- Teaching: `components/Pyramid.tsx` is the one explainer for how a pillar works; onboarding shows it after the answers (results first, then what did it, then the one rule), and Pillars and each pillar page show it too. The pyramid stands on its point: the foundation is the wide top layer and the task the point at the bottom, and a tick lands on the task and its glow runs up to the foundation. On a pillar page the layer list under it reads the same way, top down: the foundation first, projects last, done tasks after them. Each layer's meaning is a `TERMS` entry (foundation, guiding principle, value goal, goal outcome, project, task) and the Pillars how-it-works spells them out. Sheets have a close button and room to breathe (phone bottom padding is `pb-xl`). Daily, Pillars and Profile are single full-width columns (`columns={false}`); dashboards may use two columns. Search is inline (`components/SearchBox.tsx`): results appear under the field as you type, never on a new page. The onboarding explainer shows one card at a time under a tappable pyramid, never a wall of sections.
- Icons and the HIG rules (2026-11-03, the design brief): an icon in the interface is lucide (`lucide-react`), drawn only through `components/Icon.tsx` or `components/AppIcon.tsx` (a shared registry key through `lib/appIcons.ts`), never an emoji; shared lists (badges, honors, moods, interests, link blocks, sources, pieces, life areas, event types, add-ons, love languages, sounds, chat chips) carry an `icon` key, not an emoji. Emoji stay only in what people write, chat text, footer links, a reward item's own emoji and a card game's cover. The one exception is a picture, not an icon, and only on Home's Go to tiles (2026-11-07): Microsoft's Fluent Emoji 3D renders, MIT, credited in `public/app-art/LICENSE.txt` and on `/licences` (see the layout bullet); nowhere else. Line animations from useanimations.com (`react-useanimations`, 44 names in `ANIMATIONS`) play through `components/AnimatedIcon.tsx`: the player (`vendor-useanimations`) and each animation load lazily after the first paint, the still lucide icon stands in until then and under reduced motion, and the icon reacts to the button or link it sits in. They draw the tab bar and sidebar icons, the menu, search to clear, settings, trash, star, copy, focus mode, loading and more; a `Button` with `plusToX` (it also sets `aria-expanded`) and a `Fold` with `adds` wear the plus that turns into a cross. An inline toggle (one that opens or folds something in place, not a `Sheet`, dialog or menu, which have their own close) shows its state with `aria-expanded` and a mark from `components/ToggleMark.tsx`: `ToggleChevron` (turns half a turn while open; Fold, HowTo, AppGroups and every text toggle) or, on an icon-only toggle, `ToggleIcon` (the icon turns into a cross while open, as the small pencil at a task card's top right that opens its plan on Daily, for a mouse or a finger only, `aria-hidden` with `tabIndex` -1 and no tip of its own (the room beside it is its own card); the card itself, "Plan <title>" or "Close the plan for <title>", is the keyboard's stop); opacity and a turn only, 200ms on `--ease-open`, a plain swap under reduced motion. Community's Like is a thumbs up and Love is the animated heart. A link's arrow (an `<a>`, a router `Link`, `SiteArrow`, a row or card that opens another page, a menu item that opens a link) is lucide's `ArrowUpRight`, the 45-degree arrow, everywhere (2026-11-05), and its hover nudge goes up and right (`.read-arrow`); a toggle keeps its chevron or its icon-to-cross, and a button that opens a sheet, a stepper, a sequence between items and the chat's next button keep their own arrows. A button never changes its look at rest; its existing hover takes the pillar's colour (`--tint`, `--tint-ink` from the app's `data-accent`, a pillar page's `usePillarAccent`, or the `data-pillar` every box whose icon wears one of the seven colours carries), never a new filled hover. One primary (solid) button per screen or tab; the rest go behind `components/MoreMenu.tsx`; a `MoreMenu` with one item (and no trigger or ask of its own) draws as an `IconButton` instead (`components/IconButton.tsx`: round, 44px, the `.mf-round` glass of the Mindful panel, its words as a tooltip and `aria-label`), and a few icon actions on a card are a row of them; an icon action that should show its words on a laptop is an `ExpandButton` (`components/ExpandButton.tsx`: the words slide out beside the icon on hover or keyboard focus, an icon on touch; with `slide={false}`, where they would cover what sits beside it, as Inbox's two, or in a popup's rail, its name is a smart tip instead); **smart tips** (2026-11-12, the founder: "all the helper tooltips should appear in places that have no pre-existing text, like outside the popups"; `lib/smartTip.ts` over `lib/tipGeometry.ts` and `pickTipSpot` in the leaf `@kreators/shared/tipPlace`, started by `Root` after the first paint) are the one name for every `data-tip` trigger (`IconButton`, a rail's buttons, the water tabs, a labelled control's `title`): `aria-hidden`, shown to a mouse pointing or the keyboard's Tab or arrow, never a touch or a popup's first focus, gone on Escape, a press, a scroll for a mouse or a resize, placed beside its trigger only (2026-11-13, the founder: "the tooltip text should remain close to the button itself not somewhere else, position it right, left top or bottom accordingly"): at the 8px gap above, below, left or right, each lined up on the trigger's centre, start edge or end edge (or slid along that side just enough to fit the window, always spanning the trigger's middle), on the side that covers least (never over words, a control or a picture where a side is clear, 4px clear; else each thing costs what it is worth, a field and the next small control most, the trigger's own row's words, a heading, a button, other words, a picture, an empty card's fill least), the trigger's own lean winning a tie and its other alignments tried before another side, off a popup's panel when that side is free, inside the window, never further out (the far search, `TIP_REACH`'s lattice, was taken out); the page is read once around the trigger, every line of words in the parts that are drawn, a box only down to what a sticky bar or a panel leaves showing, and the condensed title bar counts as drawn though it takes no pointer, over what scrolls under it and with its own words; a long name with no clear side tries two lines, still beside it, reusing the first read; a name a mouse showed stays while the mouse crosses the gap onto it (the hull of the two, WCAG 1.4.13 hoverable) and goes once it leaves; a glossary card (`Tip.tsx`) stands beside its word the same way, however it was opened; `scripts/smoke/drive-tips.mjs` measures every one (Daily's open plan's close too, halfway down the plan), fails a tip that is not beside its trigger (`not-adjacent`: no side whose facing edges are within 12px with the two spans meeting) and checks the touch, Escape, hoverable and Close promises (before adjacency, 2026-11-13: 220 hits before the second pass and 69 after; then Inbox's Archive lost its drawn name, keeping its `aria-label`, and the assistant's bubble shows only its first three times (bubble-count and bubble-private check it), which left 14; adjacency wins over cover, so where every side of a trigger meets something the name now covers the least of it and the over-* counts may rise, reported as they are; listed in docs/DECISIONS.md); a panel in the page that is not a `Sheet` closes with `components/CloseButton.tsx` (round, 44px, glass, the X turns a quarter on hover or focus), and a popup's Close and round icon actions sit in its rail outside the card (`rail` on `Sheet`, see the Motion kit); where a header or a card's foot has one main action beside a More or round buttons, the main action takes the free width (`flex-1`) and they sit at the right end, but never in a row of equal choices, a form whose button is already full width or a toolbar of several actions; a line and the action it belongs to, or two short controls that fit side by side, sit left and right (text left, action right, `flex-wrap-reverse` on a narrow phone so the action stays on top, a title never squeezed under 60%); a helper line that says what the one button above it does is that button's caption, centred under it at 12px, and no other paragraph is centred; a row of many pills that only picks one value (a filter, a kind, a category) beside or above another control row is a `FilterSelect` (`components/FilterSelect.tsx`: a short label, 76px on a phone so the dropdowns line up, and a native `SELECT_SM` select), several of them in a `FilterBar` (one line from `md`, one per line on a phone), as on Store, Community, the Leaderboard and Journal prompts; a choice of a few views is `Segmented`, not a row of pills; a row of tabs that can outgrow its line is `components/TabRow.tsx` (2026-11-07, the founder: "on mobile the tags and filters can be right scroll items, u can keep the all button stick on right": when every pill fits it is one plain row; when they do not, on a phone and in any row under 34rem wide (a tablet's column) every pill sits on one line that scrolls sideways (touch, a mouse wheel, the arrow keys, Home and End; Tab still reaches each pill), snapped so a pill rests at its start, with the "All" button pinned outside it at the right end and a soft fade before All while more lies past the edge (`.tab-scroll` in app.css, `data-pinned-scroll` and `data-pinned-all`), the picked pill and a focused one scrolled whole into view, no scrollbar; a wider row shows at most 5 (or its `max`) then All, and scrolls like a phone's when fewer fit; All's sheet lists every tab with the picked one marked, and a pill too long even alone wraps inside itself; `href` makes every pill an in-page link, no `value` marks none picked, `disabled` items show but cannot be picked; each screen's own pill look kept through `pillClass`; since the button says All, a row's own everything pill says "Every tier", "Every category" or "Every line", never All); a spend of points asks again in `components/ConfirmSpend.tsx`, never `window.confirm`; every tap target is at least 44 by 44 (two exceptions: a row of the On this page card is 32px tall under a fine pointer, which has no finger to fit, and 44px on touch; and a task's tag as a filter, `button[data-tag-filter]`, whose hit area reaches 4px round its 24px, half the 8px between tags, so neighbours never overlap and the title is never taken, which WCAG 2.5.8 allows; target-probe.js leaves it out). Buttons are lower on a phone (2026-11-07, the founder: "make the buttons height lesser, this is more for mobile and for frontend website too"; Material's 40dp default and iOS's compact controls, umbrel's calm): `Button` lg 48, md 40, sm 36 under `md` and today's 56, 48 and 44 from it (`BTN_LG`, `BTN_MD`, `BTN_SM` in `lib/ui.ts`, `RED_YES` and `RED_YES_LG` the same), chips and pills (`chip`, `TabRow`'s pills, the `Segmented` segments, the choice pills of forms) 36 under `md`, and on the public site at every width `SITE_PRIMARY` 48, `SITE_PRIMARY_SM` and `SITE_SECONDARY` 40 and `SITE_CHIP` 36 (a `Button` on a site page or the privacy bar passes `low` to keep the phone's height on a laptop); words, font, colours and edges unchanged. Under 44 the hit area stays 44 by 44 through `.tap-pad` (app.css): an invisible ::before centred on the control that reaches out only as far as it needs (2px for 40, 4px for 36), so it never covers a neighbour 8px away; it cannot grow past a box that clips its own overflow (`fx-shimmer`, `truncate`), so those stay 44 or taller, and a field or select cannot carry one, so `FIELD` (about 49) and `SELECT_SM` (44) keep their heights. Round icon buttons (`IconButton`, `.mf-round`, `ExpandButton`) stay 44. Form strings are one copy each: `FIELD`, `FIELD_LINE`, `SELECT_SM` in `lib/ui.ts`, `FieldLabel` in `components/Field.tsx`, `errorText` and `ErrorLine` in `components/ErrorLine.tsx`, `copyPlain` in `lib/clipboard.ts`, `longDate` and `dayLabel` beside `shortDay` in `lib/date.ts`.
- Confirmations and popups (2026-11-06, the founder: "removals should always ask confirmation", "fix all buttons", then "fix all 20" popups): every destructive action asks first, never in one tap and never with `window.confirm` or `window.prompt`, in one of two ways (the founder, later the same day: "revert double tap changes that were converted into pop up confirmations, like in onboarding start over and others", then "close and highly destructive or irrevocable actions can be the popup instead"): what closes something (a link, a session, someone's access) or is highly destructive or cannot be undone (an account, a person's real records or history, money, signed paper, leaving, a refund or void) asks in an in-app `Sheet` with a RED yes in its own words and an outline Keep; a small, recoverable remove (a saved template, a reminder schedule, a question or section on paper that autosaves with Undo, Start over in a chat whose saved answers stay saved) is a second tap: the first tap turns the label to "Tap again to …" (`aria-live`), the second does it, and it disarms after about four seconds (a `MoreMenu` item takes `confirm`), the button's look at rest unchanged. Small personal items are second taps too (2026-11-13, the founder: "make delete task a double click button rather than popup, its destructive but low priority item, check for all such places and update"): your own task, subtask, reminder, not to do item, supplement, a focus or mindful session, a mood, sleep, stress, movement or eating entry, a journal entry (your own words, written again), a day template or time block (the control names what changes: the tasks lose the block and how many templates lose its slots, or the weekdays that go back to the default), a link block, a portfolio piece, a Content piece, a Human design chart, one archived note, a post nobody has answered yet, and a channel, cohort, run or event line you wrote (someone else's line still asks in a sheet, and so does your own post once it has replies, since they go with it; review 2026-11-13). A saved review (its snapshot of the numbers is never rebuilt) and a business metric (every number typed for it) keep their sheets, by the history rule above (review 2026-11-13). The words shown sit apart from one `sr-only` `aria-live` span that holds the ask only while it asks, so the lapse back to rest is never read out (`TapTwiceIcon` and `TapTwice` the pattern), and once a remove lands `afterRemove` in `lib/focusHeading.ts` says it (`sayStatus`) and moves the keyboard's place to the heading of the block the row sat in (inside a popup it only says it). `lib/useTapTwice.ts` holds the state for a control that keeps its own look (`tap(key, yes)`, one key asked at a time), and `components/TapTwiceIcon.tsx` is the icon-only remove (the trash or ✕ at rest; "Tap again to …" in place of the icon while it asks); a failed remove shows its `ErrorLine` beside the list. Popups stay for money, accounts, signed or sent paper, someone else's access (links, team, coaching), leaving, refunds, voids, whole lists ("Remove the whole list", "Delete all archived") and a layer that holds others up. The red yes is one look everywhere (2026-11-07, the founder: "red yes can be solid on hover or second confirmation tap", his own exception to "never a new filled hover"): `RED_YES` and `RED_YES_LG` in `lib/ui.ts` over `.red-yes` in app.css, the pale red outline at rest, solid red with white words (5.3:1 in both themes, weight unchanged) on a mouse hover (only under `(hover: hover)`, so a touch never leaves it stuck), on keyboard focus and while pressed; a second-tap control keeps its own look at rest and wears `.red-armed`, the same solid red, only while it asks, then goes back; `DELETE_BTN` stays the pale button that opens a question, with its faint wash on hover. `components/TapTwice.tsx` is the second tap as a component (a Content folder's link and research run, a saved review template); `components/ConfirmDelete.tsx` is the one sheet (its Close in the rail outside the card, its red yes where it is; `yes`, `keep` name the action: "Yes, leave the club", "Yes, refund it"), and `DataTable` bulk actions take a `confirm` that asks once for the set. A popup holds only what needs one (a confirmation, a long form, a picker, the assistant, an action menu): short content lives in the card it comes from as a `Fold`, an in-place edit or a panel that opens under its trigger (Mindfulness's music panel and keyboard-shortcuts fold, a not to do row that opens in place, the Journal and review calendars' day panels, Emergence's add to not to do, the card table's special card, a link's QR code, a circle's plan, Party's run it again, the trust breakdown, a house template's rename, the privacy card's three switches, the Agency documents' client picker, client permissions and password card under the paper, a client's set password and team link switch, Agency settings' currency and hours). Reading paragraphs (`prose-long`) wrap by `pretty` and fill their width.
- Smart shortcuts (2026-11-06, the founder: "suggest these kind of intelligent decisions through out the app", then "fix all these"): where a screen states a count or a state, what is hidden, off, set aside, late or unmarked can be acted on right there. Sleep and Stress fill their log form once today's entry loads, like Mood, and refill on a new day. Each field (hours and quality, level and stressors) keeps its own touch flag for the day, so a field the person moved stays as set and the others take the logged values. With nothing logged today the form starts from the last logged hours or level (else 7 hours or level 2), and Save or Update stays off until the entries have loaded. In Water and reminders, removing your own reminder is a second tap ("Tap again to remove", 2026-11-13; its label says unticking it pauses it instead). While any modal sheet is open, the laptop bubble drops to z-40 under the sheet's backdrop. The water card only swipes on touches that start inside the card, so a swipe in a sheet opened from it never changes the panel. Home's Go to section has the same hidden-apps eye button as Apps (a round `mf-round` button with `ToggleIcon` EyeOff and `aria-expanded`, shown only while an app is hidden, at the right of the section title). It opens `HiddenAppsPanel` inline under the title, listing the apps that fit the person first in each group with "Fits you: <why>" (`useFits` is shared by Profile, Apps and Home). The section's line says "Every app you have not hidden.": hide and unhide, never on and off. Emerging's Not now is recoverable and stays on the device. Every block shares one store per person (`lib/insightsHidden.ts`, `useHiddenInsightIds`, key `kreators.insightsHidden:<user>`), and each hide or Show again starts from what is stored, so blocks on one screen never undo each other. On /insights, "N set aside" is an inline toggle (aria-expanded) that lists those cards with "Show again". The Emerging and Habits blocks end with a quiet "Show N set aside" fold (`SetAsideFold`) and stay, saying "You set these aside.", when every card there is set aside. The exception is Home and Life (`quietWhenAllAside`), which show their quiet line instead. After the last card comes back, the fold closes and focus moves to the block's heading (`focusHeading`). Daily sends the picked sort to `GET /today` and `GET /today/waiting` (`&sort=`, read only through `isTaskSort` in `openOrderArgs`), so the six shown and "N more waiting" follow Day, Priority, Due date or By context (no pick: by day, or By context on a changed day; grouped: by priority). "Your day"'s "N tasks have no time or block yet" counts and lists every open task that opens on the day with no block and no time (`looseTasks`, up to 50, and `looseTotal` on `TodayResponse`), whatever the six shown or the Area filter, the same way as the room left in each block. Not to do: under the list, a quiet inline toggle "Yesterday: N not marked" (`YesterdayMarks` in NotToDoList.tsx) opens yesterday's unmarked items in place, each with Kept and Slipped, through the ordinary `POST /today/not-to-do/:id/day` with yesterday and the person's today. Only yesterday, never earlier. It shows only while the server's `dateNearToday` would accept yesterday (within `WAIT.dateWindowDays` of the UTC date), so a device far behind UTC late in its evening never sees buttons the server refuses. Wellbeing opens its tracker list with "N of M trackers on". This counts only Mood, Sleep, Stress and Mindfulness where the person may switch the app (available, not locked). The Apps page's eye button sits at its right (`aria-expanded`, `ToggleIcon` `EyeOff`) and opens the hidden trackers in place, each with an outline Show that calls `api.setModule(key, true)` and then refreshes. A hidden tracker's row keeps its place with a neutral "Hidden" `StatusTag`, and tapping it turns the tracker on and then opens it, never the "off" page. Agency: each Overview number opens the list of exactly what it counts (In review → Pipeline list `?pstage=review`; Late → `?pdue=late`, the "Late (n)" option in the Stage select, served by `GET /agency/items?due=late` with `LATE_SQL`, the rule of `deliverableOverdue`; Active clients → `cstatus=active`; Unpaid invoices → `invstatus=sent`), with the list's other filters cleared; a client page's "For them to look at" tile is two real links, not one button (the founder, 2026-11-07, "add both"): "1 waiting," opens Work's list on In review and "1 late" on Late, each a link with a 44px hit area (a `::before`, up from the first and down from the second, so they never overlap when they wrap), the tile unchanged at rest. under the Superfocus pace line, record that a project's edit sheet on the pillar page has a "Due by" date field (saved through `PATCH /nodes/:id` `{dueDate}`, `null` clears it), and that tapping a project's "Add a due day to see the pace." line opens that sheet with the cursor in the field (`data-due-hint` with `data-autofocus`). Inbox: "Mark all read" shows whenever the current tab has an unread note, using the tab's unread count from the API. Under "What happened" one line holds the view (Inbox, Unread, Archived) at the left with its one action right after it as a round `ExpandButton` with `slide={false}` (Archive read items, or Delete all archived in Archived, which still asks first) and the kinds (`TabRow` `align="end"` `natural`: Everything, Replies and mentions… then All, each pill its own width, never stretched to the free width, on the views' line) at the right; under 34rem the views and the icon take the first line and the kinds the next at full width (the founder, 2026-11-07); on a laptop the kinds' share of that line is under 34rem, so they scroll sideways with All pinned there too, as on a phone (the founder kept it, 2026-11-09). After archiving a note, one inline line (role=status) reads "Archived. Undo" with a 44 by 44 Undo that restores the note through the same archive route Restore uses. The line lasts about ten seconds or until the next action, and stays with its error if the restore fails. On the Leaderboard, a hidden person gets the "Show me on the boards" switch right in the hidden card (the same `PATCH /me` opt-in as Profile), or a line saying to finish setup first. Hide and Show on every app row are a quiet round 44px eye (the arrange arrows' `toy toy-soft` look, the useanimations `visibility` eye turning to its slashed state before the row moves), never an outline button; each group's list keeps `sm` of room either side (a wrapper, since `.fx-inset` zeroes its own margins); and the change shows at once (`useAppSwitch` writes the cached account before the call, puts it back on a refusal, reads again after), so the count and the Hidden apps fold never wait for the reload (the founder, 2026-11-06).
- Layout patterns, app-wide (2026-11-06): a list's view switch sits at the left and its other controls at the right; under a container width of 34rem every control takes the full width; Edit sits at the bottom right of what it edits (the Public page's link blocks, a review reminder on its day: the other actions at the left, and on a narrow phone Edit wraps under them, still at the right); a section's link and filters sit at its top right (`Section`'s `action` and `tools`); a card's title and its menu share one row; adding to a list is a `Fold` with `adds`; these move and size what is there, and never restyle it. A main action far down a long form stays in reach on a phone (the founder, 2026-11-07: "make it sticky above bottom menu"; `components/StickyAction.tsx`, `.sticky-action` in app.css): `StickyAction` wraps the form's own action row where it stands and is `position: sticky` under `md`, held just above the tab bar (its height measured, `--tabbar-h`), above the bottom edge on the public site, or at the foot of a popup's panel, and back in its place once that place is on screen; only while it is held (`data-stuck`) a `--card-strong` bar with a hairline top is drawn behind it edge to edge by a `::before`, so the row's look, width and focus order never change; the assistant's button and the privacy card rise above a held bar (`.lift-over-sticky`, `data-sticky-held` and `--sticky-action-h` on the root), a field or any other control that gets focus or grows under it, or under the lifted assistant's button, is scrolled up above it (only in the bar's own scroll box; the Public page's laptop `md:sticky` bar keeps fields clear the same way), and it never sits inside an `overflow` box (sticky would stop working). A row whose main button (its submit, else its last button) is disabled is not held (`data-idle`, static on a phone, no bar, nothing lifted; read live by a MutationObserver, and a tap that disables it while saving keeps it held for 1.5s), so Add task before a title, Save entry and List it wait in place until they can be used; once a held row is let go the assistant's button rides 36px above it (`data-sticky-ride`, no easing) until the row has passed above its resting place, so it never rests over the row's right end (2026-11-09, the founder's yes); only a row just held, or one rising into the lane from below, rides it, so a row coming down from above never makes the button leap (2026-11-09). A phone on its side (a window under 26rem tall, `ROOMY`) and an open keyboard (`data-kb-up`, read from the visual viewport as the assistant reads it) keep the action in its place and `FollowAction` away, since there the bar and the tab bar took up to half the window (the mobile check, 2026-11-07). A control inside a bar that sticks to the top (`data-top-bar`: the site's header, a whole-page chat's header, the coach's share row) takes `scroll-margin-top: -80px`, so focusing it never scrolls the page to "reveal" it; on a phone the page's `scroll-padding-bottom` clears the assistant's button (164px and the safe area, plus the bar's height while one is held), so the keyboard's stop is never under it, and every app screen keeps 168px and the safe area at its foot (`Screen`, 120 before 2026-11-09; the Companion, which has no button, keeps 120 with `assistant={false}`), so the last control clears the button once the page is scrolled to its end. Used on Fitness's Log movement, the Shop's product editor and its lesson sheet, the Public page's Save profile, Daily's and a pillar's Add a task (and the month's day sheet), /copyright's notice, a public event's Join, a client's onboarding steps, and the long popups (Launch a reward, Start a club, New program, Sell something, a journal entry, Timer settings, Agency's client and piece sheets). A public page whose main action stands far from most of it (the home, /features, /7rp) has `FollowStart` (`site/FollowStart.tsx`, the lazy `FollowAction`): a fixed bar at the bottom edge with the same words, shown only while none of the page's own copies (`data-main-action`), the site's header (`data-follow-wait`, which scrolls with a phone's page, so its Go to app and the bar never show together; the founder, 2026-11-09) nor the footer is on screen. A form whose action is in view, or sits at the top, keeps it as it was; the agreement's Accept stays beside the typed name. `spacing-probe.js` measures a held bar in its place, never while held. The room around them (2026-11-08, the Contribution apps): an `ErrorLine` carries its own margin (`className="mt-xs"`), never a wrapper that keeps 8px with no error; a hint under a field sits `xs` under it; a padded card's first field keeps no top margin (`*:first:mt-0`); a section's own actions sit level with its title (Work's Your listing, a client's Documents through `AgencyDocsList` `heading`); a paper sits `md` under its cover; a row's tied title keeps `flex-[1_1_14rem]` so it never runs under its tag; and a cover's tags or status start at the left on a phone (`md:justify-end`, or `flex-row-reverse` where a save line comes first). In the Connection and Creation apps (2026-11-07): a hand-made section header with a button is laid out as `Section` is (the button in a one-title-line box, `flex h-[1.2em] items-center`, and the line under the title in the title's column, never running under the button: Shop's Products and Sales, Customers, Content, Auto-DM, tracked links, Finance's Budgets); the first titled section under a cover or the Shop's tab row is `mt-lg`; a link card beside a form card pads as the form does (`p-md`), so they share a left edge; a row's name keeps 60% and its actions wrap under it at the right on a phone (Bond's friends and introductions); a name link's 44px tap area is a `::before`, so it sits as close to its line as an unlinked name (Community's posts); the Public page's picture and name read from the left on a phone; and a Party event's pictures and their add button sit under its details card. In the Reflection apps (2026-11-07): a score's own breakdown sits right under it, and By period and Emerging come after a screen's numbers (Wellbeing's What makes it under the hero; Life's five area cards under the week, with "Open … tools" at a card's foot below `lg`); Mood's coloured top uses the Screen's column (`--nav-w` and the `lg` gap), with its room above the back button on an inner block since `safe-top` sets the outer one's, and its title reads from the left on a phone; a section with nothing in it shows no heading (Sleep's Nights, Stress's Days) and never an empty wrapper (Emerging's What stands out says its empty line as content); a tinted panel that would hold only a switch's own card is not drawn (Fitness's Supplements while off); a card's line sits under its title beside the corner icon, not under the icon's height (Discover's hero and add-ons); a legend beside a donut is held to 360px, so a count stays by its name; Wellbeing's two doors share a row only from 34rem; Mindful's focus slot is `mt-lg` under the hero.
- SwiftUI Expert rules, in web terms (wave 8, 2026-11-04; `docs/swiftui-rules.md` is the checklist, 37 rules from AvdLee's SwiftUI Agent Skill, walked for every app screen and again for the newer ones on 2026-11-07, its table at the end of that file; CLAUDE.md wins over it and it never restyles a control at rest; when a row or a panel goes with the focused button, `focusHeadingAfter` in `lib/focusHeading.ts` moves the keyboard's place to the block's heading without moving the page): one owner per piece of state (React Query, the URL, `useKept`, `useFocusPrefs`), stable keys from ids (an editable list keys rows by a made id, like the FAQ, intake and quiz rows in StudioProduct, never the index), every effect cleans up its timer, listener, tween or poll, transform and opacity only, `<button>` or `<a>` for anything tappable with a name, decorative art `aria-hidden`, a sheet opened with the item it is about, and every confirmation an in-app `Sheet` with a red yes and an outline Keep, never `window.confirm` (none is left: Resources and a pyramid layer's remove in Realm ask in `ConfirmDelete` too; since 2026-11-13 a small personal item is a second tap instead, see Confirmations). One solid button per screen also holds for a reviewer on a pitch (Pitch a series turns outline, Record decision is solid), for an empty Agency (the empty card's Add client is outline beside the header's), for Profile (Save profile is solid, Rewards is outline beside Rank) and for a Content folder (Add link is solid, every Read it is outline; both the founder, 2026-11-07). What the wave made true: Companion, Party, Run, Win (`ConfirmSpend` for Pay), Tools, Store, Studio, Content, Automations, Discover, Admin and Profile's "Erase everything" (now in the Your data row) all ask in a sheet (Sleep and Stress rows, Mood's day, a journal entry, your own post and channel line became second taps on 2026-11-13); the newest Companion lines (80) and Bond and circle lines (300) are the ones shown, oldest first; a Bond channel someone was only asked into polls nothing until they say yes; Community's feed filters are one `FilterBar` of three `FilterSelect`s (Show, Sort, Topic; wave 21) under the Feed and Channels `Segmented` with one line under Write a post and the pay rules in a `Fold`; Store, Tools and the product editor keep one solid button with the rest in `MoreMenu`s, and a card with a partner link says "Partner link. Kreators may earn a share." on the card itself; Library says when the seller has not added the file yet; a cohort lesson (`LessonCard` in `screens/Cohort.tsx`) is one header button (its number in a paper circle, the whole title, its kind as an `IconTag` in the top right corner, a chevron that turns), and open it shows the words straight on the card (`prose-long`), then at its foot the source link (Skill file, Watch or Open) full width with the round pictures `IconButton` at its right and the kind's tip centred under it as its caption; the program's pictures open from an `IconButton` at the right of the cohort title; the author edits or withdraws their own TV or Fund pitch while it is Submitted (`PATCH /proposals/:id`, `POST /proposals/:id/withdraw`, guarded on the stage, 409 once the team moved it, audited `proposal.withdraw`); Reviews pages its past reviews (`r`, the daily ones apart as `rd`); line charts raise an odd whole-number span by one so the middle label is whole (`evenTop`); the public footer reads "Go to app" with a session and "Sign in" without.
- Animation: new motion is written with GSAP (`gsap` in apps/web); `motion/react` stays only where it already is, as `m as motion` inside the one `LazyMotion` in main.tsx, whose features (`domMax`, with drag and layout) come by `import()` from `lib/motionFeatures.ts` after the first download; a plain `motion` import never comes back (it would pull every feature into the entry), and only `AnimatePresence`, `useReducedMotion` and `LazyMotion` are imported by name. Respect reduced motion with `gsap.set`.
- Rewards (`/rewards`, `REWARDS` and `REWARD_GROUPS` in shared, redeemed with points through `routes/rewards.ts`): the catalog is code, grouped on shelves (books, card games, apparel, tech, drinkware, stationery, lifestyle, novelty, experiences), every cost whole points. The three books are Shubham Aggarwal's own (The Relationship Method, Dictionary of Mistakes for Kreators and Dictionary of Mistakes for 20 Somethings, from brandingbum.com; those are the reward names, while the books' own titles, which the Book page and the site use, are in `BOOKS`: Dictionary of Mistakes for Creators and Dictionary of Mistakes for 20s) and the card games are FyndGames from FyndFlow; an item may carry a `link` shown as "Read more". A new item is added there and nowhere else, each with its own emoji; the merch brand is "Kreators" ("Kreators tee", never "kreators.app tee"), while the app stays kreators.app; the shared test checks keys, shelves and costs. Gear stays a reservation until a drop ships. The nine card games (`CARD_GAMES` in shared: Ulta Seedha, DateFlow, DateGuru, HealFlow, FunFlow, 7RP Challenge, Roast or Flirt, ShadowFlow, WorkFlow) are rewards of kind `unlock`, never physical: redeeming one (`POST /rewards/game_<key>/redeem`) opens it at once and for good (`card_unlocks`, migration 0040, kept through an erase, in the export, gone with the account), cannot be cancelled, and switches the Games app (`cards`) on. The screen: Your reservations sit above the catalog, the games shelf draws `GameTile` (see Card games), Unlock and Reserve are outline (the `ConfirmSpend` yes is the one solid action), counts go through `formatCount`, and `GET /rewards` pages the reservations (`pageOf(c, 'r')`, `redemptionsPage`, a `Pager` with prefix `r` under Your reservations, which still shows on page 2 or later). Help pages your messages (`t`) and the team's queue (`q`) the same way, and a queued ticket's reply is an outline "Send reply".
- Focus mode on the card table (`useFocus` in lib/store.ts, never kept): an icon button with fullscreen-style corner brackets (labelled "Focus mode" beside it on wider screens) hides the page header and the explanations, takes the menu, the assistant and the reminders bubble out of `Shell`, makes the table fill the screen and asks the browser for real fullscreen where it has it (not every phone does, so it works without); Escape or the same button, now with the corners pointing in, leaves it, and leaving the table always leaves focus mode. When the table changes size (focus mode on or off, a phone turning, a window resized) every card is scaled onto the new table, so none is ever left outside its edge (`node scripts/smoke/drive-cards.mjs` deals nine cards, leaves and re-enters focus mode, and must report 0 outside).
- Sounds are checked in a browser, not only by types: starting a background sound must never throw halfway (each source is started once, by `play`, and a failed start stops everything it began, `startAmbient` returns null), the master switch and the volume act on a sound that is already playing (`setVolume` drops the scheduled fade-in and glides from where the sound is), the preview follows the controls, and a sound fades out in about a third of a second. The Days, Weeks and Months buttons and the Minutes and Hours switch of the focus dashboard share one line, the unit at the right (it wraps to its own line on a phone, still at the right).
- Focus rounds in Mindfulness (`components/FocusRounds.tsx`, the 2026-11-02 panel): one dark slate card (`.night-card` with `.mf-panel`, dark in both themes so the orange reads: orange text 4.96:1 or better, white 12:1) with four tabs, **Breathe, Timer, Pomodoro and Stopwatch** (the section is "Breathe and focus", the only panel on Mindful; a finished breath always logs mindful minutes, kind `breathe`; on a phone the four tabs take the first row and the main button sits full width under them). **Breathe** follows a pattern (`BREATH_PATTERNS`: Calm exhale 4·4·6·2 first, Box, 4-7-8, Even, Triangle, Longer out, Pranayam 4·4·8, Sigh; plain lines, never a health claim; `breathPatternFor`, `breathRounds`) with a cue at each step (`BREATH_CUES`: Soft chime, Bell, Voice saying In, Hold, Out through the browser's own speech, or No cue; `playBreathCue`, silent when the master switch is off), both two `ChipSelect`s saved as `FocusSettings.breath` and `breathCue`, quick lengths 1, 3, 5 and 10 minutes beside them (25 too while the panel is narrow, `.mf-quick-extra`) and a ruler from 1 to 30; the panel lays itself out by its own width, not the screen's (`container: mf` at the end of app.css): under 600px of panel, a phone or a tablet beside the sidebar, it takes the phone layout (tabs across, Start full width, the two time buttons above the ruler, the pickers two to a row, the five lengths on one even row), and under 300px the tabs, big times and pills step down so nothing passes its edge; a count can be held longer (`FocusSettings.breathPace`, 1.0 to 5.0 seconds in tenths, default 1, clamped and rounded by `parseFocusSettings`, saved with the other focus settings; `breathAtPace` in shared `focus.ts` times every step, so 4·4·6·2 at 1.5 holds 6·6·9·3 seconds, while the circles still show and count down the pattern's counts): before a session the big total on the right is a button captioned "total" and a matching "1.0s per count" button sits on the left (`.mf-time-btn` in `.mf-dial-breathe`; the picked one wears the accent ring, the other is dimmed), and the ruler between them sets whichever is picked (on a phone the two share a row above it); a break breathes with the same pattern. The breath is drawn as a row of glass circles, one per step left to right (`components/BreathFour.tsx`, our own code after wensity's liquid loader): the current step fills with liquid over its count, with a sliding wave and two ripples, and carries the word and the seconds left; earlier steps stay full and soft, later ones wait empty with their count; no outlines and no text under the row; under reduced motion no wave or ripple and the current step shows full. Mindful's Sessions list (its mindful logs) can be deleted, never edited: each row has a 44px trash that deletes on a second tap ("Tap again to delete" in place of the icon, 2026-11-13), and the delete is the owner-scoped `DELETE /wellbeing/:kind/:id`, which now refuses an unknown kind and a row of another kind with 404; logging pays no xp, so nothing is taken back (underline row on `useSlider`; the last tab is remembered on the device, `kreators.focusTab`; the other tabs are locked while any clock runs), an orange Start (Pause and Resume when running), a **`RulerPicker`** for the focus length (5 to 120 minutes, step 5, labels every 10; it picks `settings.focus` and moves this part with it, and is locked once a part has started), the time in large tabular figures, and for Pomodoro four small chips each opening a short list (`ChipSelect`: Short break, Long break, Sessions between long breaks, Total sessions, which is the sessions times the runs, so the saved shape did not change). Presets Classic 25/5, Deep 50/10, Light 15/3 and the plan line sit in the settings sheet, with the three logging switches and the sound controls; a long break comes after a chosen number of rounds; optional automatic start of the next part. Five round glass buttons (44px, `.mf-round`, in `.mf-rounds`; the fifth, Full screen, came with the Toggl pass) sit at the top right of the section, level with the line under its heading, and wrap under it on a narrow phone: the task being worked on, settings, sounds on or off (the master switch, its state in its label) and background music (`SoundControls part="music"`). The clock survives page changes: `components/FocusRoundsHost.tsx`, mounted once at the root while someone is signed in, portals `FocusRounds` into one fixed container and only moves that DOM node between a page's slot and a hidden parking spot (`lib/focusPortal.ts`; changing a portal's container made React rebuild the clock). Two pages have a slot: Mindful and Home, where Breathe and focus sits between the line of the day and the daily brief whenever the person has Mindfulness on (the founder, 2026-11-06: a hidden app's widget leaves Home); `page` says which, a page clears only its own slot, the slot is a steady `useCallback` ref, and the laptop keys act only on Mindful (`keysHere`), while the stop sheet and full screen work wherever the panel is showing; each slot keeps the panel's last height (`focusPortal.height`) until the panel is inside, so Back's restored scroll is not pushed down, and the corner pill links to Mindfulness when the person's switch is on and to Home otherwise, and a running clock shows as the corner pill (`components/FocusPill.tsx`, colour by stage, restart, stop and pause on hover or focus, sent through `lib/focusSession.ts`; blue while more than half is left, green past halfway, orange the last five minutes, red with a soft pulse the last minute, violet in a break, green for Breathe, indigo for the Stopwatch, dimmed with a pause mark when paused) on the app, the whole-page chats, the vibe check, the card table and the public site header. On a phone the pill is never pinned: in the app, Standalone pages and the vibe check it sits at the top of the page (`absolute`) and scrolls away with it, pinned only from `md` up; the whole-page chats keep it in their own sticky header, which is the chat's controls. It is never pinned on a phone: there it takes the logo's top-left corner (`TabBar`, `Standalone`, the vibe check) and scrolls away with the page, and it is pinned only from `md` up (the chats' sticky header still carries it, since that header is the chat's own controls). In the laptop sidebar the running clock is sticky at the sidebar's top on its own surface; with the sidebar folded it is `FocusPill` `dot`: a 44px circle in the stage colour showing only the minutes, a ring rippling out once a second while it runs (`.focus-dot-ripple`, none under reduced motion), a pause mark when paused, the full time in its tooltip and label, opening Mindfulness. **Timer** is one countdown (its length is its own, kept on the device as `kreators.focusTimerMin`, and follows the saved focus length until set) with no breaks; when it ends it chimes and buzzes and, only if "focus sessions count as mindful minutes" is on, keeps a focus round for the dashboard and logs `focus`; stopping early counts nothing. **Stopwatch** counts up from a stored start instant (`useStopwatch` in `lib/focusClocks.ts`), Start, Pause, Stop and Reset; Stop keeps the whole minutes (never over ten hours, `FOCUS_MAX_MINUTES` 600, nothing under one minute) the same way and only on the same switch, Reset keeps nothing. Pomodoro has **Skip** and **Stop** (a square; back to session one, the part counts for nothing unless Keep is picked) and no Reset; the actions read Skip and Stop, then the session dots and "Session N of M", then "This part" (on a phone each its own line in that order). The "This part" buttons (plus or minus 1 and 5) nudge a Timer or Pomodoro part while it runs. The clock runs on an end time, not on counted ticks, so a background tab stays right; it keeps the screen awake where the browser allows, chimes and vibrates at the end, shows the time in the tab title and breathes with the circle in a break. A finished focus round logs mindful minutes by default (`focus`, the `logFocus` switch), a finished break only if the person switches that on (`extra.kind` `break`, `logBreaks`); today's rounds and focus minutes come from the server (the Today list below). A skipped round counts for nothing. The person's choices are now saved on their account (migration 0053 `focus_prefs`, `GET` and `PUT /wellbeing/focus-settings`, limit `prefs`, `FocusSettings`, `FOCUS_DEFAULTS` and `parseFocusSettings` in shared the one reader, `useFocusPrefs` on the screen with the browser as a per-person copy so it never waits on the network; owner only, in the export, gone with the account; a device that kept settings before hands them over once if the account has none), so they follow the person to every device: the lengths and counts, and the defaults, which are focus sessions counted as mindful minutes on, breaks counted off and starting the next part by itself off. Sounds (`lib/sound.ts`, all made in the browser, no files): `sound` is the one master switch for every sound and alarm, beats, chimes, background sound and the buzz, off silences every timer and keeps the other choices (greyed out); under it one soft beat when a mini break starts and three when the long break starts (`BREAK_BEATS`, each with its own switch, `soundShort` and `soundLong`, and a chime when that break ends), a buzz on a phone (`vibrate`), and a background sound (`AMBIENT_SOUNDS`: rain, ocean, wind or a soft hum, with a volume) that plays while the focus timer or the breathing timer runs, fades in and out, and can be tasted for a few seconds; `tickSound()` is the very soft tick a ruler makes, silent when the master switch is off or the browser has not yet allowed sound. `components/SoundControls.tsx` is the one set of controls, on the focus sessions (with the break beats) and under the breathing timer; browsers only allow sound after a tap, so Start and the previews call `unlockAudio`. The Toggl pass (2026-11-03, migration 0073 `focus_sessions`: `focus_rounds.mode`, `log_id`, `client_ref`): **Today** under the panel lists the day's sessions newest first (`GET /wellbeing/focus/today?date=`, owner only; time, length, task, kind, rating), and the heading's tally reads from it, never from the device; each row has a 44px **Continue** that starts the same clock, length and task (`useCountdown.begin`). The **task sheet** is rows, not a select: up to five recent tasks (`GET /wellbeing/focus/tasks?recent=1`, open tasks the person focused on, newest session first), a search (`useDebounced` 300 ms over `?q=`), the results, and "No task". **Stop** on a Timer or a Pomodoro focus part past a whole minute pauses and asks in a sheet, Keep N minutes or Throw it away (two equal outline buttons); Keep always keeps the round and counts mindful minutes only with `logFocus`; under a minute it stops as before; off Mindful the pill asks itself (`stopKeep`, `stopDrop` commands). Tapping a row opens **Change this session** (a ruler for the length, the task picker, Delete asked twice), and "Add a session I forgot" adds one for today or yesterday (mode `manual`): `PATCH` takes `minutes` and `nodeId` (re-read owner only), `DELETE /wellbeing/focus/:id` is new, and a round that counted as mindful minutes writes its log in the same batch as the round and keeps its id (`log_id`), so a change or a delete moves the log with it; POST, PATCH and DELETE are in `ROUTE_LIMITS` (`prefs`). An optional **daily goal** (`goalMinutes`, 15 to 480 in steps of 15, off by default) shows as "50 of 90 minutes today" under the panel and as a "Days the goal was met" tile (`focusGoalMet` over `dayMinutes`), never a streak; an optional **daily nudge** (`reminderTime`, `reminderDays`, off by default) is one inbox note a day from `sendFocusReminders` (id `focus-<user>-<day>`, in the person's zone, within an hour after the time, skipped when that day already has a session, linking to `/mindful`). **Keys** on a laptop, on Mindful only, never while typing or with a sheet open: Space start or pause, S stop (the same sheet), K skip (Pomodoro), ? lists them. The tab title carries the task ("18:20 · Edit vlog intro · kreators.app") and the pill shows it after the time from the tablet width up. The dashboard's bars say "45 of 60 min planned" when a task has an estimate (`planned`). A **Stopwatch** left in a tab hidden for over thirty minutes asks on the way back: Keep all, or Keep up to when you left (pauses there; `useStopwatch.away`, `keepUpTo`). **Full screen** is a fifth round button: it sets focus mode (`useFocus`) and the section itself (heading, round buttons and panel) covers the whole page on phone and laptop (`.mf-cover`: fixed, under sheets, on the page wash, the panel at most 760px, scrolling inside while the body is locked; the Today list hides), left by Escape, the button or leaving Mindful (Escape and the laptop keys stand aside only for a modal dialog, `[role="dialog"][aria-modal="true"]`, so the non-modal privacy card never blocks them). Daily's time blocks show a faint "Focused 25m" bar beside a task with sessions today (`DayBlocks` reads the same `focus-today` query). **One tab logs a part**: the browser names each part `${owner}:${clock}:${endsAt}`; `claimPart` in `lib/focusSession.ts` claims it inside a Web Lock in localStorage before the chime and the log, so a duplicated tab (which copies sessionStorage) stands aside; on load a copied tab asks on the `kreators.focus` BroadcastChannel and, when another tab already runs the same clock, stops its own copy without logging and shows that clock read-only ("Running in another tab"); and `POST /wellbeing/focus` takes that name as `clientRef` (unique per person, `INSERT OR IGNORE`, a duplicate answers 200 with the first id and `duplicate: true`, and its mindful log is written only with a new round). `node scripts/smoke/drive-focus-everywhere.mjs` with `FOCUS_PARTS=toggl,twotabs` and `FOCUS_API=http://localhost:8788` drives all of it against the smoke server.
- No clickable links in what people write to each other: posts, replies, chat lines (Bond, circles, event conversations, pre-owned listings), coach notes and inbox lines all render through `components/Inert.tsx`, which keeps any web address as plain text (set in a code-like face, never an anchor, `data-inert-link`), and `index.html` tells phones not to turn numbers, emails or addresses into links (`format-detection`). `scripts/scan.sh` fails on any `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`. The only real links are the ones the owner sets on purpose: a public page's link blocks, an event host's photo-folder link and partner links. Markdown (lessons, pieces, assistant replies) has no links either.
- Unlocks (the one rule `unlockFor`, wording `unlockAt`): a product, a store item, a tool or a game can ask for an xp floor (`min_xp`, migration 0044), a points floor, a badge, or any mix. Where it is locked the button is a `LockedButton` (a padlock, "Locked", then "Unlocks at 500 xp, 2,000 points and the First tick badge", every count through `formatCount`, then what is still missing for this person); a game's lock includes its price in points (`CARD_GAMES` `minXp`, `badge`). A super admin (role `admin`) is never locked out of any of it, on the server (`haveFor` in apps/api/src/unlocks.ts sets `superAdmin`) and in the buttons. A super admin can also give xp (`POST /admin/users/:id/xp`, down to zero, audited) and badges by hand (`POST /admin/users/:id/badges`, migration 0045 `badge_grants`, shown on the profile, counted for unlocks and the leaderboard, audited) from Admin, People.
- Badges (2026-11-02, migration 0065 `badge_awards`; `BADGES`, `BADGE_FAMILIES`, `BADGE_FAMILY_META`, `BADGE_STATS`, `badgesFrom`, `badgeProgress` in shared; `badgeState` and `getStats` in db.ts; `GET /me/badges`; `components/BadgeArt.tsx`, `BadgeGallery.tsx`): about sixty-five medals in ten families (Daily habits, Pillars, Focus, Reflection, Community, Creator, Money, Fitness, Learning, Milestones), each with a tier (bronze, silver, gold, platinum), a rule that is one count reaching a number (`stat` in `BADGE_STATS`, `min`), and the old keys unchanged. The rules are data in shared and `getStats` is the one read of the counts (one query, a column named after each stat, a shared test checks every stat has one); it counts and never opens what a person wrote. It visits every row it counts, so `/me` takes the counts from the person's row when under an hour old (`keptStats`, `users.stats_json`, migration 0117): a tick moves them by what it changed (`statsAfterTick`), an untick clears them, and the gallery and an unlock read fresh, so a badge from anything but a tick can show up to an hour late on Profile. Earned badges are written down the first time they are seen (`badge_awards`, `INSERT OR IGNORE`, dated; an older account's first batch has no date), so a badge once earned is never taken back; badges the team gave by hand stay in `badge_grants`. Reflection, Focus and Money are private families (`BADGE_FAMILY_META`): they show to the owner only, never on a public page, never in the leaderboard count, never in an unlock picker (`PUBLIC_BADGES`).
